Closed Ricci Flows with Singularities Modeled on Asymptotically Conical Shrinkers
Maxwell Stolarski

Abstract
. Given an asymptotically conical, shrinking, gradient Ricci soliton, we show that there exists a Ricci flow solution on a closed manifold that forms a finite-time singularity modeled on the given soliton. No symmetry or Kähler assumptions on the soliton are required. The proof provides a precise asymptotic description of the singularity formation.



1.
Introduction

A smooth collection of Riemannian metrics
\{g(t)\}_{t\in[t_{0},T)}
on a manifold
M
is said to evolve by Ricci flow if

\partial_{t}g=-2Rc.

The Ricci flow equation is invariant under parabolic scaling and pullback by diffeomorphisms.
Considered as a dynamical system on the space of metrics modulo scaling and pullback by diffeomorphism, the Ricci flow has generalized fixed points given by Ricci solitons, self-similar Ricci flow solutions that evolve by scaling and pullback by diffeomorphism.
Ricci solitons
(M,g,X,\lambda)
may be specified by a Riemannian manifold
(M,g)
, a vector field
X
on
M
, and a real number
\lambda\in\mathbb{R}
such that

Rc+\mathcal{L}_{X}g=\lambda g.

As generalized fixed points, Ricci solitons model singularities of the Ricci flow.
For example,
[
EMT11
]
showed that any type I singularity of the Ricci flow is modeled by a nontrivial gradient shrinking Ricci soliton.
More generally,
[
Bam21
]
showed that parabolic rescalings of the flow based at any singular point subsequentially converge in a weak sense to a possibly non-smooth soliton.

However, a certain converse question has no clear answer.
Namely, given a smooth, complete,
noncompact
Ricci soliton
(M,g,X,\lambda)
, it is not always clear if there exists a Ricci flow
\{G(t)\}_{t\in[0,T)}
on a
closed
manifold
\mathcal{M}
that forms a singularity modeled on the Ricci soliton
(M,g,X,\lambda)
.
Of course, the self-similar Ricci flow solution
(M=\mathcal{M},(1-2\lambda t)\varphi_{t}^{*}g)
associated to a shrinking Ricci soliton
(M,g,X,\lambda)
forms a finite-time singularity modeled on
(M,g,X,\lambda)
,
so the restriction that
M
is noncompact and
\mathcal{M}
compact here is key.
One obstruction comes from Perelman’s no local collapsing Theorem
[
Per02
]
, which in particular shows a singularity for a Ricci flow solution on a closed manifold cannot be modeled by the product of Hamilton’s cigar soliton with Euclidean space.

In this article, we show that, given any asymptotically conical, shrinking, gradient Ricci soliton, there exists a Ricci flow solution on a closed manifold that forms a singularity modeled by that soliton.

Theorem 1.1
.

Let
(M,\overline{g},\overline{\nabla}f,\lambda=\frac{1}{2})
be a smooth, complete, asymptotically conical, shrinking, gradient Ricci soliton.
Then there exists a closed manifold
\mathcal{M}
and
a Ricci flow solution
\{G(t)\}_{t\in[t_{0},1)}
on
\mathcal{M}

that forms a local, type I singularity at time
t=1

such that,
for any sequence
t_{j}\nearrow 1
and
p_{\infty}\in M
,
there exists a sequence
p_{j}\in\mathcal{M}

such that the parabolically rescaled Ricci flows

\left(\mathcal{M},G_{j}(t)\doteqdot\frac{1}{1-t_{j}}G(t_{j}+t(1-t_{j})),p_{j}\right)

converge to
(M,(1-t)\varphi_{t}^{*}\overline{g},p_{\infty})
in the pointed Cheeger-Gromov sense as
j\nearrow\infty
.

Moreover, there exists an open subset
U\subset\mathcal{M}
and
\Phi:U\times[t_{0},1)\to M

such that:

(1)

for every
t\in[t_{0},1)
,

\Phi_{t}=\Phi(\cdot,t):U\to M
is a diffeomorphism onto its image,

(2)

for any compact subset
K\subset M
,

K\subset\Phi_{t}(U)
for all
t
in a neighborhood of
1
with
t<1
,

(3)

\frac{1}{1-t}(\Phi_{t}^{-1})^{*}G(t)\xrightarrow[t\nearrow 1]{C^{\infty}_{loc}(M)}\overline{g},

and

(4)

Rm
is uniformly bounded outside
U

\sup_{(x,t)\in(\mathcal{M}\setminus U)\times[t_{0},1)}|Rm|_{g}(x,t)<\infty.

We shall obtain a more precise asymptotic description of the singularity formation in the course of the proof of Theorem
1.1
.
We note that, unlike many previous constructions of Ricci flow singularity formation (e.g.
[
AK04
,
Max14
,
Wu14
,
AIK15
,
App23
,
Sto24
,
Gio20
]
among others), no symmetry or Kähler condition on
(M,\overline{g})
or
(\mathcal{M},G(t))
is assumed.
Recently,
[
LZ24
]
adapted the proof of Theorem
1.1
to the mean curvature flow setting.
Precise definitions of asymptotically conical, shrinking gradient Ricci soliton are provided in Section
2
.

Examples of complete, noncompact Ricci solitons constructed in
[
AK22
]
provide additional motivation for Theorem
1.1
.
In dimensions
n\geq 5
,

[
AK22
]
construct a self-similar Ricci flow solution
(M^{n}_{-},g(t))_{t\in(-\infty,0)}
that converges to a cone
(\mathcal{C},g_{\mathcal{C}})
as
t\nearrow 0
,
and distinct self-similar Ricci flow solutions
(M^{n}_{+},g(t))_{t\in(0,+\infty)}
,
(M^{\prime n}_{+},g^{\prime}(t))_{t\in(0,+\infty)}

that converge to the cone
(\mathcal{C},g_{\mathcal{C}})
as
t\searrow 0
.
Concatenating the time intervals, these solutions demonstrate nonuniqueness of the Ricci flow through singularities in dimensions
n\geq 5
.
These nonuniqueness examples, however, occur on
noncompact
topologies
M_{-},\mathcal{C},M_{+}
.

[
AK22
]
thus leaves open whether this nonuniqueness of the Ricci flow through singularities can
only
occur on noncompact topologies, as is the case for the heat equation,
or if there exist Ricci flows on closed manifolds that demonstrate such nonuniqueness behavior.

The shrinking Ricci solitons in the above example
[
AK22
]
are smooth, complete, asymptotically conical, and gradient.
By demonstrating that these shrinking solitons model singularities of Ricci flows on closed manifolds, Theorem
1.1
thereby provides a first step toward showing that nonuniqueness of the Ricci flow through singularities occurs on compact topologies.
Indeed,
[
AIV
,
IW24
,
LZ24
]
have analogous results for the mean curvature flow.
We expect that such nonuniqueness behavior will inform the nascent theory of weak Ricci flows in high dimensions.

Further applications of Theorem
1.1
come from taking the soliton
(M,\overline{g},\overline{\nabla}f,\lambda=\frac{1}{2})
to be the FIK shrinkers
[
FIK03
]
or their generalizations constructed in
[
DW11
]
.
Theorem
1.1
thereby provides an alternative proof of
[
Max14
, Theorem 1.2]
.
The construction of the Ricci flow
G(t)
in Theorem
1.1
is sufficiently flexible as to conclude
G(t)
is non-Kähler as well (see Remark
4.10
).
While the only known examples of smooth, complete, asymptotically conical, gradient shrinking Ricci solitons come from
[
FIK03
,
DW11
,
AK22
]
, we expect Theorem
1.1
may provide an additional dynamical perspective that can contribute to the construction of new shrinking Ricci solitons.

The outline of the paper is as follows:
We begin in Section
2
by recording properties of shrinking Ricci solitons that will be used throughout the rest of the paper.
Section
3
then outlines the Ważewski retraction principle argument
[
Waż47
]
used to prove Theorem
1.1
.
The remaining Sections
4
–
8
carry out the proof in detail.
First, Section
4
describes the manifold
\mathcal{M}
, initial metric
G(t_{0})
, and map
\Phi
that arise in the statement of Theorem
1.1
as well as sets
\mathcal{B},\mathcal{P}
used to carry out the Ważewski retraction principle argument.
Section
5
records some coarse preliminary estimates on the Ricci flow of
G(t_{0})
,
and then Section
6
provides more precise
C^{2}
control on the evolving metrics.
We use these estimates in Section
7
to describe the behavior of the evolving metrics in a weighted
L^{2}
space.
Finally, Section
8
combines the estimates of the prior sections to complete the proof of Theorem
1.1
.
Appendix
A
provides explicit examples of Riemannian manifolds
(\mathcal{M},G(t_{0}))
considered in Section
4
.
Appendix
B
records the properties of the harmonic map heat flow used throughout the paper.
Appendix
C
states interior Hölder estimates for parabolic equations that are used for the interior estimates in Section
6
.
Appendix
D
lists the constants used throughout the proof and their dependencies for the readers’ convenience.

Acknowledgements:
I would like to thank Dan Knopf for initially suggesting a version of this problem and Brett Kotschwar for useful discussions on asymptotically conical shrinking solitons.
I am grateful for Richard Bamler’s observation that led to the removal of a technical assumption from Theorem
1.1
.

The author is supported by a Leverhulme Trust Early Career Fellowship (ECF-2023-182).

2.
Preliminaries on Shrinking Solitons

Definition 2.1
.

A
shrinking, gradient Ricci soliton
is a triple
(M,g,f)
consisting of a smooth Riemannian manifold
(M,g)
and a smooth function
f:M\to\mathbb{R}
, called the
potential function
, such that

(2.1)

Rc+\nabla^{2}f=\frac{1}{2}g\quad\text{ and }\quad R+|\nabla f|^{2}=f\quad\text{ on }M.

Henceforth, we will simply call
(M,g,f)
a
shrinker
.

Remark 2.2
.

More generally, a shrinking, gradient Ricci soliton could be defined by the equation

Rc+\nabla^{2}f=\lambda g.

The case of
\lambda=\frac{1}{2}
may then be achieved by rescaling
g
.

Additionally,
Rc+\nabla^{2}f=\frac{1}{2}g
implies that
\nabla(R+|\nabla f|^{2}-f)\equiv 0
throughout
M
(see for example
[
CCG+07
, Proposition 1.15]
).
Hence, the second equation in (
2.1
) may be achieved from the first by adding an appropriate constant to
f
on each connected component of
M
.

By taking the trace of
Rc+\nabla^{2}f=\frac{1}{2}g
, we see

Proposition 2.3
.

If
(M^{n},g,f)
is a shrinking, gradient Ricci soliton, then

R+\Delta f=\frac{n}{2}\qquad\text{ on }M.

In particular,
\Delta f-\nabla_{\nabla f}f+f=\frac{n}{2}
on
M
.

Definition 2.4
.

Given a shrinker
(M,g,f)
,
\varphi:M\times(-\infty,1)\to M

will denote the one-parameter family of diffeomorphisms generated by
\frac{1}{1-t}\nabla f
, that is

(2.2)

\partial_{t}\varphi=\frac{1}{1-t}\nabla f\circ\varphi\text{ on }M\times(-\infty,1)\qquad\text{ with }\varphi(\cdot,0)=Id_{M}.

[
Zha09
]
ensures that
\varphi
exists when
(M,g)
is complete.
Using
\varphi
, one can construct a self-similar Ricci flow solution from a shrinker.

Proposition 2.5
.

If
(M,\overline{g},f)
is a shrinker and
(M,\overline{g})
is complete,
then
g(t)\doteqdot(1-t)\varphi_{t}^{*}\overline{g}
evolves by Ricci flow
\partial_{t}g=-2Rc
for all
t\in(-\infty,1)
.

Proposition 2.6
.

If
(M,g,f)
is a complete shrinker, then the scalar curvature
R\geq 0
is nonnegative throughout
M
.

If
M
is also connected, then
R=0
somewhere if and only if
(M,g)
is Ricci-flat.

Proof.

The fact that
R\geq 0
is a direct consequence of
[
Che09
, Corollary 2.5]
and Proposition
2.5
.

The statement regarding equality follows from the fact that
R
satisfies

\Delta R-\nabla_{\nabla f}R-\frac{1}{2}R=-2|Rc|^{2}\leq 0\qquad\text{ on }M

(see for example
[
CCG+07
, Proposition 1.13]
) and the strong maximum principle.
∎

The following result of
[
CZ10
]
says that the potential function of a complete, non-compact shrinker grows quadratically at infinity:

Proposition 2.7

(Theorem 1.1 of
[
CZ10
]
)
.

Let
(M,g,f)
be a complete, non-compact, connected shrinker and let
p_{0}\in M
.
Then the potential function
f:M\to\mathbb{R}
satisfies

\frac{1}{4}\left(\dist_{g}(p_{0},x)-c_{1}\right)^{2}\leq f(x)\leq\frac{1}{4}\left(\dist_{g}(p_{0},x)+c_{2}\right)^{2}\qquad\text{for all }x\in M

where
c_{1},c_{2}
are positive constants depending only on
n
and the geometry of the unit ball
B_{g}(p_{0},1)\subset M
centered at
p_{0}
.

[
CZ10
, theorem 1.2]
also shows that complete, non-compact, connected shrinkers have at most Euclidean volume growth. Combined with Proposition
2.7
, this fact yields the following moment estimates for
f
(see also
[
CZ10
, Corollary 1.1]
):

Proposition 2.8

(Moment estimates)
.

Let
(M,g,f)
be a complete, connected shrinker.
Then, for any
c>0
and
\kappa\geq 0
,

\int_{M}f^{\kappa}e^{-cf}dV_{g}<\infty.

2.1.
Functional Analysis on Shrinking Solitons

Definition 2.9
.

Let
(M,g,f)
be a shrinker. For any tensor
T
, define the operators

\displaystyle\div_{f}(T)\doteqdot\div(T)-\langle\nabla f,T\rangle

\displaystyle\Delta_{f}T\doteqdot\div_{f}(\nabla T)=\Delta T-\nabla_{\nabla f}T.

Observe that these operators satisfy the integration by parts formula

\int_{M}\langle S,\div_{f}(T)\rangle e^{-f}dV_{g}=-\int_{M}\langle\nabla S,\nabla T\rangle e^{-f}dV_{g}

for any smooth, compactly supported tensors
S,T
of the same type.

Proposition 2.10
.

If
(M,g,f)
is a shrinker, then
\div_{f}Rc\equiv 0
.

Proof.

Tracing
Rc+\nabla^{2}f=\frac{1}{2}g
and taking
\nabla
yields

R+\Delta f=\frac{n}{2}\implies\nabla_{j}R+\nabla_{j}\Delta f=0.

The contracted Bianchi identity then implies that

(2.3)

2\nabla^{i}Rc_{ij}+\nabla_{j}\Delta f=0.

On the other hand, taking the divergence of
Rc+\nabla^{2}f=\frac{1}{2}g
and commuting covariant derivatives on
f
yields

(2.4)

0=\nabla^{i}Rc_{ij}+\nabla^{i}\nabla_{i}\nabla_{j}f=\nabla^{i}Rc_{ij}+\nabla_{j}\Delta f+Rc_{jk}\nabla^{k}f.

Subtracting (
2.4
) from (
2.3
) shows that
\div_{f}Rc\equiv 0
. ∎

Remark 2.11
.

The proof of Proposition
2.10
generalizes to steady and expanding gradient Ricci solitons as well.

Definition 2.12

(Weighted Sobolev Spaces)
.

Let
(M,g,f)
be a shrinker.
Let
E
be a vector bundle on an open set
\Omega\subset M
and assume
g
induces a metric on
E
.
Let
\Gamma(\Omega,E)
denote the set of smooth sections of
E
.

L^{2}_{f}(\Omega,E)
is the completion of

\left\{T\in\Gamma(\Omega,E):\int_{\Omega}|T|^{2}_{g}e^{-f}dV_{g}<\infty\right\}

with respect to the inner product

(S,T)_{L^{2}_{f}(\Omega)}\doteqdot\int_{\Omega}\langle S,T\rangle e^{-f}dV_{g}.

For any
m\in\mathbb{N}
,
H^{m}_{f}(\Omega,E)
is the completion of

\left\{T\in\Gamma(\Omega,E):\sum_{j=0}^{m}\int_{\Omega}|\nabla^{j}T|^{2}_{g}e^{-f}dV_{g}<\infty\right\}

with respect to the inner product

(S,T)_{H^{m}_{f}(\Omega)}\doteqdot\sum_{j=0}^{m}\int_{\Omega}\langle\nabla^{j}S,\nabla^{j}T\rangle e^{-f}dV_{g}.

Note
L^{2}_{f}(\Omega,E)=H^{0}_{f}(\Omega,E)
.
In practice, we will often consider the case where
\Omega=M
and simplify the notation by writing
\Gamma(E)=\Gamma(M,E),

L^{2}_{f}(E)=L^{2}_{f}(M,E)
,
(\cdot,\cdot)_{L^{2}_{f}}=(\cdot,\cdot)_{L^{2}_{f}(M)}
, and similarly for
H^{m}_{f}(E)
.
If also the vector bundle
E
is implicit from context, we may analogously write
L^{2}_{f}(M)
or
L^{2}_{f}
.

Lemma 2.13
.

Let
(M^{n},g,f)
be a complete shrinker.
Then there exists a constant
C>0
such that

\|\nabla f\otimes T\|_{L^{2}_{f}}\leq C\|T\|_{H^{1}_{f}}

for any smooth tensor
T
on
M
.

Proof.

By density, we can additionally assume that
T
is compactly supported.
For a constant
C>0
to be determined, consider

0\leq|C\nabla T-\nabla f\otimes T|^{2}

Integrating both sides yields

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} 0&\leq\int_{M}|C\nabla T-\nabla f\otimes T|^{2}e^{-f}dV\\
&=\int_{M}\left[C^{2}|\nabla T|^{2}+|\nabla f|^{2}|T|^{2}\right]e^{-f}dV-C\int_{M}\langle 2\nabla T,\nabla f\otimes T\rangle e^{-f}dV\\
&=\int_{M}\left[C^{2}|\nabla T|^{2}+|\nabla f|^{2}|T|^{2}\right]e^{-f}dV-C\int_{M}\left\langle\nabla\left(|T|^{2}\right),\nabla f\right\rangle e^{-f}dV\\
&=\int_{M}\left[C^{2}|\nabla T|^{2}+|\nabla f|^{2}|T|^{2}\right]e^{-f}dV+C\int_{M}|T|^{2}\div_{f}(\nabla f)e^{-f}dV\\
&=\int_{M}\left[C^{2}|\nabla T|^{2}+|\nabla f|^{2}|T|^{2}\right]e^{-f}dV+C\int_{M}|T|^{2}\left(\Delta f-|\nabla f|^{2}\right)e^{-f}dV\\
&=\int_{M}\left[C^{2}|\nabla T|^{2}+|\nabla f|^{2}|T|^{2}\right]e^{-f}dV+C\int_{M}|T|^{2}\left(-R+\frac{n}{2}-|\nabla f|^{2}\right)e^{-f}dV\\
&\leq\int_{M}\left[C^{2}|\nabla T|^{2}+|\nabla f|^{2}|T|^{2}\right]e^{-f}dV+C\int_{M}|T|^{2}\left(\frac{n}{2}-|\nabla f|^{2}\right)e^{-f}dV\\
\end{aligned}

where the last inequality follows from Proposition
2.6
.
Therefore,

(C-1)\int_{M}|T|^{2}|\nabla f|^{2}e^{-f}dV\leq\int_{M}\left[C^{2}|\nabla T|^{2}+C\frac{n}{2}|T|^{2}\right]e^{-f}dV

Taking
C=2
yields the desired conclusion.
∎

Lemma 2.14
.

Let
(M,g,f)
be a complete shrinker with bounded curvature.
Then there exists a constant
C>0
such that

\|\sqrt{f}T\|_{L^{2}_{f}}\leq C\|T\|_{H^{1}_{f}}

for any smooth tensor
T
on
M
.

Proof.

By density, we can additionally assume that
T
is compactly supported.
By Proposition
2.6
,

0\leq R+|\nabla f|^{2}=f.

Multiplying both sides by
|T|^{2}
and integrating, it follows that

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} &\qquad\|\sqrt{f}T\|_{L^{2}_{f}}^{2}\\
&=\int_{M}f|T|^{2}e^{-f}dV_{g}\\
&=\int_{M}R|T|^{2}e^{-f}dV_{g}+\int_{M}|\nabla f|^{2}|T|^{2}e^{-f}dV_{g}\\
&\leq\left(\sup_{M}|R|\right)\|T\|^{2}_{L^{2}_{f}}+\|\nabla f\otimes T\|^{2}_{L^{2}_{f}}\\
&\leq\left(\sup_{M}|R|\right)\|T\|_{L^{2}_{f}}^{2}+\|T\|_{H^{1}_{f}}^{2}&&(\text{Lemma \ref{multGradfEst}}).\end{aligned}

∎

Combining Proposition
2.7
and Lemma
2.14
yields the following result:

Corollary 2.15
.

Let
(M,g,f)
be a complete, connected shrinker with bounded curvature
and let
p_{0}\in M
.
Then there exists a constant
C>0
such that

\|\dist_{g}(\cdot,p_{0})T\|_{L^{2}_{f}}\leq C\|T\|_{H^{1}_{f}}

for any smooth tensor
T
on
M
.

Lemma 2.16
.

Let
(M,g,f)
be a complete, connected shrinker with bounded curvature
and let
E
be a tensor bundle on
M
.
Then the embedding

H^{1}_{f}(M,E)\to L^{2}_{f}(M,E)

is compact.

Proof.

Let
\{T_{i}\}_{i\in\mathbb{N}}
be a sequence of tensors of the same type and assume the sequence is uniformly bounded in
H^{1}_{f}(M)
.
After passing to a subsequence obtained through a diagonalization argument,
the usual Rellich theorem implies that there exists
T_{\infty}\in H^{1}_{f,loc}(M)

such that

T_{i}\xrightarrow[i\to\infty]{L^{2}_{f,loc}}T_{\infty}

By weak compactness of balls in
H^{1}_{f}(M)
, in fact
T_{\infty}\in H^{1}_{f}(M)
.

Fix
p_{0}\in M
and let
\overline{r}(p)\doteqdot\dist_{g}(p,p_{0})
.
For any
R>0
,

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} &\qquad\|T_{i}-T_{\infty}\|_{L^{2}_{f}(M)}^{2}\\
&=\int_{M}|T_{i}-T_{\infty}|^{2}e^{-f}dV_{g}\\
&=\int_{B_{g}(p_{0},R)}|T_{i}-T_{\infty}|^{2}e^{-f}dV+\int_{M\setminus B_{g}(p_{0},R)}|T_{i}-T_{\infty}|^{2}e^{-f}dV\\
&\leq\|T_{i}-T_{\infty}\|_{L^{2}_{f}(B_{g}(p_{0},R))}^{2}+\frac{1}{R^{2}}\int_{M\setminus B_{g}(p_{0},R)}\overline{r}^{2}|T_{i}-T_{\infty}|^{2}e^{-f}dV\\
&\leq\|T_{i}-T_{\infty}\|_{L^{2}_{f}(B_{g}(p_{0},R))}^{2}+\frac{1}{R^{2}}\|T_{i}-T_{\infty}\|_{H^{1}_{f}(M)}^{2}&&(\text{Corollary \ref{momentEst}})\\
&\leq\|T_{i}-T_{\infty}\|_{L^{2}_{f}(B_{g}(p_{0},R))}^{2}+\frac{C}{R^{2}}\end{aligned}

where
C=\sup_{i}\|T_{i}\|_{H^{1}_{f}}^{2}+\|T_{\infty}\|_{H^{1}_{f}}^{2}<\infty
.
Since the last line may be made arbitrarily small by taking
R
and
i
large,
it follows that in fact

T_{i}\xrightarrow[i\to\infty]{L^{2}_{f}}T_{\infty}.

∎

2.2.
The
\Delta_{f}+2Rm
Operator

Let
(M,g,f)
be a complete shrinker.
In this subsection, we analyze the weighted Lichnerowicz Laplacian
\Delta_{f}+2Rm
associated to the soliton that acts on symmetric 2-tensors
h\in\Gamma(M,Sym^{2}T^{*}M)
in coordinates by

\Delta_{f}h_{ij}+2Rm[h]_{ij}=\nabla^{a}\nabla_{a}h_{ij}-\nabla^{a}f\nabla_{a}h_{ij}+2R^{{{a}\mathchoice{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.68811pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.2058pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{b}\mathchoice{\makebox[3.71356pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.71356pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.29834pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.64166pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.33765pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.33765pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.59009pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.85005pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{i}\mathchoice{\makebox[3.51666pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.51666pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.1029pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.50208pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{j}}}h_{ab}.

Define

D(\Delta_{f})\doteqdot\left\{h\in H^{1}_{f}(M,Sym^{2}T^{*}M)\ |\ \Delta_{f}h\in L^{2}_{f}(M,Sym^{2}T^{*}M)\right\}

where here
\Delta_{f}h
is taken in the sense of distributions.
By
[
Gri09
, theorem 4.6]
,
\Delta_{f}
extends to a self-adjoint operator
\Delta_{f}:D(\Delta_{f})\to L^{2}_{f}(M,Sym^{2}T^{*}M)
that agrees with the usual
\Delta_{f}
operator on smooth, compactly supported 2-tensors.
If the shrinker
(M,g,f)
has bounded curvature, then
\Delta_{f}+2Rm
also defines a self-adjoint operator

\Delta_{f}+2Rm:D(\Delta_{f})\to L^{2}_{f}(M,Sym^{2}T^{*}M).

Lemma 2.17
.

Let
(M,g,f)
be a complete shrinker with bounded curvature.
Then
\Delta_{f}+2Rm
is bounded above on
L^{2}_{f}(M,Sym^{2}T^{*}M)
. In other words, there exists a constant
C
such that

(h,\Delta_{f}h+2Rm[h])_{L^{2}_{f}}\leq C\|h\|_{L^{2}_{f}}\qquad\text{ for all }h\in D(\Delta_{f}).

Proof.

Observe that

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} &\left(h,\Delta_{f}h+2Rm[h]\right)_{L^{2}_{f}}\\
={}&-\int_{M}|\nabla h|^{2}e^{-f}dV_{g}+\int_{M}2\langle Rm[h],h\rangle e^{-f}dV_{g}\\
\leq{}&2\left(\sup_{M}|Rm|\right)\int_{M}|h|^{2}e^{-f}dV_{g}.\end{aligned}

Since
Rm
is uniformly bounded, taking

C=2\left(\sup_{M}|Rm|\right)<\infty

completes the proof.
∎

Lemma 2.18
.

Let
(M,g,f)
be a complete, non-compact shrinker with bounded curvature.
Then the resolvent operator

(-\lambda+\Delta_{f}+2Rm)^{-1}:L^{2}_{f}(M,Sym^{2}T^{*}M)\to L^{2}_{f}(M,Sym^{2}T^{*}M)

is a compact operator for all
\lambda\in\rho(\Delta_{f}+2Rm)
, the resolvent of
\Delta_{f}+2Rm
.

Proof.

Let
k_{j}
be a bounded sequence in
L^{2}_{f}
and define
h_{j}
such that

(-\lambda+\Delta_{f}+2Rm)^{-1}k_{j}=h_{j}\qquad\text{or}\qquad k_{j}=\Delta_{f}h_{j}+2Rm[h_{j}]-\lambda h_{j}.

By definition of the resolvent,
\lambda\in\rho(\Delta_{f}+2Rm)
implies

(-\lambda+\Delta_{f}+2Rm)^{-1}:L^{2}_{f}\to L^{2}_{f}

is bounded and thus

h_{j}
is a bounded sequence in
L^{2}_{f}
.
By integrating both sides of

k_{j}=\Delta_{f}h_{j}+2Rm[h_{j}]-\lambda h_{j}

against
-h_{j}
and using the uniform bounds on
\|h_{j}\|_{L^{2}_{f}}
and
\|k_{j}\|_{L^{2}_{f}}
, it follows that
h_{j}
is a bounded sequence in
H^{1}_{f}
.
By Lemma
2.16
,
h_{j}
then has a convergent subsequence in
L^{2}_{f}
.
∎

Lemmas
2.17
and
2.18
show that
\Delta_{f}+2Rm:D(\Delta_{f})\to L^{2}_{f}
is a self-adjoint operator that’s bounded above and has compact resolvent.

[
RS78
, theorem XIII.64]
therefore yields the following description of its spectrum:

Theorem 2.19
.

Assume
(M,g,f)
is a complete, connected shrinker with bounded curvature.
Then there exists an orthonormal basis
\{h_{j}\}_{j=1}^{\infty}
of
L^{2}_{f}(M,Sym^{2}T^{*}M)

such that, for all
j\in\mathbb{N}
,
h_{j}\in D(\Delta_{f})
is an eigenmode of
\Delta_{f}+2Rm
with eigenvalue
\lambda_{j}\in\mathbb{R}
,
and the eigenvalues
\{\lambda_{j}\}_{j=1}^{\infty}
satisfy
\lambda_{1}\geq\lambda_{2}\geq\dots
.

Moreover, the eigenvalues
\{\lambda_{j}\}_{j=1}^{\infty}
are each of finite-multiplicity, are given by the min-max principle, and tend to
-\infty
as
j\to\infty
.
The spectrum
\sigma(\Delta_{f}+2Rm)
of
\Delta_{f}+2Rm:D(\Delta_{f})\to L^{2}_{f}
equals
\{\lambda_{j}\}_{j=1}^{\infty}
.

Note also that classical elliptic regularity theory implies the eigenmodes
h_{j}
in Theorem
2.19
are smooth as well.

2.2.1.
Geometric Eigenmodes

We can specify a few of the eigenmodes and eigenvalues from Theorem
2.19
explicitly.

Proposition 2.20
.

Let
(M,g,f)
be a shrinker.
Then

\Delta_{f}Rc+2Rm[Rc]=Rc\qquad\text{ on }M.

In other words,
Rc
is an eigenmode of the weighted Lichnerowicz Laplacian with eigenvalue
+1
so long as
Rc\not\equiv 0
.

Proof.

We first note that Proposition
2.10
and the contracted Bianchi identity imply

(2.5)

\nabla_{i}R=2R_{ij}\nabla^{j}f

Applying
\Delta
to the soliton equation (
2.1
)

R_{ij}=\frac{1}{2}g_{ij}-\nabla_{i}\nabla_{j}f

and commuting derivatives, we obtain

\displaystyle\Delta R_{ij}=

\displaystyle-\nabla^{k}\nabla_{k}\nabla_{i}\nabla_{j}f

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\nabla^{k}\left(\nabla_{i}\nabla_{k}\nabla_{j}f+R_{kij}{}^{l}\nabla_{l}f\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\nabla^{k}\nabla_{i}\nabla_{j}\nabla_{k}f-R_{kijl}\nabla^{k}\nabla^{l}f-(\nabla^{k}R_{kijl})\nabla^{l}f

\displaystyle\ =

\displaystyle-\nabla^{k}\nabla_{i}\nabla_{j}\nabla_{k}f-R_{kijl}\nabla^{k}\nabla^{l}f+(\nabla_{l}R_{ji}-\nabla_{j}R_{li})\nabla^{l}f.

Also, using the contracted Bianchi identity and (
2.5
),

\displaystyle\nabla^{k}\nabla_{i}\nabla_{j}\nabla_{k}f=

\displaystyle\nabla_{i}\nabla^{k}\nabla_{k}\nabla_{j}f+R_{kij}{}^{l}\nabla^{k}\nabla_{l}f+R_{il}\nabla_{j}\nabla^{l}f

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\nabla_{i}\nabla^{k}R_{kj}+R_{kij}{}^{l}\nabla^{k}\nabla_{l}f+R_{il}\nabla_{j}\nabla^{l}f

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{2}\nabla_{i}\nabla_{j}R+R_{kij}{}^{l}\nabla^{k}\nabla_{l}f+R_{il}\nabla_{j}\nabla^{l}f

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\nabla_{j}(R_{ik}\nabla^{k}f)+R_{kij}{}^{l}\nabla^{k}\nabla_{l}f+R_{il}\nabla_{j}\nabla^{l}f

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\nabla_{j}R_{ik}\nabla^{k}f-R_{ik}\nabla_{j}\nabla^{k}f+R_{kij}{}^{l}\nabla^{k}\nabla_{l}f+R_{il}\nabla_{j}\nabla^{l}f

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\nabla_{j}R_{ik}\nabla^{k}f+R_{kijl}\nabla^{k}\nabla^{l}f.

Therefore,

\displaystyle\Delta R_{ij}=

\displaystyle\nabla_{j}R_{ik}\nabla^{k}f-2R_{kijl}\nabla^{k}\nabla^{l}f+(\nabla_{l}R_{ji}-\nabla_{j}R_{li})\nabla^{l}f

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-2R_{kijl}(-R^{kl}+\frac{1}{2}g^{kl})+\nabla_{l}R_{ji}\nabla^{l}f

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-2R_{kilj}R^{kl}+\nabla^{l}f\nabla_{l}R_{ij}+R_{ij}.

This completes the proof.
∎

Proposition
2.20
and the soliton equations (
2.1
) also imply the following corollary:

Corollary 2.21
.

If
(M,g,f)
is a shrinker then

\Delta_{f}\nabla^{2}f+2Rm[\nabla^{2}f]=0\qquad\text{ on }M.

In other words,
\nabla^{2}f
is an eigenmode of the weighted Lichnerowicz Laplacian with eigenvalue
0
so long as
\nabla^{2}f\not\equiv 0
.

Remark 2.22
.

For a shrinker
(M,g,f)
, the metric
g
is
not
an eigenmode of
\Delta_{f}+2Rm
in general.

2.2.2.
The Eigenmode Growth Condition

Recall that Theorem
2.19
says that the spectrum of the weighted Lichnerowicz Laplacian on a complete, connected shrinker with bounded curvature consists entirely of finite multiplicity eigenvalues.
We now introduce the eigenmode growth condition which amounts to a pointwise bound at infinity for the eigenmodes
h_{j}
.

Definition 2.23

(Eigenmode Growth Condition)
.

A complete, connected shrinker
(M^{n},g,f)
with bounded curvature is said to satisfy the
eigenmode growth condition
if, for all
\lambda\in\mathbb{R}
and
\delta>0
,
there exists
C=C(n,M,g,f,\lambda,\delta)
such that
if
h\in D(\Delta_{f})

has
\|h\|_{L^{2}_{f}}=1

and satisfies

(2.6)

\Delta_{f}h+2Rm[h]=\lambda h,

then

(2.7)

|h|_{g}(x)\leq Cf(x)^{\max\{-\lambda,0\}+\delta}\qquad\text{ for all }x\in M.

We make a few remarks about this condition before continuing:

•

Informally, one can think of the eigenmode growth condition as saying,

``\Delta_{f}h+2Rm[h]=\lambda h\implies|h|\leq Cf^{-\lambda}"

but where we instead allow for slightly larger growth
f^{-\lambda+\delta}
and add a caveat in the case
\lambda>0
.

•

By Theorem
2.19
, the eigenmode growth condition holds so long as it holds for all
\lambda=\lambda_{j}\in\sigma(\Delta_{f}+2Rm)
and all
h=h_{j}
.

•

When
(M,g,f)
is also not Ricci-flat, the eigenmode growth condition automatically holds
locally
.
Indeed, Proposition
2.6
and (
2.1
) imply
0<R+|\nabla f|^{2}=f
.
Since
f
grows quadratically at infinity (Proposition
2.7
),
\inf_{M}f>0
.
Thus, for any compact
\Omega\subset M
and continuous
h
, there exists a constant
C=C(M,g,f,\lambda,\delta,h,\Omega)
such that

|h|_{g}(x)\leq Cf(x)^{\max\{-\lambda,0\}+\delta}\qquad\text{ for all }x\in\Omega.

•

Shrinking generalized cylinder solitons
M^{n}=\mathbb{R}^{n-k}\times\mathbb{S}^{k}
do
not
satisfy the eigenmode growth condition.
Indeed, the weighted Lichnerowicz Laplacian
\Delta_{f}+2Rm
has eigenmodes of the form
h=(\mathbf{z}\cdot\mathbf{e})g_{\mathbb{S}^{k}}
where
\mathbf{z}
denotes the usual coordinates on
\mathbb{R}^{n-k}
and
\mathbf{e}
is any fixed vector in
\mathbb{R}^{n-k}
.
These eigenmodes have eigenvalue
\lambda=+\frac{1}{2}
but grow linearly at infinity, and thus

|h|\sim f^{\frac{1}{2}}\not\leq Cf^{\delta}=Cf^{\max\{-\frac{1}{2},0\}+\delta}\qquad\text{ for $0<\delta<\frac{1}{2}$}.

•

In the next subsection, we shall see that
(M^{n},g,f)
satisfies the eigenmode growth condition whenever it is asymptotically conical (Proposition
2.34
).
This fact follows from recent results in
[
CM21
]
which uses a weighted version of the frequency functions introduced in
[
Alm79
]
.

•

It is worth mentioning some additional related work.
When
(M,g,f)
is asymptotically conical (see Definition
2.26
below),
[
Ber20
, Theorem 1.2]
showed that a stronger version of the eigenmode growth condition holds for scalar-valued
h=u:M\to\mathbb{R}
.
Also,
[
DS23
]
have a related result for tensors on expanding solitons when (
2.6
) contains an additional Lie derivative term.

2.3.
Asymptotically Conical Shrinking Solitons

In this subsection, we introduce the notion of an asymptotically conical manifold and state some basic results contained in
[
KW15
]
.

Definition 2.24
.

Let
(\Sigma,g_{\Sigma})
be a smooth, closed Riemannian manifold (not necessarily connected).
The (regular)
cone
on
\Sigma
, denoted
(\mathcal{C}(\Sigma),g_{\mathcal{C}})
, is the space
\mathcal{C}(\Sigma)=(0,\infty)\times\Sigma
with the metric

g_{\mathcal{C}}=dr^{2}+r^{2}g_{\Sigma}

where
r
is the standard coordinate on
(0,\infty)
.

We write
\mathcal{C}_{R}(\Sigma)=(R,\infty)\times\Sigma
for any
R\geq 0
.
When the
link

\Sigma
of the cone is clear from context, we shall simply write
\mathcal{C}_{R}
or
\mathcal{C}=\mathcal{C}_{0}
.

For any
\lambda>0
, the
dilation map
is defined by

\displaystyle\rho_{\lambda}:\mathcal{C}_{0}\to\mathcal{C}_{0}

\displaystyle\rho_{\lambda}(r,\sigma)=(\lambda r,\sigma).

Definition 2.25
.

An
end
of a smooth Riemannian manifold
(M,g)
is an unbounded, connected component of
M\setminus\Omega
for some compact
\Omega\subset M
.

Definition 2.26
.

Let
(M,g)
be a smooth Riemannian manifold and let
V
be an end of
M
.
We say that
V
is
asymptotic to the cone

(\mathcal{C}(\Sigma),g_{\mathcal{C}})
if
for some
R\geq 0

there is a diffeomorphism
\Psi:\mathcal{C}_{R}\to V

such that

\lim_{\lambda\to+\infty}\lambda^{-2}\rho_{\lambda}^{*}\Psi^{*}g=g_{\mathcal{C}}\qquad\text{ in }C^{2}_{loc}\left(\mathcal{C}_{0},g_{\mathcal{C}}\right).

We say
V
is an
asymptotically conical end
if it’s asymptotic to some regular cone.

We say
(M,g)
is
asymptotically conical
if
M
is noncompact and has finitely many ends
all of which are asymptotically conical ends.

Remark 2.27
.

By taking the disjoint union of the links and diffeomorphisms for each end
V
in Definition
2.26
, one can show that

(M,g)
is asymptotically conical if and only if there exists some compact
\Omega\subset M
and some smooth, closed
(\Sigma,g_{\Sigma})
(not necessarily connected) such that
for some
R\geq 0
there is a diffeomorphism
\Psi:\mathcal{C}_{R}\to M\setminus\Omega

such that

\lim_{\lambda\to+\infty}\lambda^{-2}\rho_{\lambda}^{*}\Psi^{*}g=g_{\mathcal{C}}\qquad\text{ in }C^{2}_{loc}(\mathcal{C}_{0},g_{\mathcal{C}}).

In this case,
\Sigma
is connected if and only if
M
has exactly one end.

To conclude this subsection, we record some basic facts regarding asymptotically conical shrinkers.
The first result here is from
[
KW15
]
.

Lemma 2.28

(Lemma A.1 in
[
KW15
]
)
.

Let
V
be an end of a smooth Riemannian manifold
(M,g)
.
Let
(\mathcal{C}(\Sigma),g_{\mathcal{C}})
be a (regular) cone and let

\Psi_{R_{0}}:\mathcal{C}_{R_{0}}\to V
be a diffeomorphism for some
R_{0}\geq 0
.
For all
k\geq 0
, define the proposition

(AC_{k})\qquad\lim_{\lambda\to+\infty}\lambda^{-2}\rho_{\lambda}^{*}\Psi^{*}g=g_{\mathcal{C}}\quad\text{ in }C^{k}_{loc}(\mathcal{C}_{0},g_{\mathcal{C}}).

Then

(1)

(AC_{k})
holds if and only if

\lim_{R\to+\infty}R^{l}\|\nabla_{g_{\mathcal{C}}}^{(l)}(\Psi^{*}g-g_{\mathcal{C}})\|_{C^{0}(\mathcal{C}_{R},g_{\mathcal{C}})}=0

for every
l=0,1,\dots,k
.

(2)

If
(AC_{0})
holds, then
the metrics
\Psi^{*}g
and
g_{\mathcal{C}}
are uniformly equivalent on
\overline{\mathcal{C}_{R}}
for any
R>R_{0}
,
and, for any
\epsilon>0
there exists
R>R_{0}
such that

(1-\epsilon)|r-R|\leq\overline{r}_{R}(r,\sigma)\leq(1+\epsilon)|r-R|

for all
(r,\sigma)\in\mathcal{C}_{R}
.
Here and below
\overline{r}_{R}(r,\sigma)=\dist_{\Psi^{*}g}((r,\sigma),\partial\mathcal{C}_{R})
.

(3)

If
(AC_{2})
holds, then
for any
R>R_{0}

there exists a constant
C
depending on
R
and
g_{\Sigma}
such that

\left(1+\overline{r}^{2}_{R}(p)\right)|Rm[{\Psi^{*}g}]|_{\Psi^{*}g}(p)\leq C

for all
p\in\mathcal{C}_{R}
.

When
(M,g,f)
is also a shrinker, the quadratic curvature decay in Lemma
2.28
(
3
) improves to decay of all derivatives of curvature.

Lemma 2.29
.

Let
(M^{n},g,f)
be an asymptotically conical, complete, connected shrinker.
Then for all
m\in\mathbb{N}
, there exists a constant
C_{m}=C_{m}(n,M,g,f,m)
such that

\sup_{M}f^{1+\frac{m}{2}}|\nabla^{m}Rm|_{g}\leq C_{m}.

Proof.

Choose an element
p_{0}
in the compact set
\{p\in M:f(p)\leq 1+\inf_{M}f\}
.

[
KW15
, Proposition 2.1(3)]
implies that for all
m\in\mathbb{N}
, there exists
C_{m}
such that

\sup_{p\in M}\left(1+\dist_{g}(p,p_{0})^{2+m}\right)|\nabla^{m}Rm|_{g}\leq C_{m}.

Since
f
grows quadratically by Proposition
2.7
, the statement of the lemma follows.
∎

Lemma 2.30
.

Let
(M^{n},g,f)
be an asymptotically conical, complete, connected shrinker that is not flat.
Then
(M^{n},g)
is not Ricci-flat, the scalar curvature
R>0
is positive, and
\inf_{M}f>0
.

Proof.

Suppose for the sake of contradiction that
(M,g)
is Ricci-flat.
Consider the family of diffeomorphisms
\varphi(\cdot,t)=\varphi_{t}:M\to M
from Definition
2.4
.
Then
t\mapsto(1-t)\varphi_{t}^{*}g
and
t\mapsto g
are both Ricci flow solutions with initial condition
g
.
Note that Lemma
2.28
implies asymptotically conical manifolds
(M,g)
have bounded curvature and Euclidean volume growth.
By the uniqueness of Ricci flow solutions that are complete with bounded curvature
[
CZ06
]
, it follows that
(1-t)\varphi_{t}^{*}g=g
for all
t\in(-\infty,1)
.
Therefore, for all
t\in(-\infty,1)
,

\sup_{M}|Rm[g]|_{g}=\sup_{M}|Rm[(1-t)\varphi_{t}^{*}g]|_{(1-t)\varphi_{t}^{*}g}=\frac{1}{1-t}\sup_{M}|Rm[g]|_{g},

which forces
(M,g)
to be flat.

Now, since
(M,g)
is complete, connected, and not Ricci-flat, Proposition
2.6
implies the scalar curvature
R>0
is positive.
(
2.1
) then yields

0<R+|\nabla f|^{2}=f\qquad\text{ on }M.

Since
f
also grows quadratically by Proposition
2.7
,
it follows that
\inf_{M}f>0
.
∎

Proposition 2.31

(Weighted Volume Decay)
.

Let
(M^{n},g,f)
be an asymptotically conical, complete, connected shrinker.
There exists a constant
C=C(n,M,g,f)
such that
if
f_{0}\gg 1
is sufficiently large depending on
n,M,g,f
,
then

\int_{\{x\in M:f(x)\geq f_{0}\}}e^{-f}dV_{\overline{g}}\leq Cf_{0}^{\frac{n}{2}-1}e^{-\frac{1}{8}f_{0}}.

Proof.

If
f_{0}\gg 1
is sufficiently large depending on
n,M,g,f
,
then Proposition
2.7
and Lemma
2.28
(2) imply that, after pulling back to the cone, the integral can be estimated by

\displaystyle\int_{\{x\in M:f(x)\geq f_{0}\}}e^{-f}dV_{g}

\displaystyle\leq C\int_{\frac{1}{2}\sqrt{4f_{0}}}^{\infty}r^{n-1}e^{-\frac{1}{2}\frac{r^{2}}{4}}dr

\displaystyle\leq C\sqrt{f_{0}}^{n-2}e^{-\frac{1}{8}\sqrt{f_{0}}^{2}}

\displaystyle(f_{0}\gg 1)

\displaystyle=Cf_{0}^{\frac{n}{2}-1}e^{-\frac{1}{8}f_{0}}.

where the constants
C
above may change from line to line but depend only on
n,M,g,f
.
∎

Remark 2.32
.

One ought to be able to improve Proposition
2.31
to

\int_{\{x\in M:f(x)\geq f_{0}\}}e^{-f}dV_{\overline{g}}\leq Cf_{0}^{\frac{n}{2}-1}e^{-f_{0}}.

However, Proposition
2.31
as written will suffice for our applications.

Lemma 2.33
.

Let
(M^{n},g,f)
be an asymptotically conical, complete, connected shrinker.
Recall that the one-parameter family
\{\varphi(\cdot,t)=\varphi_{t}:M\to M\}_{t\in(-\infty,1)}
of diffeomorphisms from Definition
2.4
is defined by

(
2.2
)

\partial_{t}\varphi_{t}=\frac{1}{1-t}\nabla f\circ\varphi_{t},\qquad\varphi_{0}=Id_{M}.

There exists a constant
C=C(n,M,g,f)
such that

\max\left\{0,\varphi_{t}^{*}f-\frac{C}{\varphi_{t}^{*}f}\right\}\leq(1-t)\partial_{t}\left(\varphi_{t}^{*}f\right)\leq\varphi_{t}^{*}f\qquad\text{ for all }t\in(-\infty,1).

Proof.

First, observe that

\displaystyle\partial_{t}(\varphi_{t}^{*}f)

\displaystyle=\frac{\partial}{\partial t}\left(f\circ\varphi_{t}\right)

\displaystyle=\langle\nabla f\circ\varphi_{t},\partial_{t}\varphi_{t}\rangle

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{1-t}|\nabla f|^{2}_{g}\circ\varphi_{t}

(
2.2
)

\displaystyle\geq 0.

By (
2.1
) and Proposition
2.6
,

0\leq|\nabla f|^{2}\leq R+|\nabla f|^{2}=f.

Hence,

\partial_{t}(\varphi_{t}^{*}f)=\frac{1}{1-t}|\nabla f|_{g}^{2}\circ\varphi_{t}\leq\frac{1}{1-t}f\circ\varphi_{t}=\frac{1}{1-t}\varphi_{t}^{*}f

which proves the upper bound on
(1-t)\partial_{t}\varphi_{t}^{*}f
.

For the other lower bound,
first note that Lemma
2.29
and Proposition
2.7
imply there exists
C=C(n,M,g,f)
such that

0\leq R(x)\leq\frac{C}{f(x)}\qquad\text{ for all }x\in M.

Thus, by (
2.1
),

|\nabla f|^{2}_{g}=f-R\geq f-\frac{C}{f},

and so

\partial_{t}(\varphi_{t}^{*}f)=\frac{1}{1-t}|\nabla f|^{2}_{g}\circ\varphi_{t}\geq\left(f-\frac{C}{f}\right)\circ\varphi_{t}=\varphi_{t}^{*}f-\frac{C}{\varphi_{t}^{*}f}.

∎

Proposition 2.34
.

If
(M^{n},g,f)
is an asymptotically conical, complete, connected shrinker that is not flat,
then
(M^{n},g,f)
satisfies the eigenmode growth condition (Definition
2.23
).

Proof.

Let
\delta>0
and

j\in\mathbb{N}
.
As in Theorem
2.19
,
denote the corresponding eigenvalue
\lambda_{j}\in\sigma(\Delta_{f}+2Rm)

and its eigenmode

h_{j}
with
\|h_{j}\|_{L^{2}_{f}}=1
and

(
2.6
)

\Delta_{f}h_{j}+2Rm[h_{j}]=\lambda_{j}h_{j}.

By standard elliptic regularity and unique continuation results,

h_{j}
is smooth and cannot vanish on any open set.

Taking the inner product of (
2.6
) with
h_{j}
implies

\langle\Delta_{f}h_{j},h_{j}\rangle_{g}=(\lambda_{j}-C_{n}|Rm|_{g})|h_{j}|^{2}\geq-(\max\{-\lambda_{j},0\}+C_{n}|Rm|_{g})|h_{j}|^{2}

for some constant
C_{n}>0
that depends only on
n
.
Since
|Rm|_{g}
decays quadratically (Lemma
2.28
(
3
)),
there exists a compact set
\Omega\subset M
such that

C_{n}|Rm|_{g}(x)\leq\delta\qquad\text{for all }x\in M\setminus\Omega.

Thus,

\langle\Delta_{f}h_{j},h_{j}\rangle_{g}\geq-(\max\{-\lambda_{j},0\}+\delta)|h|_{g}^{2}\qquad\text{for all }x\in M\setminus\Omega.

[
CM21
, Theorem 0.7]
now applies to show
|h_{j}|
grows at most polynomially with rate
2\max\{-\lambda_{j},0\}+2\delta
at infinity.
Because
\inf_{M}f>0
(Lemma
2.30
) and
f
grows quadratically (Proposition
2.7
),
it follows that, for some
C_{j}=C_{j}(n,M,g,f,j,\delta)
,

|h_{j}|_{g}(x)\leq C_{j}f(x)^{\max\{-\lambda_{j},0\}+\delta}\qquad\text{for all }x\in M.

Since the eigenspaces of
\Delta_{f}+2Rm
have finite dimension (Theorem
2.19
),
the eigenmode growth condition (Definition
2.23
) for general
\lambda\in\mathbb{R}
follows with
C=C(n,M,g,f,\lambda,\delta)=\max_{\lambda_{j}=\lambda}C_{j}
.
∎

For asymptotically conical shrinkers, the pointwise bound in the eigenmode growth condition can be bootstrapped to derivative bounds of any order.

Proposition 2.35
.

Let
(M^{n},g,f)
be an asymptotically conical, complete, connected shrinker that is not flat.
Let
h_{j}
be an eigenmode of the weighted Lichnerowicz Laplacian
\Delta_{f}+2Rm
with eigenvalue
\lambda_{j}
as in Theorem
2.19
.
Then for all
\delta>0
and all
m\in\mathbb{N}
, there exists
C_{m}=C_{m}(n,M,g,f,j,\delta,m)
such that

|\nabla^{m}h_{j}|_{g}(x)\leq C_{m}f(x)^{\max\{-\lambda_{j},0\}+\delta}\qquad\text{ for all }x\in M.

Proof.

We shall add a time variable
t
and apply a rescaling argument to bootstrap the
C^{0}
-estimates

|h_{j}|_{g}\leq C_{0}(n,M,g,f,j,\delta)f^{\max\{-\lambda_{j},0\}+\delta}

from Proposition
2.34
to
C^{m}
-estimates.
Consider the family
\mathcal{H}(t)
of symmetric 2-tensors on
M
given by

\mathcal{H}(t)\doteqdot(1-t)\varphi_{t}^{*}h_{j}

where
\varphi_{t}:M\to M
is the family of diffeomorphisms solving

(
2.2
)

\partial_{t}\varphi_{t}=\frac{1}{1-t}\nabla f\circ\varphi_{t}\qquad\text{with}\qquad\varphi_{0}=Id_{M}:M\to M.

Then
\mathcal{H}
satisfies the evolution equation

\displaystyle\partial_{t}\mathcal{H}

\displaystyle={}

\displaystyle-\varphi_{t}^{*}h+(1-t)\varphi_{t}^{*}\mathcal{L}_{\partial_{t}\varphi\circ\varphi_{t}^{-1}}h_{j}

\displaystyle={}

\displaystyle-\varphi_{t}^{*}h+\varphi_{t}^{*}\mathcal{L}_{\nabla f}h_{j}+\varphi_{t}^{*}\left(\Delta_{f}h_{j}+2Rm[h_{j}]-\lambda_{j}h_{j}\right)

\displaystyle={}

\displaystyle-\varphi_{t}^{*}h+\varphi_{t}^{*}\left(\nabla_{\nabla f}h_{j}+(h_{j})_{ac}\nabla_{b}\nabla^{c}f+(h_{j})_{bc}\nabla_{a}\nabla^{c}f\right)

\displaystyle+\varphi_{t}^{*}\left(\Delta h_{j}-\nabla_{\nabla f}h_{j}+2Rm[h_{j}]-\lambda_{j}h_{j}\right)

\displaystyle={}

\displaystyle\varphi_{t}^{*}\left(\Delta h_{j}+2Rm[h_{j}]-(h_{j})_{ac}Rc_{b}^{c}-(h_{j})_{bc}Rc_{a}^{c}-\lambda_{j}h_{j}\right)

where we use the soliton equation (
2.1
)
Rc+\nabla^{2}f=\frac{1}{2}g
in the last equality.
With respect to the metrics
\check{g}(t)\doteqdot(1-t)\varphi_{t}^{*}g
, it follows that

(2.8)

\partial_{t}\mathcal{H}=\check{\Delta}\mathcal{H}+2\check{Rm}[\mathcal{H}]-\check{Rc}*\mathcal{H}-\frac{\lambda_{j}}{1-t}\mathcal{H}

where
\check{\Delta}=\Delta_{\check{g}},\check{Rm}=Rm_{\check{g}},
and
\check{Rc}=Rc_{\check{g}}
.
Note that
\check{g}(t)=(1-t)\varphi_{t}^{*}g
is the Ricci flow solution with
\check{g}(0)=g

and is smooth with uniformly bounded curvature for all
t\in[-1,0]
.
Hence, for all
t\in[-1,0]
, the coefficients in (
2.8
) and their derivatives of order at most
m
can be uniformly bounded by constants that depend only on
n,M,g,f,j,m
.

Since
|Rm_{g}|_{g}
is uniformly bounded, the conjugate radius of
(M,g)
is bounded below by say
2r=2r(n,M,g,f)\in\left(0,1\right]
.
After possibly passing to a local cover and pulling back to
\mathbb{R}^{n}
using the exponential map (with respect to
g
) based at an arbitrary
p\in M
,
Lemma
C.1
applies to equation (
2.8
)
with
\Omega^{\prime}=B_{2r}\times[-4r^{2},0]\subset\mathbb{R}^{n}\times[-1,0]

and no inhomogeneous term.
Since the coefficients of (
2.8
) and
r
have bounds that depend only on
n,M,g,f,j
, it follows from Lemma
C.1
that

\displaystyle\quad\,\,|\nabla^{m}h_{j}|_{g}(p)

\displaystyle=|\check{\nabla}^{m}\mathcal{H}|_{\check{g}}(p,t=0)

\displaystyle\leq C(n,M,g,f,j,m)\sup_{(x,t)\in B_{g}(p,2r)\times[-1,0]}|\mathcal{H}|_{\check{g}}(x,t)

\displaystyle(\text{Lemma \ref{Lem Lin Int Est}})

\displaystyle=C(n,M,g,f,j,m)\sup_{(x,t)\in B_{g}(p,2r)\times[-1,0]}|h_{j}|_{g}(\varphi_{t}(x))

\displaystyle\leq C(n,M,g,f,j,\delta,m)\sup_{(x,t)\in B_{g}(p,2r)\times[-1,0]}f(\varphi_{t}(x))^{\max\{-\lambda_{j},0\}+\delta}

\displaystyle(\text{Proposition \ref{Prop Asymp Conical Implies Eigmode Growth Assumption}}).

By Lemma
2.33
,
0\leq\partial_{t}\varphi_{t}^{*}f
and thus
f(\varphi_{t}(x))\leq f(\varphi_{0}(x))=f(x)
for all
t\leq 0
.
Hence,

\displaystyle|\nabla^{m}h_{j}|_{g}(p)

\displaystyle\leq C(n,M,g,f,j,\delta,m)\sup_{(x,t)\in B_{g}(p,2r)\times[-1,0]}f(\varphi_{t}(x))^{\max\{-\lambda_{j},0\}+\delta}

\displaystyle\leq C(n,M,g,f,j,\delta,m)\sup_{x\in B_{g}(p,2r)}f(x)^{\max\{-\lambda_{j},0\}+\delta}

\displaystyle\leq C(n,M,g,f,j,\delta,m)f(p)^{\max\{-\lambda_{j},0\}+\delta}

where the last equality follows from the fact that
f
grows quadratically (Proposition
2.7
) and has
\inf_{M}f>0
(Lemma
2.30
).
This completes the proof since
p\in M
was arbitrary and no constants depend on
p
. ∎

3.
Strategy of the Proof of Theorem
1.1

Having completed our preliminary discussion of gradient shrinking Ricci solitons, we shall now fix a few choices for the remainder of the paper.

Assumption 3.1
.

Henceforth,
(M,\overline{g},f)
will denote an asymptotically conical, complete, connected shrinker that is not flat.
We shall use overlines to refer to geometric objects associated to
(M,\overline{g},f)
, e.g.
\overline{Rm},\overline{\nabla},
etc.
As in Theorem
2.19
, fix an
L^{2}_{f}
-orthonormal basis
\{h_{j}\}_{j\in\mathbb{N}}
of eigenmodes with corresponding eigenvalues
\{\lambda_{j}\}_{j\in\mathbb{N}}
for the weighted Lichnerowicz Laplacian
\overline{\Delta}_{f}+2\overline{Rm}
.
Fix
\lambda_{*}\in(-\infty,0)\setminus\{\lambda_{j}\}_{j\in\mathbb{N}}
and let
K\in\mathbb{N}
denote the eigenmode such that
\lambda_{K}>\lambda_{*}>\lambda_{K+1}
.

We now proceed to sketch the proof of Theorem
1.1
.
Outside of some compact set,
(M,\overline{g})
looks approximately like a cone
(\mathcal{C}(\Sigma),g_{\mathcal{C}})
.
We can therefore find a suitably large precompact set
M^{\prime}\subset M
whose closure
\overline{M^{\prime}}
is a smooth manifold with boundary.
Identifying two copies of
\overline{M^{\prime}}
along their boundary yields a closed manifold
\mathcal{M}
known as the double of
\overline{M^{\prime}}
.

After adjusting the metric
\overline{g}
near
\partial\overline{M^{\prime}}
,
\overline{g}
induces a
\mathbb{Z}_{2}
-invariant Riemannian metric on
\mathcal{M}
which equals
\overline{g}
away from
\partial\overline{M^{\prime}}\subset\mathcal{M}
.
Here, the
\mathbb{Z}_{2}
symmetry interchanges the two copies of
M^{\prime}
in the double
\mathcal{M}
.

We can repeat this same process with the family of metrics

G_{\mathbf{p}}(t_{0})=(1-t_{0})\varphi_{t_{0}}^{*}\left[\overline{g}+\sum_{j=1}^{K}p_{j}h_{j}\right]

indexed by
\mathbf{p}=(p_{1},p_{2},\dots,p_{K})\in\mathbb{R}^{K}
and
t_{0}\in[0,1)

to obtain a family of
\mathbb{Z}_{2}
-invariant metrics
G_{\mathbf{p}}(t_{0})
on
\mathcal{M}
.

Consider the evolution of each of these metrics by Ricci flow.
In other words, let
\{G(t)=G_{\mathbf{p}}(t)\}_{t\in[t_{0},T(\mathbf{p}))}
denote the maximal solution to the Ricci flow on
\mathcal{M}

\partial_{t}G=-2Rc\qquad\text{ with initial condition }G(t_{0})=G_{\mathbf{p}}(t_{0}).

These metrics on
\mathcal{M}
remain
\mathbb{Z}_{2}
-invariant and can thus be identified with a Ricci flow solution on
M^{\prime}\subset M
.
We extend these to
approximate
Ricci flow solutions
\acute{G}_{\mathbf{p}}(t)
on
M
by extending
G_{\mathbf{p}}(t)
by
(1-t)\varphi_{t}^{*}\overline{g}
on
M\setminus M^{\prime}
and smoothing near
\partial\overline{M^{\prime}}
.

We then rescale and reparametrize
\acute{G}_{\mathbf{p}}(t)
to

g_{\mathbf{p}}(t)\doteqdot\frac{1}{1-t}(\Phi_{t}^{-1})^{*}\acute{G}_{\mathbf{p}}(t)

where
\Phi_{t}:M\to M

is given by
\Phi_{t}=\varphi_{t}\circ\tilde{\Phi}_{t}
.
Here
\varphi_{t}:M\to M
is the family of diffeomorphisms associated to the soliton

\partial_{t}\varphi_{t}=\frac{1}{1-t}\nabla f\circ\varphi_{t}\qquad\text{ with }\varphi_{0}=Id_{M}

and
\tilde{\Phi}_{t}:M\to M
solves the harmonic map heat flow

\partial_{t}\tilde{\Phi}_{t}=\Delta_{\acute{G}_{\mathbf{p}}(t),(1-t)\varphi_{t}^{*}\overline{g}}\tilde{\Phi}_{t}\qquad\text{ with }\tilde{\Phi}_{t_{0}}=Id_{M}.

Remark 3.2
.

Note that
g_{\mathbf{p}}(t)
is only well-defined when
\Phi_{t}
or equivalently
\tilde{\Phi}_{t}
is well-defined and a diffeomorphism.
We address this issue by, in effect, developing well-posedness results for the solution
\tilde{\Phi}_{t}
of the harmonic map heat flow that hold so long as
h_{\mathbf{p}}=g_{\mathbf{p}}-\overline{g}
is small.
Subsection
8.2
and Appendix
B
establish these well-posedness results.
This well-posedness when
h_{\mathbf{p}}
is small will be sufficient for our Ważewski box argument below to show that
h_{\mathbf{p}}
must exit the box
\mathcal{B}
before
\tilde{\Phi}_{t}
fails to exist, be unique, or be a diffeomorphism.

In order to prove Theorem
1.1
, we will show that for some
\mathbf{p}
and
t_{0}
,
g_{\mathbf{p}}(t)
converges to the soliton metric
\overline{g}
as
t\nearrow 1
.
To do so, we consider the difference
h_{\mathbf{p}}(t)\doteqdot g_{\mathbf{p}}(t)-\overline{g}
.
This difference satisfies an equation of the form

(1-t)\partial_{t}h_{\mathbf{p}}=\overline{\Delta}_{f}h_{\mathbf{p}}+2\overline{Rm}[h_{\mathbf{p}}]+\mathcal{E}_{1}+\mathcal{E}_{2}

where
\mathcal{E}_{1},\mathcal{E}_{2}
are small error terms that arise from linearizing the Ricci tensor at
\overline{g}
and the fact that
\acute{G}_{\mathbf{p}}(t)
is only an approximate Ricci flow solution, respectively.
The details of the construction thus far are provided in detail in Subsections
4.1
–
4.3
.

Because the linearized operator
\overline{\Delta}_{f}+2\overline{Rm}
has positive eigenvalues (Proposition
2.20
), one should not expect
h_{\mathbf{p}}(t)
to converge to
0
for general choices of
\mathbf{p}
and
t_{0}
.
If the flow were given purely by the linearized equation

(1-t)\partial_{t}h_{\mathbf{p}}\approx\overline{\Delta}_{f}h_{\mathbf{p}}+2\overline{Rm}[h_{\mathbf{p}}]

one could simply take
\mathbf{p}=\mathbf{0}
and show
h_{\mathbf{0}}(t)
limits to
0
.
However, the nonlinear error terms
\mathcal{E}_{1},\mathcal{E}_{2}
preclude such an argument from going through.

Beginning in Subsection
4.4
, we implement a Ważewski box argument
[
Waż47
]
to show that
h_{\mathbf{p}}(t)
converges to
0
as
t\nearrow 1
for
some
small
\mathbf{p}
and
t_{0}<1
sufficiently close to
1
.
Informally, the general strategy is as follows: we design a time-dependent set
\mathcal{B}
of time-dependent symmetric 2-tensors that becomes more restrictive as
t\nearrow 1
.
Namely, the set
\mathcal{B}
consists of time-dependent symmetric 2-tensors
h(t)
on
M
that display an
L^{2}_{f}(M)
decay and uniform
C^{2}(M)
bounds on a given time interval.
Through Sections
5
–
8
, we prove a series of estimates designed to show that, for a suitable choice of parameters, if
h_{\mathbf{p}}
exits the set
\mathcal{B}
before time
t=1
then it must exit through a
(K-1)
-dimensional sphere
S^{K-1}
in the boundary
\partial\mathcal{B}
of
\mathcal{B}
.
If, for all
\mathbf{p}
in
K
-dimensional ball
B^{K}\subset\mathbb{R}^{K}
,
h_{\mathbf{p}}(t)
were to exit
\mathcal{B}
at some time
t^{*}(\mathbf{p})<1
, then the exit map
\mathbf{p}\mapsto h_{\mathbf{p}}(t^{*}(\mathbf{p}))
would yield a continuous map
B^{K}\to S^{K-1}
that, by construction, restricts to a map
S^{K-1}\approx\partial B^{K}\to S^{K-1}
on the boundary which is homotopic to the identity.
However, such a map cannot exist as the identity map
Id:S^{K-1}\to S^{K-1}
is not null-homotopic.
Therefore there must exist some
\mathbf{p}^{*}
such that
h_{\mathbf{p}^{*}}(t)
remains in
\mathcal{B}
for all
t\in[t_{0},1)
.
By the construction of
\mathcal{B}
, the corresponding Ricci flow
G_{\mathbf{p}^{*}}(t)
then yields the desired Ricci flow in Theorem
1.1
.

Sections
5
–
7
provide the estimates necessary to make the above argument rigorous.
We complete the proof of Theorem
1.1
in Section
8
.

[
AV97
,
AIK15
,
LZ24
]
have successfully used a similar strategy in other singularity constructions for the mean curvature flow and Ricci flow.

[
HV
,
Vel94
,
Miz04
,
BS17
,
Sto24
]
among others have also applied a closely related technique to exhibit singularity formation for parabolic partial differential equations and geometric flows.

Remark 3.3
.

For concreteness, we outlined the proof strategy above when
\mathcal{M}
is the double of
\overline{M}^{\prime}\subset M
and the metrics
G_{\mathbf{p}}(t)
on
\mathcal{M}
are
\mathbb{Z}_{2}
-invariant.
However, because the analysis mainly depends on the behavior of the rescaled and reparameterized metrics
g_{\mathbf{p}}(t)
on the soliton manifold
M
, the proof in fact generalizes to a broader class of closed manifolds
\mathcal{M}
and symmetries
\mathcal{S}
defined in Subsection
4.1
below.

4.
Setup

4.1.
The Manifold

Consider the asymptotically conical shrinker
(M^{n},\overline{g},f)
.
For any
\Gamma_{0}>1
, define the open submanifold
M^{\prime}=M^{\prime}_{\Gamma_{0}}\doteqdot\{x\in M:f(x)<100\Gamma_{0}\}\subset M
.

Assume
\mathcal{M}^{n}
is a closed
n
-manifold that contains (finitely many) disjoint diffeomorphic copies
\mathcal{M}_{\omega}^{\prime\prime}\subsetneq\mathcal{M}
of
M
indexed by
\omega
in some (finite) set
A
.
Fix diffeomorphisms
\iota_{\omega}^{\prime\prime}:\mathcal{M}_{\omega}^{\prime\prime}\to M
for each
\omega\in A
and let
\iota^{\prime\prime}
be the naturally induced diffeomorphism
\iota^{\prime\prime}:\sqcup_{\omega\in A}\mathcal{M}_{\omega}^{\prime\prime}\to\sqcup_{\omega\in A}M
.
For all
\omega\in A
, these maps restrict to diffeomorphisms

\iota_{\omega}:\mathcal{M}_{\omega}^{\prime}\to M^{\prime}=\{x\in M:f(x)<100\Gamma_{0}\}

on some open subset
\mathcal{M}_{\omega}^{\prime}\subset\mathcal{M}_{\omega}^{\prime\prime}\subset\mathcal{M}
.
Let

\mathcal{M}^{\prime}\doteqdot\bigsqcup_{\omega\in A}\mathcal{M}_{\omega}^{\prime}\subset\mathcal{M}\quad\text{and}\quad\iota:\mathcal{M}^{\prime}\to\bigsqcup_{\omega\in A}M^{\prime}

denote the diffeomorphism naturally obtained from the
\iota_{\omega}
.
Note that the
\iota_{\omega}
and
\iota
depend on
\Gamma_{0}
, but the
\iota_{\omega}^{\prime\prime}
and
\iota^{\prime\prime}
are independent of
\Gamma_{0}
.

While
f:M\to\mathbb{R}
is strictly speaking a function on
M
, we will abuse notation and use
f
to also refer to the continuous, piecewise smooth function
\mathcal{M}\to[0,100\Gamma_{0}]\subset\mathbb{R}
defined by
\iota_{\omega}^{*}f
on every
\mathcal{M}_{\omega}^{\prime}\approx M^{\prime}
and extended by
100\Gamma_{0}
on
\mathcal{M}\setminus\mathcal{M}^{\prime}
.
Note that
f:\mathcal{M}\to\mathbb{R}
is smooth on the complement of
\partial\mathcal{M}^{\prime}\subset\mathcal{M}
and also depends on
\Gamma_{0}
.

Assume furthermore that
\mathcal{S}\leq\text{Diff}(\mathcal{M})
is a subgroup of the diffeomorphism group of
\mathcal{M}
that respects the identifications
\iota_{\omega}^{\prime\prime}:\mathcal{M}^{\prime\prime}_{\omega}\to M
.
Namely, for all
\psi\in\mathcal{S}
and all
\omega\in A
, there exists
\tilde{\omega}\in A
such that

(4.1)

\psi(\mathcal{M}^{\prime\prime}_{\omega})=\mathcal{M}^{\prime\prime}_{\tilde{\omega}}\text{ and }\iota_{\tilde{\omega}}^{\prime\prime}\circ\psi|_{\mathcal{M}^{\prime\prime}_{\omega}}=\iota_{\omega}^{\prime\prime}:\mathcal{M}^{\prime\prime}_{\omega}\to M.

By restriction, the same properties hold for every
\mathcal{M}^{\prime}_{\omega}\subset\mathcal{M}^{\prime\prime}_{\omega}
, that is, for any
\psi\in\mathcal{S}
and any
\omega\in A
there exists
\tilde{\omega}\in A
such that

(4.2)

\psi(\mathcal{M}^{\prime}_{\omega})=\mathcal{M}^{\prime}_{\tilde{\omega}}\text{ and }\iota_{\tilde{\omega}}\circ\psi|_{\mathcal{M}^{\prime}_{\omega}}=\iota_{\omega}:\mathcal{M}^{\prime}_{\omega}\to M.

In particular,
\psi(\mathcal{M}^{\prime})=\mathcal{M}^{\prime}
,
\iota\circ\psi=\iota:\mathcal{M}^{\prime}\to\sqcup_{\omega\in A}M^{\prime}
, and
\psi^{*}f=f:\mathcal{M}\to[0,100\Gamma_{0}]
.
It follows that there is also an induced action of
\mathcal{S}
on
A
by permutations where, given
\psi\in\mathcal{S}
and
\omega\in A
,
\psi\cdot\omega
is the unique element of
A
such that
\psi(\mathcal{M}_{\omega}^{\prime\prime})=\mathcal{M}_{\psi\cdot\omega}^{\prime\prime}
.
By replacing
A
with an orbit of this
\mathcal{S}
action, we shall additionally assume without loss of generality that
\mathcal{S}
acts transitively on
A
.

This data is summarized in Figure
1
below.

Remark 4.1
.

For the simplest situation, one can consider the case where
\mathcal{S}=\{Id\}
and
A
is a set of one element.
In this case,
\mathcal{M}
is a closed manifold that contains a diffeomorphic copy
\mathcal{M}^{\prime\prime}
of
M
.
There is a fixed diffeomorphism
\iota^{\prime\prime}:\mathcal{M}^{\prime\prime}\to M
and the restriction
\iota:\mathcal{M}^{\prime}\to M^{\prime}
.
The function
f:\mathcal{M}\to[0,100\Gamma_{0}]
is defined to be
\iota^{*}f
on
\mathcal{M}^{\prime}
and
100\Gamma_{0}
on
\mathcal{M}\setminus\mathcal{M}^{\prime}
.

In Appendix
A
, we present a construction where
\mathcal{S}\cong\mathbb{Z}_{2}
and
A
is a set of two elements.
The manifold
\mathcal{M}
in this case is the double of a large subset of
M
, and
\mathcal{S}
permutes the two pieces of the double. (See also Remark
4.4
.)

\mathcal{M}
\approx
\iota_{\omega}:\mathcal{M}_{\omega}^{\prime}\to M^{\prime}
\mathcal{M}^{\prime}_{\omega}\subset\mathcal{M}^{\prime\prime}_{\omega}
M^{\prime}\subset M
\mathcal{M}^{\prime}=\bigsqcup_{\omega\in A}\mathcal{M}_{\omega}^{\prime}

.

Remark 4.3
.

Since
\mathcal{M}
is closed, the metrics
G_{\mathbf{0}}(t_{0})

Because any
\psi\in\mathcal{S}

) and is an isometry (Definition
