)), it follows that if
\psi_{1},\psi_{2}\in\mathcal{S}
and

\psi_{1}(\mathcal{M}_{\omega}^{\prime\prime})=\psi_{2}(\mathcal{M}_{\omega}^{\prime\prime})
for some
\omega\in A
, then
\psi_{1}=\psi_{2}
.
In other words, the action of
\mathcal{S}
on
A
is free.
In particular,
\mathcal{S}
must be a finite group.

Remark 4.4
.

It is not clear a priori that such families of metrics
G_{\mathbf{0}}(\Gamma_{0},t_{0})
exist.
Appendix
A
provides a detailed construction of such an assignment of metrics when
\mathcal{M}
is the double of a suitable truncation of
M
and
\mathcal{S}\cong\mathbb{Z}_{2}
is the action that exchanges the two pieces of the double.
The assignment of metrics
G_{0}(t_{0})
informally looks like the soliton metric
(1-t_{0})\varphi_{t_{0}}^{*}\overline{g}
on each piece of the double but has been smoothed near the doubling boundary.

For
\gamma_{0}>0
, let
\eta_{\gamma_{0}}:M\to[0,1]
denote a compactly supported bump function
such that:

(1)

\eta_{\gamma_{0}}(x)=1
for all
x\in M
such that
f(x)\leq\frac{\gamma_{0}}{2(1-t_{0})}
,

(2)

\supp\eta_{\gamma_{0}}\subset\left\{x\in M:f(x)<\frac{\gamma_{0}}{1-t_{0}}\right\}
,

(3)

\overline{\{x\in M:0<\eta_{\gamma_{0}}(x)<1\}}\subset\left\{x\in M:\frac{\gamma_{0}}{2(1-t_{0})}<f(x)<\frac{\gamma_{0}}{1-t_{0}}\right\}
, and

(4)

for all
m\in\mathbb{N}
, there exists
C
depending only on
n,M,\overline{g},f,m
such that, if
0<1-t_{0}\ll 1
is sufficiently small, then

|\overline{\nabla}^{m}\eta_{\gamma_{0}}|_{\overline{g}}\leq C.

Note that
\eta_{\gamma_{0}}
depends on
t_{0}
as well but we will typically elide this dependence.
Such bump functions
\eta_{\gamma_{0}}:M\to[0,1]
can be obtained by taking a suitable bump function
\hat{\eta}:(0,\infty)\to[0,1]
and letting

\eta_{\gamma_{0}}(x)=\hat{\eta}\left(\frac{1-t_{0}}{\gamma_{0}}f(x)\right).

In particular, Equations (
2.1
), Corollary
2.21
, and the proof of Proposition
2.35
adapted to the case of
\delta=0
imply derivative bounds for
f
,
which then yield the desired derivative bounds for
\eta_{\gamma_{0}}(x)=\hat{\eta}\left(\frac{1-t_{0}}{\gamma_{0}}f(x)\right)
.

For all
\mathbf{p}=(p_{1},\dots,p_{K})\in\mathbb{R}^{K}
with
|\mathbf{p}|\leq\overline{p}(1-t_{0})^{|\lambda_{*}|}
, define a (possibly non-smooth) symmetric 2-tensor
G_{\mathbf{p}}(t_{0})=G_{\mathbf{p}}(\Gamma_{0},\gamma_{0},t_{0})
on
\mathcal{M}
by

(4.3)

G_{\mathbf{p}}(t_{0})\doteqdot G_{\mathbf{0}}(t_{0})+(1-t_{0})\iota^{*}\varphi_{t_{0}}^{*}\left(\eta_{\gamma_{0}}\sum_{j=1}^{K}p_{j}h_{j}\right).

Note that a priori these
G_{\mathbf{p}}(t_{0})
need not be metrics on
\mathcal{M}
nor even smooth.
To conclude this subsection, we show when the symmetric 2-tensors
G_{\mathbf{p}}(t_{0})
are (smooth) metrics on
\mathcal{M}
and estimate their difference from
G_{\mathbf{0}}(t_{0})
.

Lemma 4.5
.

If
\Gamma_{0}\gg 1
is sufficiently large (depending on
n,M,\overline{g},f
),

0<\gamma_{0}\leq 1
,
and
0\leq t_{0}<1
,
then

\supp\varphi_{t_{0}}^{*}\eta_{\gamma_{0}}\subset\{x\in M:f(x)<\Gamma_{0}\}.

Proof.

By Lemma
2.33
,
there exists a positive constant
C=C(n,M,\overline{g},f)>0
such that

\max\left\{0,\varphi_{t}^{*}f-\frac{C}{\varphi_{t}^{*}f}\right\}\leq(1-t)\partial_{t}(\varphi_{t}^{*}f)\qquad\text{for all }t<1.

In terms of the reparametrized time coordinate
\tau=-\ln(1-t)
, this estimate reads

\max\left\{0,\varphi_{t(\tau)}^{*}f-\frac{C}{\varphi_{t(\tau)}^{*}f}\right\}\leq\partial_{\tau}(\varphi_{t(\tau)}^{*}f)\qquad\text{for all }\tau\in\mathbb{R}

where
t(\tau)=1-e^{-\tau}
.
Integrating with respect to
\tau
and using that
\varphi_{0}=Id_{M}
, it follows that

f(x)\leq\varphi_{t(\tau)}^{*}f(x)\qquad\text{ for all }(x,\tau)\in M\times\mathbb{R},\text{ and}

\sqrt{C+(f(x)^{2}-C)e^{2\tau}}\leq\varphi_{\tau}^{*}f(x)\qquad\text{ for all }(x,\tau)\in\left\{x\in M:f(x)\geq\sqrt{C}\right\}\times\mathbb{R}.

Assume
\Gamma_{0}>\sqrt{C+1}
.
Observe

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} \supp\varphi_{t_{0}}^{*}\eta_{\gamma_{0}}&=\varphi_{t_{0}}^{-1}(\supp\eta_{\gamma_{0}})\\
&\subset\varphi_{t_{0}}^{-1}\{x\in M:f\leq\gamma_{0}e^{\tau_{0}}\}\\
&=\{x\in M:f\circ\varphi_{t_{0}}(x)\leq\gamma_{0}e^{\tau_{0}}\}\\
&=\{x\in M:[\varphi_{t_{0}}^{*}f](x)\leq\gamma_{0}e^{\tau_{0}}\}.\end{aligned}

Let
x\in M
be such that
[\varphi_{t_{0}}^{*}f](x)\leq\gamma_{0}e^{\tau_{0}}
.
If
f(x)\leq\sqrt{C}
, then
f(x)<\Gamma_{0}
.
If
f(x)\geq\sqrt{C}
, then

\sqrt{C+(f(x)^{2}-C)e^{2\tau_{0}}}\leq\varphi_{t_{0}}^{*}f(x)\leq\gamma_{0}e^{\tau_{0}}

which implies

f(x)\leq\sqrt{C+\gamma_{0}^{2}-Ce^{-2\tau_{0}}}\leq\sqrt{C+1-Ce^{-2\tau_{0}}}\leq\sqrt{C+1}<\Gamma_{0}.

In either case, it follows that
f(x)<\Gamma_{0}
.
∎

Remark 4.6
.

In practice, the parameters
\Gamma_{0},\gamma_{0},t_{0}
will be taken so that Lemma
4.5
implies

\supp\varphi_{t_{0}}^{*}\eta_{\gamma_{0}}\subset\{f<\Gamma_{0}/2\}.

We make this condition explicit in Assumption
4.9
.

Before continuing, we make a few notational simplifications that will be used throughout the rest of the paper.

We use the notation “
A\lesssim B
” to mean that there exists a constant
C
such that
A\leq CB
.
We shall write “
A\lesssim_{a,b}B
” when the constant
C
depends on
a
and
b
.

We say “
A\sim B
” when
A\lesssim B
and
B\lesssim A
.
Similarly, “
A\sim_{a,b}B
” means
A\lesssim_{a,b}B
and
B\lesssim_{a,b}A
.

We shall write “
\{f<A\}
” for example to mean either

\{x\in M:f(x)<A\}
or
\{x\in\mathcal{M}:f(x)<A\}
.
It will often be clear from context whether we refer to the former or the latter, but we will write
\{f<A\}\subset M
for example for additional specificity.

Proposition 4.7
.

If
\Gamma_{0}\gg 1
is sufficiently large (depending on
n,M,\overline{g},f
),

0<\gamma_{0}\leq 1
,
and
0\leq t_{0}<1
, then

|G_{\mathbf{p}}(t_{0})-G_{\mathbf{0}}(t_{0})|_{G_{\mathbf{0}}(t_{0})}\lesssim_{n,M,\overline{g},f,\lambda_{*}}\overline{p}\gamma_{0}^{|\lambda_{*}|}

for all
|\mathbf{p}|\leq\overline{p}(1-t_{0})^{|\lambda_{*}|}
.

Proof.

By Lemma
4.5
, we may assume
\supp\varphi_{t_{0}}^{*}\eta_{\gamma_{0}}\subset\{f\leq\Gamma_{0}\}
.
It then follows that
G_{\mathbf{p}}(t_{0})-G_{\mathbf{0}}(t_{0})
is supported in
\{f\leq\Gamma_{0}\}
.
Moreover,
G_{\mathbf{0}}(t_{0})=(1-t_{0})\iota^{*}\varphi_{t_{0}}^{*}\overline{g}
in this region by Definition
4.2
(
1
).
Set
\tau_{0}\doteqdot-\ln(1-t_{0})
.
It follows that

\displaystyle|G_{\mathbf{p}}(t_{0})-G_{\mathbf{0}}(t_{0})|_{G_{\mathbf{0}}(t_{0})}

\displaystyle={}

\displaystyle\left|(1-t_{0})\iota^{*}\varphi_{t_{0}}^{*}\left(\eta_{\gamma_{0}}\sum_{j=1}^{K}p_{j}h_{j}\right)\right|_{(1-t_{0})\iota^{*}\varphi_{t}^{*}\overline{g}}

\displaystyle={}

\displaystyle\left|\eta_{\gamma_{0}}\sum_{j=1}^{K}p_{j}h_{j}\right|_{\overline{g}}

\displaystyle\leq{}

\displaystyle\eta_{\gamma_{0}}\sum_{j=1}^{K}\overline{p}e^{\lambda_{*}\tau_{0}}|h_{j}|_{\overline{g}}.

By the eigenmode growth condition (Definition
2.23
and Proposition
2.34
),
this quantity may then be bounded above by

\displaystyle\quad\eta_{\gamma_{0}}\sum_{j=1}^{K}\overline{p}e^{\lambda_{*}\tau_{0}}C(n,M,\overline{g},f,j,\delta)f^{\max\{-\lambda_{j},0\}+\delta}

\displaystyle\lesssim_{n,M,\overline{g},f,\lambda_{*},\delta}\eta_{\gamma_{0}}f^{-\lambda_{*}}\overline{p}e^{\lambda_{*}\tau_{0}}\sum_{j=1}^{K}f^{\max\{-\lambda_{j},0\}+\delta+\lambda_{*}}

\displaystyle\leq\overline{p}\gamma_{0}^{|\lambda_{*}|}\sum_{j=1}^{K}f^{\max\{\lambda_{*}-\lambda_{j}+\delta,\lambda_{*}+\delta\}}.

Since
\lambda_{*}<0
and
\lambda_{*}<\lambda_{K}\leq\dots\leq\lambda_{1}
,
there exists
\delta=\delta(n,M,\overline{g},f,\lambda_{*})>0
such that

\lambda_{*}-\lambda_{j}+\delta<0\text{ and }\lambda_{*}+\delta<0\qquad\text{for all }1\leq j\leq K.

Together with the fact that
f>0
by Lemma
2.30
, it follows that

\overline{p}\gamma_{0}^{|\lambda_{*}|}\sum_{j=1}^{K}f^{\max\{\lambda_{*}-\lambda_{j}+\delta,\lambda_{*}+\delta\}}\lesssim_{n,M,\overline{g},f,\lambda_{*}}\overline{p}\gamma_{0}^{|\lambda_{*}|}.

∎

Corollary 4.8
.

If

\Gamma_{0}\gg 1
is sufficiently large (depending on
n,M,\overline{g},f
),

0<\gamma_{0}\ll 1
is sufficiently small (depending on
n,M,\overline{g},f,\lambda_{*}
),

0<\overline{p}\leq 1
, and

0\leq t_{0}<1
,
then
G_{\mathbf{p}}(t_{0})
is a smooth
\mathcal{S}
-invariant metric on the closed manifold
\mathcal{M}
for all
|\mathbf{p}|\leq\overline{p}(1-t_{0})^{|\lambda_{*}|}
.

Proof.

By Lemma
4.5
, we may assume
\supp\varphi_{t_{0}}^{*}\eta_{\gamma_{0}}\subset\{f\leq\Gamma_{0}\}\subset M^{\prime}
.
In particular,
\iota^{*}\varphi_{t_{0}}^{*}\eta_{\gamma_{0}}^{*}
has support on the interior of
\mathcal{M}^{\prime}
.
Hence,
G_{\mathbf{p}}(t_{0})
is a smooth symmetric 2-tensor since the eigenmodes
h_{j}
are smooth as well.
Proposition
4.7
implies that
G_{\mathbf{p}}(t_{0})
is positive definite for all
|\mathbf{p}|\leq\overline{p}(1-t_{0})^{|\lambda_{*}|}
,
\overline{p}\leq 1
,
0<\gamma_{0}\ll 1
sufficiently small, and
0\leq t_{0}<1
.

Finally, for any
\psi\in\mathcal{S}
,

\psi^{*}G_{\mathbf{p}}(t_{0})=\psi^{*}G_{\mathbf{0}}(t_{0})+(1-t_{0})\psi^{*}\iota^{*}\varphi_{t_{0}}^{*}\left(\eta_{\gamma_{0}}\sum_{j=1}^{K}p_{j}h_{j}\right)\\
=G_{\mathbf{0}}(t_{0})+(1-t_{0})\iota^{*}\varphi_{t_{0}}^{*}\left(\eta_{\gamma_{0}}\sum_{j=1}^{K}p_{j}h_{j}\right)=G_{\mathbf{p}}(t_{0})

by Definition
4.2
(
6
) and Equation (
4.1
).
∎

Assumption 4.9
.

Henceforth, we will implicitly assume that

\Gamma_{0}\gg 1
is sufficiently large (depending on
n,M,\overline{g},f
),

0<\gamma_{0}\ll 1
is sufficiently small (depending on
n,M,\overline{g},f,\lambda_{*})
,

0<\overline{p}\leq 1
, and

0\leq t_{0}<1

so that Corollary
4.8
implies
G_{\mathbf{p}}(t_{0})
is a smooth
\mathcal{S}
-invariant metric on
\mathcal{M}
for all
|\mathbf{p}|\leq\overline{p}(1-t_{0})^{|\lambda_{*}|}
and
Lemma
4.5
implies
\supp\varphi_{t_{0}}^{*}\eta_{\gamma_{0}}\subset\{f<\Gamma_{0}/2\}
.

Remark 4.10
.

By perturbing the initial metrics
G_{\mathbf{0}}(\Gamma_{0},t_{0})
on
\mathcal{M}\setminus\overline{\mathcal{M}^{\prime}}\neq\emptyset

and averaging over
\mathcal{S}
via
G\mapsto\frac{1}{\#\mathcal{S}}\sum_{\psi\in\mathcal{S}}\psi^{*}G
,
we may additionally assume without loss of generality that the metrics
G_{\mathbf{p}}(t_{0})
are non-Kähler.

4.3.
The Flow

We let
G_{\mathbf{p}}(t)=G_{\mathbf{p}}(t;\Gamma_{0},\gamma_{0},t_{0})
denote the maximal solution to the Ricci flow

\partial_{t}G_{\mathbf{p}}(t)=-2Rc[G_{\mathbf{p}}(t)]

on
\mathcal{M}
with initial data
G_{\mathbf{p}}(t_{0})
given by (
4.3
) at time
t=t_{0}
.
Let
T(\mathbf{p})=T(\mathbf{p};\Gamma_{0},\gamma_{0},t_{0})
denote the minimum of
1
and the maximal existence time of
G_{\mathbf{p}}(t;\Gamma_{0},\gamma_{0},t_{0})
.

Let
\eta_{\Gamma_{0}}:M\to[0,1]
be a compactly supported bump function such that:

(1)

\eta_{\Gamma_{0}}(x)=1
for all
x\in M
such that
f(x)\leq\frac{1}{2}\Gamma_{0}
,

(2)

\text{supp}(\eta_{\Gamma_{0}})\subset\{f<\Gamma_{0}\}
,

(3)

\overline{\{x\in M:0<\eta_{\Gamma_{0}}(x)<1\}}\subset\{\frac{4}{6}\Gamma_{0}<f<\frac{5}{6}\Gamma_{0}\}\subset\{\frac{1}{2}\Gamma_{0}<f<\Gamma_{0}\}
,

(4)

for all
m\in\mathbb{N}
,
|{}^{(1-t)\varphi_{t}^{*}\overline{g}}\nabla^{m}\eta_{\Gamma_{0}}|_{(1-t)\varphi_{t}^{*}\overline{g}}\lesssim_{n,M,\overline{g},f,m}1
,
and

(5)

|{}^{(1-t)\varphi_{t}^{*}\overline{g}}\nabla\eta_{\Gamma_{0}}|_{(1-t)\varphi_{t}^{*}\overline{g}}\lesssim_{n,M,\overline{g},f}\Gamma_{0}^{-1/2}
.

Such bump functions can be obtained by taking a suitable bump function
\hat{\eta}:(0,\infty)\to[0,1]
and letting

\eta_{\Gamma_{0}}(x)\doteqdot\hat{\eta}\left(\frac{f(x)}{\Gamma_{0}}\right).

The derivative bounds (4), (5) follow from the fact
(1-t)\varphi_{t}^{*}\overline{g}
converges to (a pullback of) the cone metric
g_{\mathcal{C}}
on
\{\frac{1}{2}\Gamma_{0}<f<\Gamma_{0}\}
as
t\nearrow 1
so long as
\Gamma_{0}\gg 1
is sufficiently large depending on
n,M,\overline{g},f
(see Proposition
A.1
).
The coarser derivative bounds in (4) suffice for most applications.
The finer derivative bounds (5) yield Lemma
5.4
in Section
5
, which in turn simplifies estimates later in the paper.

Recall the diffeomorphisms
\iota_{\omega}:\mathcal{M}^{\prime}_{\omega}\to M^{\prime}=\{f<100\Gamma_{0}\}\subset M
for every
\omega\in A
.
Thus, the push-forwards

(\iota_{\omega})_{*}\left(G_{\mathbf{p}}(t)|_{\mathcal{M}^{\prime}_{\omega}}\right)=(\iota_{\omega}^{-1})^{*}\left(G_{\mathbf{p}}(t)|_{\mathcal{M}^{\prime}_{\omega}}\right)

define Riemannian metrics on
M^{\prime}\subset M
for each
\omega\in A
.
Since
G_{\mathbf{p}}(t_{0})
is
\mathcal{S}
-invariant (Definition
4.2
(
6
)), the metrics
G_{\mathbf{p}}(t)
are also
\mathcal{S}
-invariant on
\mathcal{M}
for all
t\in[t_{0},T(\mathbf{p}))
.
Because
\mathcal{S}
acts transitively on
A
and respects the identifications
\iota_{\omega}
(
4.2
), it follows that in fact

(\iota_{\omega})_{*}\left(G_{\mathbf{p}}(t)|_{\mathcal{M}^{\prime}_{\omega}}\right)=(\iota_{\tilde{\omega}})_{*}\left(G_{\mathbf{p}}(t)|_{\mathcal{M}^{\prime}_{\tilde{\omega}}}\right)\qquad\forall\omega,\tilde{\omega}\in A.

Therefore, we may identify the metric
\iota_{*}G_{\mathbf{p}}(t)=\iota_{*}G_{\mathbf{p}}(t)|_{\mathcal{M}^{\prime}}
on
\bigsqcup_{\omega\in A}M^{\prime}
with a metric on
M^{\prime}
, which will still be denoted as
\iota_{*}G_{\mathbf{p}}(t)
.
We extend these metrics
\iota_{*}G_{\mathbf{p}}(t)
on
M^{\prime}=\{x\in M:f(x)<100\Gamma_{0}\}
to metrics
\acute{G}_{\mathbf{p}}(t)
on all of
M
by setting

(4.4)

\acute{G}_{\mathbf{p}}(t)\doteqdot\eta_{\Gamma_{0}}\iota_{*}G_{\mathbf{p}}(t)+(1-\eta_{\Gamma_{0}})(1-t)\varphi_{t}^{*}\overline{g}\qquad\text{for all }t\in[t_{0},T(\mathbf{p})).

Remark 4.11
.

We make some elementary observations before continuing:

•

At
t=t_{0}
,

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} \acute{G}_{\mathbf{p}}(t_{0})&=\eta_{\Gamma_{0}}\iota_{*}G_{\mathbf{0}}(t_{0})+(1-\eta_{\Gamma_{0}})(1-t_{0})\varphi_{t_{0}}^{*}\overline{g}+\eta_{\Gamma_{0}}(1-t_{0})\varphi_{t_{0}}^{*}\left(\eta_{\gamma_{0}}\sum_{j=1}^{K}p_{j}h_{j}\right)\end{aligned}

Recall
\supp\varphi_{t_{0}}^{*}\eta_{\gamma_{0}}\subset\{f<\Gamma_{0}/2\}
by Assumption
4.9
and
\iota_{*}G_{\mathbf{0}}(t_{0})=(1-t_{0})\varphi_{t_{0}}^{*}\overline{g}
on
\{f\leq\Gamma_{0}\}\supset\supp\eta_{\Gamma_{0}}
by Definition
4.2
.
Therefore,

\acute{G}_{\mathbf{p}}(t_{0})=(1-t_{0})\varphi_{t_{0}}^{*}\overline{g}+(1-t_{0})\varphi_{t_{0}}^{*}\left(\eta_{\gamma_{0}}\sum_{j=1}^{K}p_{j}h_{j}\right)

throughout
M
.

•

On the set
\{x\in M:\eta_{\Gamma_{0}}(x)=1\}
,

\acute{G}_{\mathbf{p}}(t)=\iota_{*}G_{\mathbf{p}}(t)\qquad\text{for all }t\in[t_{0},T(\mathbf{p})).

•

On the set
\{x\in M:\eta_{\Gamma_{0}}(x)=0\}
,

\acute{G}_{\mathbf{p}}(t)=(1-t)\varphi_{t}^{*}\overline{g}\qquad\text{for all }t\in[t_{0},T(\mathbf{p})).

•

\acute{G}_{\mathbf{p}}(t)
does
not
solve Ricci flow.
However, on open subsets of
\eta_{\Gamma_{0}}^{-1}(\{1\})
and
\eta_{\Gamma_{0}}^{-1}(\{0\})
,
\acute{G}_{\mathbf{p}}(t)

does
solve Ricci flow since it’s equal to the Ricci flow solution
\iota_{*}G_{\mathbf{p}}(t)
or
(1-t)\varphi_{t}^{*}\overline{g}
.

In general,

(4.5)

\partial_{t}\acute{G}_{\mathbf{p}}+2Rc[\acute{G}_{\mathbf{p}}(t)]=2Rc[\acute{G}_{\mathbf{p}}(t)]-2\eta_{\Gamma_{0}}Rc[\iota_{*}G_{\mathbf{p}}(t)]-2(1-\eta_{\Gamma_{0}})Rc[\varphi_{t}^{*}\overline{g}],

which is supported on the closure of
\{x\in M:0<\eta_{\Gamma_{0}}(x)<1\}
.

Definition 4.12
.

Recall from Definition
2.4
that, for
t\in(-\infty,1)
,
\varphi(\cdot,t)=\varphi_{t}:M\to M
denote the soliton diffeomorphisms given as the solution to

(
2.2
)

\partial_{t}\varphi_{t}=\frac{1}{1-t}\overline{\nabla}f\circ\varphi_{t}\qquad\text{ with initial condition }\varphi_{0}=Id_{M}.

Define one-parameter families of functions

(4.6)

\Phi_{t},\tilde{\Phi}_{t}:M\to M\qquad\text{ by }\Phi_{t}=\varphi_{t}\circ\tilde{\Phi}_{t}

where
\tilde{\Phi}_{t}:M\to M
solves the time-dependent harmonic map heat flow

(4.7)

\partial_{t}\tilde{\Phi}_{t}=\Delta_{\acute{G}_{\mathbf{p}}(t),(1-t)\varphi_{t}^{*}\overline{g}}\tilde{\Phi}_{t}\qquad\text{ with initial condition }\tilde{\Phi}_{t_{0}}=Id_{M}.

Let
T_{\Phi}(\mathbf{p})\in[t_{0},T(\mathbf{p})]
denote the maximal time for which
\tilde{\Phi}_{t}
, or equivalently
\Phi_{t}
, exists, is unique, and is a diffeomorphism.

Remark 4.13
.

Given that
\tilde{\Phi}_{t}
is defined as a harmonic map heat flow on
non-compact
manifolds, there is some subtlety to establishing its short-time existence and uniqueness.
Appendix
B
addresses such well-posedness issues in detail,
and Subsection
8.2
applies the Appendix
B
results to our particular setting to prove Theorem
1.1
.

Lemma 4.14
.

For all
t\in(t_{0},T_{\Phi}(\mathbf{p}))
,

\Phi_{t}:M\to M
satisfies

(4.8)

\partial_{t}\Phi_{t}=\Delta_{\acute{G}_{\mathbf{p}}(t),\overline{g}}\Phi_{t}+\frac{1}{1-t}\overline{\nabla}f\circ\Phi_{t}\qquad\text{ with }\quad\Phi_{t_{0}}=\varphi_{t_{0}}:M\to M.

Proof.

The proof uses the following general facts about the Laplacian that hold for all real numbers
\lambda>0
, all Riemannian metrics
g,\overline{g}
on
M
, all smooth functions
f:M\to\mathbb{R}
, and all diffeomorphisms
\psi:M\to M
:

(1)

\displaystyle\Delta_{g,\lambda\overline{g}}f=\Delta_{g,\overline{g}}f=\lambda\Delta_{\lambda g,\overline{g}}f,

(2)

\displaystyle(\Delta_{g,\overline{g}}f)\circ\psi=\Delta_{\psi^{*}g,\overline{g}}(f\circ\psi),\text{ and}

(3)

\displaystyle\psi_{*}\Delta_{g,\overline{g}}Id_{M}=\Delta_{(\psi^{-1})^{*}g,(\psi^{-1})^{*}\overline{g}}Id_{M}.

It follows that, with
\acute{G}=\acute{G}_{\mathbf{p}}(t)
,

\displaystyle\partial_{t}\Phi_{t}

\displaystyle={}

\displaystyle\partial_{t}(\varphi_{t}\circ\tilde{\Phi}_{t})

\displaystyle(\text{equation \eqref{Phi Defn}})

\displaystyle={}

\displaystyle\left\{(\varphi_{t})_{*}[\partial_{t}\tilde{\Phi}_{t}\circ\tilde{\Phi}_{t}^{-1}]\right\}\circ\Phi_{t}+(\partial_{t}\varphi_{t})\circ\tilde{\Phi}_{t}

\displaystyle(\text{chain rule})

\displaystyle={}

\displaystyle\left\{(\varphi_{t})_{*}\left[\left(\Delta_{\acute{G},(1-t)\varphi_{t}^{*}\overline{g}}\tilde{\Phi}_{t}\right)\circ\tilde{\Phi}_{t}^{-1}\right]\right\}\circ\Phi_{t}

\displaystyle(\text{equation \eqref{tlPhi Evol Eqn}})

\displaystyle\quad+\frac{1}{1-t}\overline{\nabla}f\circ\varphi_{t}\circ\tilde{\Phi}_{t}

\displaystyle(\text{equation \eqref{phi Evol Eqn}})

\displaystyle={}

\displaystyle\left\{(\varphi_{t})_{*}\left[\left(\Delta_{\acute{G},\varphi_{t}^{*}\overline{g}}\tilde{\Phi}_{t}\right)\circ\tilde{\Phi}_{t}^{-1}\right]\right\}\circ\Phi_{t}

\displaystyle(\text{item \eqref{Lapl Scale Codomain}})

\displaystyle\quad+\frac{1}{1-t}\overline{\nabla}f\circ\Phi_{t}

\displaystyle(\text{equation \eqref{Phi Defn}})

\displaystyle={}

\displaystyle\left\{(\varphi_{t})_{*}\left[\Delta_{(\tilde{\Phi}_{t}^{-1})^{*}\acute{G},\varphi_{t}^{*}\overline{g}}Id_{M}\right]\right\}\circ\Phi_{t}+\frac{1}{1-t}\overline{\nabla}f\circ\Phi_{t}

\displaystyle(\text{item \eqref{Lapl Precompose}})

\displaystyle={}

\displaystyle\left\{\Delta_{(\varphi_{t}^{-1})^{*}(\tilde{\Phi}_{t}^{-1})^{*}\acute{G},\overline{g}}Id_{M}\right\}\circ\Phi_{t}+\frac{1}{1-t}\overline{\nabla}f\circ\Phi_{t}

\displaystyle(\text{item \eqref{Pushforward Lapl Id}})

\displaystyle={}

\displaystyle\left\{\Delta_{(\Phi_{t}^{-1})^{*}\acute{G},\overline{g}}Id_{M}\right\}\circ\Phi_{t}+\frac{1}{1-t}\overline{\nabla}f\circ\Phi_{t}

\displaystyle(\text{equation \eqref{Phi Defn}})

\displaystyle={}

\displaystyle\Delta_{\acute{G},\overline{g}}\Phi_{t}+\frac{1}{1-t}\overline{\nabla}f\circ\Phi_{t}

\displaystyle(\text{item \eqref{Lapl Precompose}}).

∎

For all
t\in[t_{0},T_{\Phi}(\mathbf{p}))
, define rescaled and reparametrized metrics
g_{\mathbf{p}}(t)=g_{\mathbf{p}}(t;\Gamma_{0},\gamma_{0},t_{0})
on
M
by

(4.9)

g_{\mathbf{p}}(t)=\frac{1}{1-t}\left(\Phi_{t}^{-1}\right)^{*}\acute{G}_{\mathbf{p}}(t).

Lemma 4.15
.

For all
t\in(t_{0},T_{\Phi}(\mathbf{p},t_{0}))
,
g=g_{\mathbf{p}}(t;t_{0})
satisfies

(4.10)

(1-t)\partial_{t}g=-2Rc[g]+\mathcal{L}_{B_{\overline{g}}(g)}g-\mathcal{L}_{\overline{\nabla}f}g+g+\left(\Phi_{t}^{-1}\right)^{*}\left\{\partial_{t}\acute{G}_{\mathbf{p}}+2Rc[\acute{G}_{\mathbf{p}}]\right\},

where
B_{\overline{g}}(g)
is the vector field on
M
from DeTurck’s trick defined by

(4.11)

B_{\overline{g}}(g)=-\Delta_{g,\overline{g}}Id_{M}

or, in local coordinates,

B_{\overline{g}}(g)^{k}=g^{ij}\left(\Gamma(g)_{ij}^{k}-\Gamma(\overline{g})_{ij}^{k}\right).

Remark 4.16
.

The vector field
B_{\overline{g}}(g)
is also given by the equation

\overline{g}\left\langle B_{\overline{g}}(g),\cdot\right\rangle=-\div_{g}\overline{g}+\frac{1}{2}d(\tr_{g}\overline{g}).

Proof.

Differentiating equation (
4.9
) with respect to
t
, it follows that

\displaystyle\partial_{t}g

\displaystyle={}

\displaystyle\frac{1}{(1-t)^{2}}(\Phi_{t}^{-1})^{*}\acute{G}_{\mathbf{p}}+\frac{1}{1-t}(\Phi_{t}^{-1})^{*}\mathcal{L}_{\partial_{t}\Phi_{t}^{-1}\circ\Phi_{t}}\acute{G}_{\mathbf{p}}+\frac{1}{1-t}(\Phi_{t}^{-1})^{*}\partial_{t}\acute{G}_{\mathbf{p}}

\displaystyle-\frac{2}{1-t}(\Phi_{t}^{-1})^{*}Rc[\acute{G}_{\mathbf{p}}]+\frac{2}{1-t}(\Phi_{t}^{-1})^{*}Rc[\acute{G}_{\mathbf{p}}]

\displaystyle={}

\displaystyle\frac{1}{1-t}g+\frac{1}{1-t}\mathcal{L}_{(\Phi_{t})_{*}[\partial_{t}\Phi_{t}^{-1}\circ\Phi_{t}]}(\Phi_{t}^{-1})^{*}\acute{G}_{\mathbf{p}}-\frac{2}{1-t}Rc[(\Phi_{t}^{-1})^{*}\acute{G}_{\mathbf{p}}]

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{1-t}(\Phi_{t}^{-1})^{*}\left\{\partial_{t}\acute{G}_{\mathbf{p}}+2Rc[\acute{G}_{\mathbf{p}}]\right\}

\displaystyle={}

\displaystyle\frac{1}{1-t}g+\mathcal{L}_{(\Phi_{t})_{*}[\partial_{t}\Phi_{t}^{-1}\circ\Phi_{t}]}g-\frac{2}{1-t}Rc[g]

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{1-t}(\Phi_{t}^{-1})^{*}\left\{\partial_{t}\acute{G}_{\mathbf{p}}+2Rc[\acute{G}_{\mathbf{p}}]\right\}.

Next, note that differentiating both sides of
\Phi_{t}\circ\Phi_{t}^{-1}=Id_{M}
with respect to
t
implies

(\Phi_{t})_{*}[\partial_{t}\Phi_{t}^{-1}\circ\Phi_{t}]=-(\partial_{t}\Phi_{t})\circ\Phi_{t}^{-1}.

Hence,

\displaystyle(\Phi_{t})_{*}[\partial_{t}\Phi_{t}^{-1}\circ\Phi_{t}]

\displaystyle={}

\displaystyle-(\partial_{t}\Phi_{t})\circ\Phi_{t}^{-1}

\displaystyle={}

\displaystyle-(\Delta_{\acute{G}_{\mathbf{p}},\overline{g}}\Phi_{t})\circ\Phi_{t}^{-1}-\frac{1}{1-t}\overline{\nabla}f

\displaystyle(\text{equation \eqref{Phi Evol Eqn}})

\displaystyle={}

\displaystyle-\Delta_{(\Phi_{t}^{-1})^{*}\acute{G}_{\mathbf{p}},\overline{g}}Id_{M}-\frac{1}{1-t}\overline{\nabla}f

\displaystyle(\text{item \eqref{Lapl Precompose} above})

\displaystyle={}

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{1-t}\Delta_{\frac{1}{1-t}(\Phi_{t}^{-1})^{*}\acute{G}_{\mathbf{p}},\overline{g}}Id_{M}-\frac{1}{1-t}\overline{\nabla}f

\displaystyle(\text{item \eqref{Lapl Scale Codomain} above})

\displaystyle={}

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{1-t}\Delta_{g,\overline{g}}Id_{M}-\frac{1}{1-t}\overline{\nabla}f

\displaystyle(\text{equation \eqref{g Defn}})

\displaystyle={}

\displaystyle\frac{1}{1-t}B_{\overline{g}}(g)-\frac{1}{1-t}\overline{\nabla}f.

Therefore,

\displaystyle(1-t)\partial_{t}g={}

\displaystyle g+(1-t)\mathcal{L}_{(\Phi_{t})_{*}[\partial_{t}\Phi_{t}^{-1}\circ\Phi_{t}]}g-2Rc[g]+(\Phi_{t}^{-1})^{*}\left\{\partial_{t}\acute{G}_{\mathbf{p}}+2Rc[\acute{G}_{\mathbf{p}}]\right\}

\displaystyle={}

\displaystyle-2Rc[g]+\mathcal{L}_{B_{\overline{g}}(g)}g-\mathcal{L}_{\overline{\nabla}f}g+g+(\Phi_{t}^{-1})^{*}\left\{\partial_{t}\acute{G}_{\mathbf{p}}+2Rc[\acute{G}_{\mathbf{p}}]\right\}.

∎

Corollary 4.17
.

For all
t\in(t_{0},T_{\Phi}(\mathbf{p}))
,
the tensor

h=h_{\mathbf{p}}(t;\Gamma_{0},\gamma_{0},t_{0})\doteqdot g_{\mathbf{p}}(t;\Gamma_{0},\gamma_{0},t_{0})-\overline{g}

satisfies an evolution equation given in local coordinates by

(4.12)

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} (1-t)\partial_{t}h_{ij}={}&g^{ab}\overline{\nabla}_{a}\overline{\nabla}_{b}h_{ij}-\overline{\nabla}_{\overline{\nabla}f}h+2\overline{R}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.68811pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.2058pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{k}\mathchoice{\makebox[3.71356pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.71356pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.29834pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.64166pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{l}}}_{{{i}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.42017pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.42017pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.7052pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.93228pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{j}\mathchoice{\makebox[2.56946pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[2.56946pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.55847pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.11319pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}h_{kl}\\
&+\overline{R}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[8.05121pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[8.05121pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[4.88843pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.49171pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{p}\mathchoice{\makebox[3.51666pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.51666pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.1029pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.50208pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{ja}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.12234pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.12234pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.46532pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.76094pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{b}}}\left(\overline{g}_{ip}\tilde{h}^{ab}+\hat{h}^{ab}h_{ip}\right)+\overline{R}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[7.16693pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[7.16693pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[4.2782pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.05585pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{p}\mathchoice{\makebox[3.51666pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.51666pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.1029pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.50208pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{ia}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.12234pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.12234pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.46532pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.76094pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{b}}}\left(\overline{g}_{jp}\tilde{h}^{ab}+\hat{h}^{ab}h_{jp}\right)\\
&+g^{ab}g^{pq}(\overline{\nabla}h*\overline{\nabla}h)_{abpqij}+\left(\Phi_{t}^{-1}\right)^{*}\left\{\partial_{t}\acute{G}_{\mathbf{p}}+2Rc[\acute{G}_{\mathbf{p}}]\right\},\end{aligned}

where the tensors
\hat{h},\tilde{h}
are defined by

(4.13)

(g_{\mathbf{p}})^{ab}=\overline{g}^{ab}-\hat{h}^{ab}\quad\text{and}\quad\hat{h}^{ab}=\overline{g}^{ak}\overline{g}^{bl}h_{kl}+\tilde{h}^{ab},

(\overline{\nabla}h*\overline{\nabla}h)_{abpqij}
here is given by

\displaystyle 2(\overline{\nabla}h*\overline{\nabla}h)_{abpqij}={}

\displaystyle(\overline{\nabla}_{i}h_{pa})(\overline{\nabla}_{j}h_{q}b)+2(\overline{\nabla}_{a}h_{jp})(\overline{\nabla}_{q}h_{ib})-2(\overline{\nabla}_{a}h_{jp})(\overline{\nabla}_{b}h_{iq})

\displaystyle-2(\overline{\nabla}_{j}h_{pa})(\overline{\nabla}_{b}h_{iq})-2(\overline{\nabla}_{i}h_{pa})(\overline{\nabla}_{b}h_{jq}),

and the raising and lowering of indices on the curvature terms in equation (
4.12
) is done with respect to
\overline{g}
.

Proof.

Throughout this proof, any raising or lowering of indices is done with respect to
\overline{g}
.
However, we shall only raise or lower indices on terms involving the curvature
\overline{Rm}
.
Throughout the proof, we shall also write
g
instead of
g_{\mathbf{p}}(t;\Gamma_{0},\gamma_{0},t_{0})
to simplify the notation.

By
[
Shi89
, Lemma 2.1]
,

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} &\quad-2Rc[g]+\mathcal{L}_{B_{\overline{g}}(g)}g\\
&=g^{ab}\overline{\nabla}_{a}\overline{\nabla}_{b}g_{ij}-g^{ab}g_{ip}\overline{R}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[8.05121pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[8.05121pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[4.88843pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.49171pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{p}\mathchoice{\makebox[3.51666pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.51666pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.1029pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.50208pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{ja}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.12234pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.12234pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.46532pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.76094pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{b}}}-g^{ab}g_{jp}\overline{R}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[7.16693pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[7.16693pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[4.2782pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.05585pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{p}\mathchoice{\makebox[3.51666pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.51666pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.1029pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.50208pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{ia}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.12234pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.12234pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.46532pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.76094pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{b}}}+g^{ab}g^{pq}(\overline{\nabla}g*\overline{\nabla}g)\\
&=g^{ab}\overline{\nabla}_{a}\overline{\nabla}_{b}h_{ij}-g^{ab}g_{ip}\overline{R}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[8.05121pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[8.05121pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[4.88843pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.49171pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{p}\mathchoice{\makebox[3.51666pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.51666pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.1029pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.50208pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{ja}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.12234pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.12234pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.46532pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.76094pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{b}}}-g^{ab}g_{jp}\overline{R}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[7.16693pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[7.16693pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[4.2782pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.05585pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{p}\mathchoice{\makebox[3.51666pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.51666pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.1029pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.50208pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{ia}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.12234pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.12234pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.46532pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.76094pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{b}}}+g^{ab}g^{pq}(\overline{\nabla}h*\overline{\nabla}h)&&(\text{since }\overline{\nabla}\overline{g}=0).\end{aligned}

Next, consider the tensors
\hat{h},\tilde{h}
given as in equation (
4.13
) by

g^{ab}=\overline{g}^{ab}-\hat{h}^{ab}\quad\text{and}\quad\hat{h}^{ab}=\overline{g}^{ak}\overline{g}^{bl}h_{kl}+\tilde{h}^{ab}.

We expand the Riemann tensor terms using this notation to obtain

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} &\quad g^{ab}g_{ip}\overline{R}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[8.05121pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[8.05121pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[4.88843pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.49171pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{p}\mathchoice{\makebox[3.51666pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.51666pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.1029pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.50208pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{ja}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.12234pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.12234pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.46532pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.76094pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{b}}}\\
&=(\overline{g}^{ab}-\hat{h}^{ab})(\overline{g}_{ip}+h_{ip})\overline{R}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[8.05121pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[8.05121pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[4.88843pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.49171pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{p}\mathchoice{\makebox[3.51666pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.51666pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.1029pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.50208pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{ja}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.12234pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.12234pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.46532pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.76094pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{b}}}\\
&=\overline{g}^{ab}\overline{g}_{ip}\overline{R}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[8.05121pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[8.05121pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[4.88843pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.49171pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{p}\mathchoice{\makebox[3.51666pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.51666pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.1029pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.50208pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{ja}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.12234pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.12234pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.46532pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.76094pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{b}}}-\hat{h}^{ab}\overline{g}_{ip}\overline{R}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[8.05121pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[8.05121pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[4.88843pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.49171pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{p}\mathchoice{\makebox[3.51666pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.51666pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.1029pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.50208pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{ja}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.12234pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.12234pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.46532pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.76094pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{b}}}+\overline{g}^{ab}h_{ip}\overline{R}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[8.05121pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[8.05121pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[4.88843pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.49171pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{p}\mathchoice{\makebox[3.51666pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.51666pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.1029pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.50208pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{ja}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.12234pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.12234pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.46532pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.76094pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{b}}}-\hat{h}^{ab}h_{ip}\overline{R}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[8.05121pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[8.05121pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[4.88843pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.49171pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{p}\mathchoice{\makebox &=\overline{Rc}_{ji}-\overline{g}_{ip}\overline{g}^{ak}\overline{g}^{bl}h_{kl}\overline{R}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[8.05121pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[8.05121pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[4.88843pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.49171pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{p}\mathchoice{\makebox[3.51666pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.51666pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.1029pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.50208pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{ja}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.12234pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.12234pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.46532pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.76094pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{b}}}-\overline{g}_{ip}\tilde{h}^{ab}\overline{R}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[8.05121pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[8.05121pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[4.88843pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.49171pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{p}\mathchoice{\makebox[3.51666pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.51666pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.1029pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.50208pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{ja}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.12234pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.12234pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.46532pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.76094pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{b}}}+h_{ip}\overline{Rc}_{j}^{p}-\hat{h}^{ab}h_{ip}\overline{R}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[8.05121pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[8.05121pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[4.88843pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.49171pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{p}\mathchoice{\makebox[3.51666pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.51666pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.1029pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.50208pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{ja}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.12234pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.12234pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.46532pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.76094pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{b}}}\\
&=\overline{Rc}_{ji}-\overline{R}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.71356pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.71356pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.29834pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.64166pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{k}\mathchoice{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.68811pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.2058pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{l}}}_{{{j}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.42017pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.42017pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.7052pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.93228pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{i}\mathchoice{\makebox[2.56946pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[2.56946pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.55847pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.11319pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}h_{kl}+h_{ip}\overline{Rc}_{j}^{p}-\overline{R}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[8.05121pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[8.05121pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[4.88843pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.49171pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{p}\mathchoice{\makebox[3.51666pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.51666pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.1029pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.50208pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{ja}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.12234pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.12234pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.46532pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.76094pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{b}}}\left(\overline{g}_{ip}\tilde{h}^{ab}+\hat{h}^{ab}h_{ip}\right).\end{aligned}

It follows that

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} &\quad(1-t)\partial_{t}h\\
&=(1-t)\partial_{t}g\\
&=-2Rc[g]+\mathcal{L}_{B_{\overline{g}}(g)}g-\mathcal{L}_{X}g+g+\left(\Phi_{t}^{-1}\right)^{*}\left\{\partial_{t}\acute{G}_{\mathbf{p}}+2Rc[\acute{G}_{\mathbf{p}}]\right\}&&(X\doteqdot\overline{\nabla}f)\\
&=g^{ab}\overline{\nabla}_{a}\overline{\nabla}_{b}h_{ij}-2\overline{Rc}_{ij}+2\overline{R}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.68811pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.2058pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{k}\mathchoice{\makebox[3.71356pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.71356pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.29834pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.64166pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{l}}}_{{{i}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.42017pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.42017pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.7052pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.93228pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{j}\mathchoice{\makebox[2.56946pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[2.56946pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.55847pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.11319pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}h_{kl}-\overline{Rc}_{j}^{p}h_{ip}-\overline{Rc}_{i}^{p}h_{jp}\\
&\quad+\overline{R}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[8.05121pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[8.05121pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[4.88843pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.49171pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{p}\mathchoice{\makebox[3.51666pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.51666pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.1029pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.50208pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{ja}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.12234pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.12234pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.46532pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.76094pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{b}}}\left(\overline{g}_{ip}\tilde{h}^{ab}+\hat{h}^{ab}h_{ip}\right)+\overline{R}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[7.16693pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[7.16693pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[4.2782pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.05585pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{p}\mathchoice{\makebox[3.51666pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.51666pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.1029pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.50208pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{ia}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.12234pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.12234pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.46532pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.76094pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{b}}}\left(\overline{g}_{jp}\tilde{h}^{ab}+\hat{h}^{ab}h_{jp}\right)\\
&\quad+g^{ab}g^{pq}(\overline{\nabla}h*\overline{\nabla}h)-\mathcal{L}_{X}g+g+\left(\Phi_{t}^{-1}\right)^{*}\left\{\partial_{t}\acute{G}_{\mathbf{p}}+2Rc[\acute{G}_{\mathbf{p}}]\right\}\\
&=g^{ab}\overline{\nabla}_{a}\overline{\nabla}_{b}h_{ij}+2\overline{R}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.68811pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.2058pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{k}\mathchoice{\makebox[3.71356pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.71356pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.29834pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.64166pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{l}}}_{{{i}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.42017pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.42017pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.7052pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.93228pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{j}\mathchoice{\makebox[2.56946pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[2.56946pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.55847pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.11319pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}h_{kl}-\overline{Rc}_{j}^{p}h_{ip}-\overline{Rc}_{i}^{p}h_{jp}\\
&\quad+\overline{R}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[8.05121pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[8.05121pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[4.88843pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.49171pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{p}\mathchoice{\makebox[3.51666pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.51666pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.1029pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.50208pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{ja}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.12234pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.12234pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.46532pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.76094pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{b}}}\left(\overline{g}_{ip}\tilde{h}^{ab}+\hat{h}^{ab}h_{ip}\right)+\overline{R}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[7.16693pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[7.16693pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[4.2782pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.05585pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{p}\mathchoice{\makebox[3.51666pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.51666pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.1029pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.50208pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{ia}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.12234pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.12234pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.46532pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.76094pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{b}}}\left(\overline{g}_{jp}\tilde{h}^{ab}+\hat{h}^{ab}h_{jp}\right)\\
&\quad+g^{ab}g^{pq}(\overline{\nabla}h*\overline{\nabla}h)-\mathcal{L}_{X}g+\mathcal{L}_{X}\overline{g}+g-\overline{g}&&\left(\overline{Rc}+\frac{1}{2}\mathcal{L}_{X}\overline{g}=\frac{1}{2}\overline{g}\right)\\
&\quad+\left(\Phi_{t}^{-1}\right)^{*}\left\{\partial_{t}\acute{G}_{\mathbf{p}}+2Rc[\acute{G}_{\mathbf{p}}]\right\}\\
&=g^{ab}\overline{\nabla}_{a}\overline{\nabla}_{b}h_{ij}+2\overline{R}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.68811pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.2058pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{k}\mathchoice{\makebox[3.71356pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.71356pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.29834pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.64166pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{l}}}_{{{i}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.42017pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.42017pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.7052pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.93228pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{j}\mathchoice{\makebox[2.56946pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[2.56946pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.55847pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.11319pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}h_{kl}\\
&\quad+\overline{R}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[8.05121pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[8.05121pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[4.88843pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.49171pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{p}\mathchoice{\makebox[3.51666pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.51666pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.1029pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.50208pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{ja}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.12234pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.12234pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.46532pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.76094pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{b}}}\left(\overline{g}_{ip}\tilde{h}^{ab}+\hat{h}^{ab}h_{ip}\right)+\overline{R}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[7.16693pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[7.16693pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[4.2782pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.05585pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{p}\mathchoice{\makebox[3.51666pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.51666pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.1029pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.50208pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{ia}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.12234pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.12234pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.46532pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.76094pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{b}}}\left(\overline{g}_{jp}\tilde{h}^{ab}+\hat{h}^{ab}h_{jp}\right)\\
&\quad+g^{ab}g^{pq}(\overline{\nabla}h*\overline{\nabla}h)-\overline{Rc}_{j}^{p}h_{ip}-\overline{Rc}_{i}^{p}h_{jp}-\mathcal{L}_{X}h+h\\
&\quad+\left(\Phi_{t}^{-1}\right)^{*}\left\{\partial_{t}\acute{G}_{\mathbf{p}}+2Rc[\acute{G}_{\mathbf{p}}]\right\}.\end{aligned}

Now, expand the Lie derivative term and use the soliton equations (
2.1
) to compute that

\displaystyle\mathcal{L}_{X}h_{ij}={}

\displaystyle\overline{\nabla}_{X}h_{ij}+h_{ip}\overline{\nabla}_{j}X^{p}+h_{jp}\overline{\nabla}_{i}X^{p}

\displaystyle={}

\displaystyle\overline{\nabla}_{X}h_{ij}+h_{ip}\overline{\nabla}_{j}\overline{\nabla}^{p}f+h_{jp}\overline{\nabla}_{i}\overline{\nabla}^{p}f

\displaystyle={}

\displaystyle\overline{\nabla}_{X}h_{ij}-\overline{Rc}_{j}^{p}h_{ip}-\overline{Rc}_{i}^{p}h_{jp}+\frac{1}{2}\overline{g}_{j}^{p}h_{ip}+\frac{1}{2}\overline{g}_{i}^{p}h_{jp}

\displaystyle={}

\displaystyle\overline{\nabla}_{X}h_{ij}-\overline{Rc}_{j}^{p}h_{ip}-\overline{Rc}_{i}^{p}h_{jp}+h_{ij}.

It follows that

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} (1-t)\partial_{t}h&=g^{ab}\overline{\nabla}_{a}\overline{\nabla}_{b}h_{ij}-\overline{\nabla}_{X}h+2\overline{R}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.68811pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.2058pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{k}\mathchoice{\makebox[3.71356pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.71356pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.29834pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.64166pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{l}}}_{{{i}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.42017pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.42017pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.7052pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.93228pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{j}\mathchoice{\makebox[2.56946pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[2.56946pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.55847pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.11319pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}h_{kl}\\
&\quad+\overline{R}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[8.05121pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[8.05121pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[4.88843pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.49171pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{p}\mathchoice{\makebox[3.51666pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.51666pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.1029pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.50208pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{ja}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.12234pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.12234pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.46532pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.76094pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{b}}}\left(\overline{g}_{ip}\tilde{h}^{ab}+\hat{h}^{ab}h_{ip}\right)+\overline{R}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[7.16693pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[7.16693pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[4.2782pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.05585pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{p}\mathchoice{\makebox[3.51666pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.51666pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.1029pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.50208pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{ia}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.12234pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.12234pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.46532pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.76094pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{b}}}\left(\overline{g}_{jp}\tilde{h}^{ab}+\hat{h}^{ab}h_{jp}\right)\\
&\quad+g^{ab}g^{pq}(\overline{\nabla}h*\overline{\nabla}h)+\left(\Phi_{t}^{-1}\right)^{*}\left\{\partial_{t}\acute{G}_{\mathbf{p}}+2Rc[\acute{G}_{\mathbf{p}}]\right\}.\end{aligned}

∎

Remark 4.18
.

The term

\left(\Phi_{t}^{-1}\right)^{*}\left\{\partial_{t}\acute{G}_{\mathbf{p}}+2Rc[\acute{G}_{\mathbf{p}}]\right\}

captures how far
\acute{G}=\acute{G}_{\mathbf{p}}(t)
is from being a Ricci flow.
By (
4.5
), it may also be written as

(\Phi_{t}^{-1})^{*}\left\{\partial_{t}\acute{G}+2Rc[\acute{G}]\right\}=-2(\Phi_{t}^{-1})^{*}\left\{\eta_{\Gamma_{0}}Rc[\iota_{*}G]+(1-\eta_{\Gamma_{0}})Rc[\varphi_{t}^{*}\overline{g}]-Rc[\acute{G}]\right\}

where
G=G_{\mathbf{p}}(t)
.

As noted in Remark
4.11

\left\{\eta_{\Gamma_{0}}Rc[\iota_{*}G]+(1-\eta_{\Gamma_{0}})Rc[\varphi_{t}^{*}\overline{g}]-Rc[\acute{G}]\right\}

is supported on the closure of
\Omega=\{x\in M:0<\eta_{\Gamma_{0}}(x)<1\}
.
Hence,

\supp\left((\Phi_{t}^{-1})^{*}\left\{\partial_{t}\acute{G}+2Rc[\acute{G}]\right\}\right)\subset\Phi_{t}\left(\overline{\Omega}\right)=\varphi_{t}\left(\tilde{\Phi}_{t}\left(\overline{\Omega}\right)\right).

Later, in Lemma
5.5
, we will estimate the location of this region in
M
in order to obtain estimates on
(\Phi_{t}^{-1})^{*}\left\{\partial_{t}\acute{G}+2Rc[\acute{G}]\right\}
.

Remark 4.19
.

Given the derivatives
(1-t)\partial_{t}
that appear in the above evolution equations,
it will be convenient to work with respect to the reparameterized time coordinate

\tau\doteqdot-\ln(1-t).

We also denote
\tau_{0}\doteqdot-\ln(1-t_{0})
or equivalently
(1-t_{0})=e^{-\tau_{0}}
.
Note that
(1-t_{0})\ll 1
being sufficiently small is equivalent to
\tau_{0}\gg 1
being sufficiently large.

Observe that
(1-t)\frac{\partial}{\partial t}=\frac{\partial}{\partial\tau}
.
Henceforth, we may simplify notation by writing
h(\tau)
or
\varphi_{\tau}
for example to denote
h(t(\tau))
or
\varphi_{t(\tau)}
, respectively, where
t(\tau)=1-e^{-\tau}
.

4.4.
Definition of the Box

Recall from assumption
3.1
that
we have fixed a
L^{2}_{f}
-orthonormal basis
\{h_{j}\}_{j\in\mathbb{N}}
of eigenmodes
with corresponding eigenvalues
\{\lambda_{j}\}_{j\in\mathbb{N}}

for the weighted Lichnerowicz Laplacian
\overline{\Delta}_{f}+2\overline{Rm}=\overline{\Delta}-\overline{\nabla}_{\overline{\nabla}f}+2\overline{Rm}
.
Moreover,
\lambda_{*}<0
was chosen so that
\lambda_{*}\notin\{\lambda_{j}\}_{j\in\mathbb{N}}

and
K=K(n,M,\overline{g},f,\lambda_{*})
denotes the index such that
\lambda_{K+1}<\lambda_{*}<\lambda_{K}
.

Definition 4.20
.

For general
h\in L^{2}_{f}(M)
, define projections

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} h_{u}\doteqdot&\sum_{j=1}^{K}(h,h_{k})_{L^{2}_{f}}h_{k},\text{ and}\\
h_{s}\doteqdot&\sum_{j=K+1}^{\infty}(h,h_{k})_{L^{2}_{f}}h_{k}=h-h_{u}.\\
\end{aligned}

Definition 4.21
.

For
\lambda_{*}<0
as in assumption
3.1
,
constants
\mu_{u},\mu_{s},\epsilon_{0},\epsilon_{1},\epsilon_{2}\in(0,1)
, and an interval
I\subset\mathbb{R}
,
define

\mathcal{B}=\mathcal{B}[\lambda_{*},\mu_{u},\mu_{s},\epsilon_{0},\epsilon_{1},\epsilon_{2},I]

to be the set of smooth sections
h
of
\pi^{*}Sym^{2}T^{*}M\to M\times I
where
\pi:M\times I\to M
is the projection map
such that:

(1)

h(\cdot,\tau)
is compactly supported in
M
for all
\tau\in I
,

(2)

(Fine
L^{2}_{f}
Estimates)

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} \left\|h_{u}(\cdot,\tau)\right\|_{L^{2}_{f}}&\leq\mu_{u}e^{\lambda_{*}\tau}&&\text{for all }\tau\in I,\\
\left\|h_{s}(\cdot,\tau)\right\|_{L^{2}_{f}}&\leq\mu_{s}e^{\lambda_{*}\tau}&&\text{for all }\tau\in I,\\
\end{aligned}

and

(3)

(Coarse
C^{2}
Estimates)

\displaystyle|h|_{\overline{g}}

\displaystyle\leq\epsilon_{0}

\displaystyle\text{for all }\tau\in I,

\displaystyle|\overline{\nabla}h|_{\overline{g}}

\displaystyle\leq\epsilon_{1}

\displaystyle\text{for all }\tau\in I,\text{ and}

\displaystyle|\overline{\nabla}^{2}h|_{\overline{g}}

\displaystyle\leq\epsilon_{2}

\displaystyle\text{for all }\tau\in I.

With a slight abuse of notation, we will also write

``\mathcal{B}[\lambda_{*},\mu_{u},\mu_{s},\epsilon_{0},\epsilon_{1},\epsilon_{2},[\tau(t_{0}),\tau(t_{1}))]"

\text{as }``\mathcal{B}[\lambda_{*},\mu_{u},\mu_{s},\epsilon_{0},\epsilon_{1},\epsilon_{2},[t_{0},t_{1})]"

for example.

Definition 4.22
.

For
\lambda_{*}
as in assumption
3.1
;

\overline{p}>0
;

\Gamma_{0}\gg 1
and
0<\gamma_{0}\ll 1
as in assumption
4.9
;

\mu_{u},\mu_{s},\epsilon_{0},\epsilon_{1},\epsilon_{2}\in(0,1)
;
and
0\leq t_{0}<t_{1}\leq 1
;
let

\mathcal{P}=\mathcal{P}[\lambda_{*},\overline{p},\Gamma_{0},\gamma_{0},\mu_{u},\mu_{s},\epsilon_{0},\epsilon_{1},\epsilon_{2},t_{0},t_{1}]\subset\mathbb{R}^{K}

denote the set of
\mathbf{p}=(p_{1},\dots,p_{K})\in\mathbb{R}^{K}

such that:

(1)

|\mathbf{p}|\leq\overline{p}e^{\lambda_{*}\tau_{0}}
,

(2)

T(\mathbf{p})\in[t_{1},1]
, and

(3)

there exists a smooth function
\tilde{\Phi}:M\times[t_{0},t_{1})\to M

solving (
4.7
)

\partial_{t}\tilde{\Phi}=\Delta_{\acute{G}_{\mathbf{p}}(t),(1-t)\varphi_{t}^{*}\overline{g}}\tilde{\Phi}\text{ on }M\times(t_{0},t_{1})\qquad\text{with initial condition }\tilde{\Phi}(\cdot,t_{0})=Id_{M}

where

\tilde{\Phi}_{t}=\tilde{\Phi}(\cdot,t):M\to M
is a diffeomorphism for all
t\in[t_{0},t_{1})
and

h_{\mathbf{p}}(t)\doteqdot\frac{1}{1-t}(\Phi_{t}^{-1})^{*}\acute{G}_{\mathbf{p}}(t)-\overline{g}\in\mathcal{B}[\lambda_{*},\mu_{u},\mu_{s},\epsilon_{0},\epsilon_{1},\epsilon_{2},[t_{0},t_{1})].

5.
Preliminary Estimates

The goal of this section is to estimate and control the error term

(\Phi_{t}^{-1})^{*}\left\{\partial_{t}\acute{G}_{\mathbf{p}}+2Rc[\acute{G}_{\mathbf{p}}]\right\}

that appears in (
4.12
).
First, we obtain bounds for
\partial_{t}\acute{G}+2\acute{Rc}
.
Such estimates are obtained from Perelman’s pseudolocality theorem applied to our setting.
Second, we estimate the location of the support of
(\Phi_{t}^{-1})^{*}\left\{\partial_{t}\acute{G}_{\mathbf{p}}+2Rc[\acute{G}_{\mathbf{p}}]\right\}
.
As noted in Remark
4.18
, such estimates are obtained through drift estimates on
\tilde{\Phi}_{t},\varphi_{t}
which are recorded in Lemma
5.5
.

We begin by recording the following version of Perelman’s pseudolocality theorem (Theorem 10.1 in
[
Per02
]
)
2
2

2

See also
[
CCG+10
, Ch. 21]
and
[
Lu10
]
.

and its consequences for our setting.

Theorem 5.1

(Perelman’s Pseudolocality Theorem)
.

For any dimension
n
, there exist
\epsilon_{n},\delta_{n}>0
with the following property:
If
(M^{n},g(t))
is a smooth Ricci flow on a closed manifold
M
defined for
t\in[t_{0},T)
that, at initial time
t_{0}
, satisfies

\displaystyle|Rm|_{g(t_{0})}(x)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{r_{0}^{2}}\quad\text{ for all }x\in B_{g(t_{0})}(x_{0},r_{0})

\displaystyle\text{and }\text{Vol}_{g(t_{0})}B_{g(t_{0})}(x_{0},r_{0})

\displaystyle\geq(1-\delta_{n})\omega_{n}r_{0}^{n}

for some
x_{0}\in M
and
r_{0}>0
,
then

|Rm|_{g(t)}(x,t)\leq\frac{1}{\epsilon_{n}^{2}r_{0}^{2}}\qquad\text{for all }x\in B_{g(t)}(x_{0},\epsilon_{n}r_{0}),t\in[t_{0},\min\{T,t_{0}+(\epsilon_{n}r_{0})^{2}\}).

Proposition 5.2

(Consequences of Pseudolocality)
.

Let
\Omega\Subset\{f>\Gamma_{0}/2\}\subset\mathcal{M}
.
3
3

3

U\Subset V
means that the closure of
U
is a compact subset of
V
.

If

\Gamma_{0}\gg 1
is sufficiently large (depending on
n,M,\overline{g},f
),
then there exists
\mathcal{K}_{0}
(depending on
n,M,\overline{g},f,\Gamma_{0},\Omega
) such that if

0<1-t_{0}\ll 1
is sufficiently small (depending on
n,M,\overline{g},f,\Gamma_{0},\Omega
),
then
for all
|\mathbf{p}|\leq\overline{p}e^{\lambda_{*}\tau_{0}}

|Rm[G_{\mathbf{p}}(t)]|_{G_{\mathbf{p}}(t)}(x,t)\leq\mathcal{K}_{0}\qquad\text{for all }(x,t)\in\Omega\times[t_{0},T(\mathbf{p})).

Proof.

Let
\epsilon_{n},\delta_{n}>0
be as in the statement of Perelman’s pseudolocality Theorem
5.1
above.

Throughout
\{f>\Gamma_{0}/2\}
,

G_{\mathbf{p}}(t_{0})=G_{\mathbf{0}}(t_{0})
for all
\mathbf{p}

and, for some
C=C(n,M,\overline{g},f)
,

(5.1)

|Rm[G_{\mathbf{p}}(t_{0})]|_{G_{\mathbf{p}}(t_{0})}\leq\frac{C}{\Gamma_{0}}

by Definition
4.2
(
4
) if
0<1-t_{0}\ll 1
is sufficiently small.
Additionally, Definition
4.2
(
2
) & (
3
) imply that the restriction of the metrics
G_{\mathbf{p}}(t_{0})
to
\{f>\Gamma_{0}/2\}
stay within an arbitrarily small
C^{0}
-neighborhood for all
0<1-t_{0}\ll 1
sufficiently small.

Since
\Omega\Subset\{f>\Gamma_{0}/2\}
,
there exists
0<r_{0}\ll 1
sufficiently small
depending on
n,M,\overline{g},f,\Gamma_{0},\Omega

so that, for all
0<1-t_{0}\ll 1
sufficiently small,

\frac{C}{\Gamma_{0}}\leq\frac{1}{r_{0}^{2}}\qquad\text{and}\qquad B_{G_{\mathbf{p}}(t_{0})}(x,r_{0})\subset\{f>\Gamma_{0}/2\}\qquad\text{for all }x\in\Omega.

Moreover, by taking
0<r_{0}\ll 1
possibly smaller (depending on
n,M,\overline{g},f,\Gamma_{0},\Omega
), we claim that additionally

(5.2)

\text{Vol}\,B_{G_{\mathbf{p}}(t_{0})}(x,r_{0})\geq(1-\delta_{n})\omega_{n}r_{0}^{n}\qquad\text{for all }x\in\Omega\text{ and all }0<1-t_{0}\ll 1.

Indeed, if not, then there exists a sequence
x_{i}\in\Omega
,
t_{i}\nearrow 1
, and
r_{i}\searrow 0
where (
5.2
) fails.
By the curvature estimates (
5.1
) and non-collapsing condition (Definition
4.2
(
5
)), the blow-up sequence
r_{i}^{-2}G_{\mathbf{p}}(t_{i})
based at
x_{i}
has a subsequence which converges in the pointed
C^{1,\alpha}
-topology to Euclidean space
(\mathbb{R}^{n},g_{Euc})
.
4
4

4

See for example
[
Pet16
, proof of Lemma 11.4.9, p. 433]
and references therein for a detailed justification of this statement.

Thus, the inequality (
5.2
) holds for
i\gg 1
, which contradicts the choice of the sequence.
This contradiction proves (
5.2
).

If
0<1-t_{0}\ll 1
is also small enough so that

T(\mathbf{p})-t_{0}\leq 1-t_{0}\leq\epsilon_{n}^{2}r_{0}^{2},

then Perelman’s pseudolocality Theorem
5.1

applies for all
x_{0}=x\in\Omega
and implies that

|Rm|(x,t)\leq\frac{1}{\epsilon_{n}^{2}r_{0}^{2}}\qquad\text{for all }(x,t)\in\Omega\times[t_{0},T(\mathbf{p})).

Taking
\mathcal{K}_{0}=(\epsilon_{n}r_{0})^{-2}
completes the proof.
∎

Remark 5.3
.

As a corollary of Proposition
5.2
, it follows that no singularity of the flow
\{G_{\mathbf{p}}(t)\}_{t\in[t_{0},T(\mathbf{p}))}

occurs on
\Omega\Subset\{f>\Gamma_{0}/2\}\subset\mathcal{M}
, so long as the parameters
\Gamma_{0}\gg 1
and
0<1-t_{0}\ll 1
are chosen appropriately and
|\mathbf{p}|\leq\overline{p}e^{\lambda_{*}\tau_{0}}
.

5.1.
Estimates on the Grafting Region

Next, we obtain estimates on the
grafting region

(5.3)

\Omega_{\eta_{\Gamma_{0}}}\doteqdot\{x\in M:0<\eta_{\Gamma_{0}}(x)<1\}.

Recall from Subsection
4.3
that
\eta_{\Gamma_{0}}
was chosen so that
\Omega_{\eta_{\Gamma_{0}}}\subset\{\frac{4}{6}\Gamma_{0}<f<\frac{5}{6}\Gamma_{0}\}\Subset\{\frac{1}{2}\Gamma_{0}<f<\Gamma_{0}\}\subset M
.
Therefore, Proposition
5.2
applied to
\Omega=\iota^{-1}\left(\bigsqcup_{\omega\in A}\Omega_{\eta_{\Gamma_{0}}}\right)
implies that
we may assume without loss of generality that

(5.4)

|Rm[G_{\mathbf{p}}(t)]|_{G_{\mathbf{p}}(t)}(x,t)\leq\mathcal{K}_{0}\qquad\text{for all }(x,t)\in\iota^{-1}\left(\bigsqcup_{\omega\in A}\Omega_{\eta_{\Gamma_{0}}}\right)\times[t_{0},T(\mathbf{p}))

for some
\mathcal{K}_{0}
(depending on
n,M,\overline{g},f,\Gamma_{0}
)
when
0<1-t_{0}\ll 1
is sufficiently small (depending on
n,M,\overline{g},f,\Gamma_{0}
).

We also note that Definition
4.2
implies

•

on
\overline{\iota^{-1}\left(\bigsqcup_{\omega\in A}\Omega_{\eta_{\Gamma_{0}}}\right)}\subset\mathcal{M}

(5.5)

\iota^{*}\acute{G}_{\mathbf{p}}(t_{0})=G_{\mathbf{p}}(t_{0})=G_{\mathbf{0}}(t_{0})=(1-t_{0})\iota^{*}\varphi_{t_{0}}^{*}\overline{g},

and

•

there exists an an embedding
\Psi:\{x\in M:\Gamma_{0}/2<f(x)<32\Gamma_{0}\}\to\mathcal{C}_{1}(\Sigma)
such that

\acute{G}_{\mathbf{p}}(t_{0})\xrightarrow[t_{0}\nearrow 1]{C^{\infty}\left(\overline{\Omega_{\eta_{\Gamma_{0}}}}\right)}\Psi^{*}g_{\mathcal{C}}.

We combine these facts to estimate how much
\acute{G}
differs from a Ricci flow.

Lemma 5.4
.

There exists
C
(depending only on
n,M,\overline{g},f
) such that
if
0<1-t_{0}\ll 1
is sufficiently small (depending on
n,M,\overline{g},f,\Gamma_{0}
),
then

\displaystyle\left|\partial_{t}\acute{G}_{\mathbf{p}}+2Rc[\acute{G}_{\mathbf{p}}(t)]\right|_{\acute{G}_{\mathbf{p}}(t)}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{C}{\Gamma_{0}}\quad\text{and}

\displaystyle\left|{}^{\acute{G}_{\mathbf{p}}(t)}\nabla\left(\partial_{t}\acute{G}_{\mathbf{p}}+2Rc[\acute{G}_{\mathbf{p}}(t)]\right)\right|_{\acute{G}_{\mathbf{p}}(t)}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{C}{\Gamma_{0}^{3/2}}

for all
x\in M
,
t\in[t_{0},T(\mathbf{p}))
, and all
|\mathbf{p}|\leq\overline{p}e^{\lambda_{*}\tau_{0}}
.

Proof.

Throughout this proof, we shall omit the subscript
\mathbf{p}
to simplify notation.
First, note that by Remark
4.18

\partial_{t}\acute{G}=-2Rc[\acute{G}]\qquad\text{ on }M\setminus\overline{\Omega_{\eta_{\Gamma_{0}}}}

so it suffices to estimate
\partial_{t}\acute{G}+2Rc[\acute{G}]
on
\overline{\Omega_{\eta_{\Gamma_{0}}}}
.

Throughout the proof, set

\Omega\doteqdot\iota^{-1}\left(\bigsqcup_{\omega\in A}\Omega_{\eta_{\Gamma_{0}}}\right)\subset\mathcal{M}.

By (
5.4
),

|Rm[G(t)]|_{G(t)}(x,t)\leq\mathcal{K}_{0}
for all
(x,t)\in\overline{\Omega}\times[t_{0},T(\mathbf{p}))
, which implies that

|G(t)-G(t_{0})|_{G(t)}\leq C_{n}\mathcal{K}_{0}(t-t_{0})\leq C_{n}\mathcal{K}_{0}(1-t_{0})\qquad\text{on }\overline{\Omega}\times[t_{0},T(\mathbf{p})).

In particular,
for any
C^{0}(\overline{\Omega})
-neighborhood of
G(t_{0})
,
G(t)
remains in that neighborhood for all
t\in[t_{0},T(\mathbf{p}))
if
0<1-t_{0}\ll 1
is sufficiently small.

By applying Proposition
5.2
to a slightly larger neighborhood
\Omega^{\prime}
containing
\overline{\Omega}
,
the curvature bounds
|Rm|_{G}\leq\mathcal{K}_{0}
on
\Omega^{\prime}\times[t_{0},T(\mathbf{p}))
,
Shi’s local derivative estimates
[
Shi89
]
,
5
5

5

See also
[
CCG+07
, Theorem 14.16]
.

and the regularity of the initial data
G(t_{0})
on
\{\Gamma_{0}/2<f<\Gamma_{0}\}

imply that
for all
m\in\mathbb{N}
there exists
\mathcal{K}_{m}
(depending only on
n,M,\overline{g},f,\Gamma_{0},\mathcal{K}_{0}
)
such that

|{}^{G(t)}\nabla^{m}Rm|_{G(t)}\leq\mathcal{K}_{m}\qquad\text{ on }\overline{\Omega}\times[t_{0},T(\mathbf{p})).

It follows that, for any
m\in\mathbb{N}
,

G(t)
can be made to stay in an arbitrarily small
C^{m}(\overline{\Omega})
-neighborhood
of
G(t_{0})
for all
t\in[t_{0},T(\mathbf{p}))

by taking
0<T(\mathbf{p})-t_{0}\leq 1-t_{0}\ll 1
sufficiently small.

Therefore, for all
t\in[t_{0},T(\mathbf{p}))
,

\iota_{*}G(t),\iota_{*}G(t_{0}),(1-t)\varphi_{t}^{*}\overline{g},(1-t_{0})\varphi_{t_{0}}^{*}\overline{g}
can all be made to stay within an arbitrarily small
C^{3}(\overline{\Omega_{\eta_{\Gamma_{0}}}})
-neighborhood of each other by taking
0<1-t_{0}\ll 1
sufficiently small depending on
n,M,\overline{g},f,\Gamma_{0}
.
Hence,

\acute{G}(t)-\acute{G}(t_{0})=\eta_{\Gamma_{0}}\iota_{*}\left[G(t)-G(t_{0})\right]+(1-\eta_{\Gamma_{0}})\left[(1-t)\varphi_{t}^{*}\overline{g}-(1-t_{0})\varphi_{t_{0}}^{*}\overline{g}\right]

can be made
C^{3}
-small on
\overline{\Omega_{\eta_{\Gamma_{0}}}}\times[t_{0},T(\mathbf{p}))

by taking
0<1-t_{0}\ll 1
sufficiently small.
Recall also (
5.5
) that
\iota_{*}G(t_{0})=\acute{G}(t_{0})=(1-t_{0})\varphi_{t_{0}}^{*}\overline{g}
on
\overline{\Omega_{\eta_{\Gamma_{0}}}}
.
In particular,
0<1-t_{0}\ll 1
sufficiently small implies that on
\overline{\Omega_{\eta_{\Gamma_{0}}}}\times[t_{0},T(\mathbf{p}))

\displaystyle|Rc[\acute{G}(t)]|_{\acute{G}(t)}\leq 2|Rc[\acute{G}(t_{0})]|_{\acute{G}(t_{0})}=2|Rc[G_{\mathbf{0}}(t_{0})]|_{G_{\mathbf{0}}(t_{0})}\lesssim_{n,M,\overline{g},f}\Gamma_{0}^{-1}\quad\text{and}

\displaystyle|{}^{\acute{G}(t)}\nabla Rc[\acute{G}(t)]|_{\acute{G}(t)}\leq 2|{}^{\iota^{*}\acute{G}_{\mathbf{0}}(t_{0})}\nabla Rc[G_{\mathbf{0}}(t_{0})]|_{\iota^{*}\acute{G}_{\mathbf{0}}(t_{0})}\lesssim_{n,M,\overline{g},f}\Gamma_{0}^{-3/2}

where we have used Definition
4.2
(
4
) in the last inequality of each line above.

Similarly,
0<1-t_{0}\ll 1
sufficiently small implies that on
\overline{\Omega_{\eta_{\Gamma_{0}}}}\times[t_{0},T(\mathbf{p}))

\displaystyle\quad\left|\partial_{t}\acute{G}\right|_{\acute{G}(t)}

\displaystyle=\left|\eta_{\Gamma_{0}}\iota_{*}\partial_{t}G+(1-\eta_{\Gamma_{0}})\partial_{t}\left((1-t)\varphi_{t}^{*}\overline{g}\right)\right|_{\acute{G}(t)}

\displaystyle=\left|-2\eta_{\Gamma_{0}}\iota_{*}Rc[G(t)]-2(1-\eta_{\Gamma_{0}})Rc\left[(1-t)\varphi_{t}^{*}\overline{g}\right]\right|_{\acute{G}(t)}

\displaystyle\leq 2\left|Rc[G(t)]\right|_{\iota^{*}\acute{G}(t)}+2\left|Rc[(1-t)\varphi_{t}^{*}\overline{g}]\right|_{\acute{G}(t)}

\displaystyle\leq 4\left|Rc[G(t_{0})]\right|_{\iota^{*}\acute{G}(t_{0})}+4\left|Rc[(1-t_{0})\varphi_{t_{0}}^{*}\overline{g}]\right|_{\acute{G}(t_{0})}

\displaystyle=8\left|Rc[G_{\mathbf{0}}(t_{0})]\right|_{G_{\mathbf{0}}(t_{0})}

\displaystyle\lesssim_{n,M,\overline{g},f}\Gamma_{0}^{-1},

and similarly

\displaystyle\quad\left|{}^{\acute{G}(t)}\nabla\partial_{t}\acute{G}\right|_{\acute{G}(t)}

\displaystyle=\left|{}^{\acute{G}(t)}\nabla\left(-2\eta_{\Gamma_{0}}\iota_{*}Rc[G(t)]-2(1-\eta_{\Gamma_{0}})Rc[(1-t)\varphi_{t}^{*}\overline{g}]\right)\right|_{\acute{G}(t)}

\displaystyle\lesssim_{n}\left|{}^{\acute{G}(t)}\nabla\eta_{\Gamma_{0}}\right|_{\acute{G}(t)}\left(\left|Rc[G(t)]\right|_{\iota^{*}\acute{G}(t)}+\left|Rc[(1-t)\varphi_{t}^{*}\overline{g}]\right|_{\acute{G}(t)}\right)

\displaystyle\qquad+\left|{}^{\iota^{*}\acute{G}(t)}\nabla Rc[G(t)]\right|_{\iota^{*}\acute{G}(t)}+\left|{}^{\acute{G}(t)}\nabla Rc[(1-t)\varphi_{t}^{*}\overline{g}]\right|_{\acute{G}(t)}

\displaystyle\leq 2\left|{}^{\acute{G}(t_{0})}\nabla\eta_{\Gamma_{0}}\right|_{\acute{G}(t_{0})}\left(\left|Rc[G(t_{0})]\right|_{\iota^{*}\acute{G}(t_{0})}+\left|Rc[(1-t_{0})\varphi_{t_{0}}^{*}\overline{g}]\right|_{\acute{G}(t_{0})}\right)

\displaystyle\qquad+2\left|{}^{\iota^{*}\acute{G}(t_{0})}\nabla Rc[G(t_{0})]\right|_{\iota^{*}\acute{G}(t_{0})}+2\left|{}^{\acute{G}(t_{0})}\nabla Rc[(1-t_{0})\varphi_{t_{0}}^{*}\overline{g}]\right|_{\acute{G}(t_{0})}

\displaystyle=4\left|{}^{\acute{G}_{\mathbf{0}}(t_{0})}\nabla\eta_{\Gamma_{0}}\right|_{\acute{G}_{\mathbf{0}}(t_{0})}\left|Rc[G_{\mathbf{0}}(t_{0})]\right|_{\iota^{*}\acute{G}_{\mathbf{0}}(t_{0})}+4\left|{}^{\iota^{*}\acute{G}_{\mathbf{0}}(t_{0})}\nabla Rc[G_{\mathbf{0}}(t_{0})]\right|_{\iota^{*}\acute{G}_{\mathbf{0}}(t_{0})}

\displaystyle\lesssim_{n,M,\overline{g},f}\left|{}^{\acute{G}_{\mathbf{0}}(t_{0})}\nabla\eta_{\Gamma_{0}}\right|_{\acute{G}_{\mathbf{0}}(t_{0})}\frac{1}{\Gamma_{0}}+\frac{1}{\Gamma_{0}^{3/2}}

\displaystyle\lesssim_{n,M,\overline{g},f}\Gamma_{0}^{-3/2}.

∎

Lemma 5.5

(Drift of the Grafting Region)
.

Assume
0\leq t_{0}<t_{1}\leq T(\mathbf{p})
,

\tilde{\Phi}:M\times[t_{0},t_{1})\to M
is a smooth solution of the harmonic map heat flow

\partial_{t}\tilde{\Phi}_{t}=\Delta_{\acute{G}_{\mathbf{p}}(t),(1-t)\varphi_{t}^{*}\overline{g}}\tilde{\Phi}\qquad\text{with initial condition }\tilde{\Phi}(\cdot,t_{0})=Id_{M},

\tilde{\Phi}_{t}=\tilde{\Phi}(\cdot,t):M\to M
is a diffeomorphism for all
t\in[t_{0},t_{1})
,
and

h=h_{\mathbf{p}}(t)=\frac{1}{1-t}(\Phi_{t}^{-1})^{*}\acute{G}_{\mathbf{p}}(t)-\overline{g}\in\mathcal{B}[\lambda_{*},\mu_{u},\mu_{s},\epsilon_{0},\epsilon_{1},\epsilon_{2},[t_{0},t_{1})].

If
\Gamma_{0}\gg 1
is sufficiently large (depending on
n,M,\overline{g},f
),

0<\epsilon_{0},\epsilon_{1}\ll 1
are sufficiently small (depending on
n
),
and
0<1-t_{0}\ll 1
is sufficiently small (depending on
n,M,\overline{g},f,\Gamma_{0}
),
then, for all
t\in[t_{0},t_{1})
,

\displaystyle\tilde{\Phi}_{t}(\overline{\Omega_{\eta_{\Gamma_{0}}}})\subset{}

\displaystyle\{\Gamma_{0}/2<f<\Gamma_{0}\},

\displaystyle\Phi_{t}(\overline{\Omega_{\eta_{\Gamma_{0}}}})\subset{}

\displaystyle\{(1-t)^{-1}<f<\Gamma_{0}(1-t)^{-1}\},

\displaystyle\supp\left((\Phi_{t}^{-1})^{*}\left\{\partial_{t}\acute{G}+2Rc[\acute{G}]\right\}\right)\subset{}

\displaystyle\{(1-t)^{-1}<f<\Gamma_{0}(1-t)^{-1}\},\text{ and}

\displaystyle\supp h\subset{}

\displaystyle\{f<\Gamma_{0}(1-t)^{-1}\}.

Proof.

Recall

\Omega_{\eta_{\Gamma_{0}}}\subset\left\{\frac{4}{6}\Gamma_{0}<f<\frac{5}{6}\Gamma_{0}\right\}\Subset\Omega^{\prime}\doteqdot\{\Gamma_{0}/2<f<\Gamma_{0}\}\Subset M

and
(1-t)\varphi_{t}^{*}\overline{g}
solves Ricci flow on
M\times(-\infty,1)
.
If
\Gamma_{0}\gg 1
is sufficiently large,

(1-t)\varphi_{t}^{*}\overline{g}
has uniformly bounded curvature on
\Omega^{\prime}\times[0,1)
and
converges to a pullback of the cone metric on
\Omega^{\prime}
as
t\nearrow 1
.
In particular, for all
t_{0}\in[0,1)
,
\dist_{(1-t_{0})\varphi_{t_{0}}^{*}\overline{g}}(\Omega_{\eta_{\Gamma_{0}}},M\setminus\Omega^{\prime})
is bounded below by a constant depending only on
n,M,\overline{g},f,\Gamma_{0}
.

Since
h\in\mathcal{B}[\lambda_{*},\mu_{u},\mu_{s},\epsilon_{0},\epsilon_{1},\epsilon_{2},[t_{0},t_{1})]
,

\left|(\tilde{\Phi}_{t}^{-1})^{*}\acute{G}_{\mathbf{p}}(t)-(1-t)\varphi_{t}^{*}\overline{g}\right|_{(1-t)\varphi_{t}^{*}\overline{g}}=\left|\frac{1}{1-t}(\Phi_{t}^{-1})^{*}\acute{G}_{\mathbf{p}}(t)-\overline{g}\right|_{\overline{g}}=\left|h_{\mathbf{p}}(t)\right|_{\overline{g}}\leq\epsilon_{0}

and similarly

\sqrt{1-t}\left|{}^{(1-t)\varphi_{t}^{*}\overline{g}}\nabla\left((\tilde{\Phi}_{t}^{-1})^{*}\acute{G}_{\mathbf{p}}(t)-(1-t)\varphi_{t}^{*}\overline{g}\right)\right|_{(1-t)\varphi_{t}^{*}\overline{g}}=|\overline{\nabla}h_{\mathbf{p}}(t)|_{\overline{g}}\leq\epsilon_{1}.

If
\epsilon_{0}+\epsilon_{1}\ll 1
is sufficiently small depending on
n

and
0<1-t_{0}\ll 1
is sufficiently small depending on
n,M,\overline{g},f,\Gamma_{0}
,
then the local drift estimates of Lemma
B.5
imply

\tilde{\Phi}_{t}(x)\in\Omega^{\prime}=\{\Gamma_{0}/2<f<\Gamma_{0}\}\qquad\text{for all }x\in\overline{\Omega_{\eta_{\Gamma_{0}}}},t\in[t_{0},t_{1})

since
\tilde{\Phi}_{t_{0}}(x)=x\in\overline{\Omega_{\eta_{\Gamma_{0}}}}
.
This proves the first claim.

For the remainder of the proof, we follow Remark
4.19
and work in terms of
\tau=-\ln(1-t)
.
To prove the next claim,
it suffices to show that

\varphi_{\tau}(\{\Gamma_{0}/2<f<\Gamma_{0}\})\subset\{e^{\tau}<f<\Gamma_{0}e^{\tau}\}

when
\Gamma_{0}\gg 1
is sufficiently large depending on
n,M,\overline{g},f
.

By Lemma
2.33
, there exists a constant
C=C(n,M,\overline{g},f)
such that

\varphi_{\tau}^{*}f-\frac{C}{\varphi_{\tau}^{*}f}\leq\partial_{\tau}(\varphi_{\tau}^{*}f)\leq\varphi_{\tau}^{*}f\qquad\text{ for all }\tau\in\mathbb{R}.

Assume
\Gamma_{0}/2>\sqrt{C+1}
and
let
x\in M
such that
\sqrt{C+1}<\Gamma_{0}/2<f(x)<\Gamma_{0}.

Integrating the inequalities for
\partial_{\tau}(\varphi_{\tau}^{*}f)
from
0
to
\tau
and using that
\varphi_{0}=Id_{M}
then implies

f(\varphi_{\tau}(x))=(\varphi_{\tau}^{*}f)(x)\leq f(x)e^{\tau}<\Gamma_{0}e^{\tau}\qquad\text{and}

f(\varphi_{\tau}(x))\geq\sqrt{C+(f(x)^{2}-C)e^{2\tau}}>\sqrt{C+e^{2\tau}}\geq e^{\tau}.

This completes the proof that

\Phi_{t}(\overline{\Omega_{\eta_{\Gamma_{0}}}})\subset\{(1-t)^{-1}<f<\Gamma_{0}(1-t)^{-1}\}.

The remaining two claims then follow from Remarks
4.11
,
4.18
, and the definition of
h
as in Corollary
4.17
.
∎

6.
Preservation & Improvement of
C^{2}
Bounds

In what follows, we consider
h=h_{\mathbf{p}}
as a function of the reparametrized time variable
\tau=-\ln(1-t)
and write its evolution equation (
4.12
) as

(6.1)

\partial_{\tau}h=\overline{\Delta}_{f}h+2\overline{Rm}[h]+\mathcal{E}_{1}(\tau)+\mathcal{E}_{2}(\tau)

where

(6.2)

\displaystyle\begin{split}\mathcal{E}_{1}(\tau)\doteqdot{}&\overline{R}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[8.05121pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[8.05121pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[4.88843pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.49171pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{p}\mathchoice{\makebox[3.51666pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.51666pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.1029pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.50208pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{ja}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.12234pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.12234pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.46532pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.76094pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{b}}}\left(\overline{g}_{ip}\tilde{h}^{ab}+\hat{h}^{ab}h_{ip}\right)+\overline{R}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[7.16693pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[7.16693pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[4.2782pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.05585pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{p}\mathchoice{\makebox[3.51666pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.51666pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.1029pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.50208pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{ia}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.12234pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.12234pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.46532pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.76094pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{b}}}\left(\overline{g}_{jp}\tilde{h}^{ab}+\hat{h}^{ab}h_{jp}\right)\\
&+g^{ab}g^{pq}(\overline{\nabla}h*\overline{\nabla}h)-\hat{h}^{ab}\overline{\nabla}_{a}\overline{\nabla}_{b}h\qquad\text{and}\end{split}

(6.3)

\displaystyle\mathcal{E}_{2}(\tau(t))\doteqdot{}

\displaystyle(\Phi_{t}^{-1})^{*}\{\partial_{t}\acute{G}+\acute{Rc}\}.

In light of Lemmas
5.4
and
5.5
, we shall often assume

|\mathcal{E}_{2}|_{\overline{g}}\leq\frac{C_{0}}{\Gamma_{0}}e^{-\tau},\,|\overline{\nabla}\mathcal{E}_{2}|_{\overline{g}}\leq\frac{C_{0}}{\Gamma_{0}^{3/2}}e^{-\frac{3}{2}\tau},\text{ and }\supp\mathcal{E}_{2}\subset\{e^{\tau}\leq f\leq\Gamma_{0}e^{\tau}\}

where
C_{0}=C_{0}(n,M,\overline{g},f)\geq 1
is a constant that depends on
n,M,\overline{g},f
.

In this section, we aim to show the following theorem which says that
h
cannot exit through the sides of the box
\mathcal{B}
defined by the coarse
C^{2}
bounds:

Theorem 6.1
.

Assume
\lambda_{*}<0
is taken as in assumption
3.1
,

\overline{p}\in(0,1]
is taken as in assumption
4.9
, and that

h=h_{\mathbf{p}}
solves the equation

(
4.12
)

\partial_{\tau}h=\overline{\Delta}_{f}h+2\overline{Rm}[h]+\mathcal{E}_{1}+\mathcal{E}_{2}\qquad\text{ on }M\times(\tau_{0},\tau_{1})

where
|\mathbf{p}|\leq\overline{p}e^{\lambda_{*}\tau_{0}}
.
Assume

\displaystyle\|h\|_{L^{\infty}(M\times[\tau_{0},\tau_{1}])}\leq\epsilon<1,

\displaystyle h(\tau_{0})=\eta_{\gamma_{0}}\sum_{j=1}^{K}p_{j}h_{j},

\displaystyle\|h(\tau)\|_{L^{2}_{f}(M)}\leq\mu e^{\lambda_{*}\tau},

\displaystyle|\mathcal{E}_{2}|_{\overline{g}}\leq\frac{C_{0}}{\Gamma_{0}}e^{-\tau},\,|\overline{\nabla}\mathcal{E}_{2}|_{\overline{g}}\leq\frac{C_{0}}{\Gamma_{0}^{3/2}}e^{-\frac{3}{2}\tau},\text{ and }\supp\mathcal{E}_{2}\subset\{e^{\tau}\leq f\leq\Gamma_{0}e^{\tau}\}

where
C_{0}=C_{0}(n,M,\overline{g},f)\geq 1
depends only on
n,M,\overline{g},f
.

Let
0<\delta\leq\epsilon
.
If
0<\overline{p}\ll 1
is sufficiently small (depending on
n,M,\overline{g},f,\lambda_{*}
),

0<\epsilon\ll 1
is sufficiently small (depending on
n,M,\overline{g},f,\lambda_{*}
),

0<\mu\ll 1
is sufficiently small (depending on
n,M,\overline{g},f,\lambda_{*}
),

0<\gamma_{0}\ll 1
is sufficiently small (depending on
n,M,\overline{g},f,\lambda_{*},\delta
),

\tau_{0}\gg 1
is sufficiently large (depending on
n,M,\overline{g},f,\lambda_{*},\gamma_{0},\overline{p},\epsilon,\delta
),
then
h
satisfies the estimates

|h|+|\overline{\nabla}h|+|\overline{\nabla}^{2}h|\leq Wf^{|\lambda_{*}|}e^{\lambda_{*}\tau}\text{ and}

|h|+|\overline{\nabla}h|+|\overline{\nabla}^{2}h|\leq W^{\prime}\delta

throughout
M\times[\tau_{0},\tau_{1}]

where
W=W(n,M,\overline{g},f,\lambda_{*})

and
W^{\prime}=W^{\prime}(n,M,\overline{g},f)
.

The proof is based on partitioning
M
into three regions or scales
\{f<\Gamma\}
,
\{\Gamma<f<\gamma e^{\tau}\}
, and
\{\gamma e^{\tau}<f<\Gamma e^{\tau}\}
,
with
\Gamma\gg 1
sufficiently large and
0<\gamma\ll 1
sufficiently small.
For regions
\{\Gamma<f<\gamma e^{\tau}\}
and
\{\gamma e^{\tau}<f<\Gamma e^{\tau}\}
,
barriers provide
C^{0}
estimates for
h
and interior estimates bootstrap these
C^{0}
estimates to
C^{2}
estimates.
The estimates in the former region
\{\gamma e^{\tau}<f<\Gamma e^{\tau}\}
are simplified by the fact that
\mathcal{E}_{2}
is supported on
\{\gamma e^{\tau}<f<\Gamma e^{\tau}\}
for suitable choices of the parameters
\gamma,\Gamma
.
To estimate
h
on
\{f<\Gamma\}
, we use local derivative estimates and the
L^{2}_{f}
decay of
h
.

6.1.
Local Estimates

Lemma 6.2

(Local Interior Estimates:
C^{0}_{loc}
to
C^{m}_{loc}
)
.

Let
m\in\mathbb{N}
,
\Omega\Subset\Omega^{\prime}\Subset M
, and
\tau_{0}\leq\tau_{1}<\tau_{2}
.
Assume

\partial_{\tau}h=\overline{\Delta}_{f}h+2\overline{Rm}[h]+\mathcal{E}_{1}+\mathcal{E}_{2}\qquad\text{on }\Omega^{\prime}\times(\tau_{0},\tau_{2})

and that
\mathcal{E}_{2}\equiv 0
on
\Omega^{\prime}\times[\tau_{0},\tau_{2}]
.

If
\tau_{1}>\tau_{0}
, then there exists
\epsilon>0
(depending on
n,m
) and
C
(depending on
n,M,\overline{g},f,m,\sup_{\Omega^{\prime}}f,\dist_{\overline{g}}(\Omega,M\setminus\Omega^{\prime}),\tau_{1}-\tau_{0}
)
such that

\displaystyle\|h\|_{L^{\infty}(\Omega^{\prime}\times[\tau_{0},\tau_{2}])}<\epsilon\implies\|h\|_{C^{m}(\Omega\times[\tau_{1},\tau_{2}])}\leq C\|h\|_{L^{\infty}(\Omega^{\prime}\times[\tau_{0},\tau_{2}])}.

If
\tau_{1}=\tau_{0}
,
then there exists
\epsilon>0
(depending on
n,m
) and

C
(depending on
n,M,\overline{g},f,m,\sup_{\Omega^{\prime}}f,\dist_{\overline{g}}(\Omega,M\setminus\Omega^{\prime})
) such that

\displaystyle\|h\|_{L^{\infty}(\Omega^{\prime}\times[\tau_{0},\tau_{2}])}+\|h\|_{C^{m+1}(\Omega^{\prime}\times\{\tau_{0}\})}<\epsilon\implies

\displaystyle\|h\|_{C^{m}(\Omega\times[\tau_{0},\tau_{2}])}\leq C\left(\|h\|_{L^{\infty}(\Omega^{\prime}\times[\tau_{0},\tau_{2}])}+\|h\|_{C^{m+1}(\Omega^{\prime}\times\{\tau_{0}\})}\right).

Proof.

By assumption,

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} \partial_{\tau}h_{ij}={}&\overline{\Delta}h_{ij}-\overline{\nabla}_{\overline{\nabla}f}h+2\overline{R}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.68811pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.2058pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{k}\mathchoice{\makebox[3.71356pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.71356pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.29834pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.64166pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{l}}}_{{{i}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.42017pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.42017pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.7052pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.93228pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{j}\mathchoice{\makebox[2.56946pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[2.56946pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.55847pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.11319pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}h_{kl}\\
&+\overline{R}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[8.05121pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[8.05121pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[4.88843pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.49171pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{p}\mathchoice{\makebox[3.51666pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.51666pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.1029pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.50208pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{ja}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.12234pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.12234pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.46532pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.76094pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{b}}}\left(\overline{g}_{ip}\tilde{h}^{ab}+\hat{h}^{ab}h_{ip}\right)+\overline{R}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[7.16693pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[7.16693pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[4.2782pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.05585pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{p}\mathchoice{\makebox[3.51666pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.51666pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.1029pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.50208pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{ia}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.12234pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.12234pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.46532pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.76094pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{b}}}\left(\overline{g}_{jp}\tilde{h}^{ab}+\hat{h}^{ab}h_{jp}\right)\\
&+g^{ab}g^{pq}(\overline{\nabla}h*\overline{\nabla}h)_{abpqij}-\hat{h}^{ab}\overline{\nabla}_{a}\overline{\nabla}_{b}h\end{aligned}

on
\Omega^{\prime}\times(\tau_{0},\tau_{2})
.

In the case where
\tau_{1}>\tau_{0}
, define

r_{0}\doteqdot\frac{1}{2}\min\left\{\dist_{\overline{g}}(\Omega,M\setminus\Omega^{\prime}),\sqrt{\tau_{1}-\tau_{0}}\right\}>0.

By Klingenberg’s Theorem, there exists
0<r\leq r_{0}
depending on
n
and
\sup_{M}|\overline{Rm}|_{\overline{g}}<\infty
such that, for all
p\in M
,
B_{\overline{g}}(p,2r)
contains no points conjugate to
p
.

At each point
p\in\Omega
and each
\tau\in[\tau_{1},\tau_{2}]
, consider the domains
B_{\overline{g}}(p,r)\times[\tau-r^{2},\tau]\subset B_{\overline{g}}(p,2r)\times[\tau-(2r)^{2},\tau]\subset\Omega^{\prime}\times[\tau_{0},\tau_{2}]
.
By passing to a local cover if necessary, we may assume the exponential map at
p
is a diffeomorphism to
B_{\overline{g}}(p,2r)
.
After pulling back under this diffeomorphism,
h
satisfies a differential equation for which Proposition
C.3
applies with
F=0
.
Since
\overline{Rc}+\overline{\nabla}^{2}f=\frac{1}{2}\overline{g}
and
\overline{R}+|\overline{\nabla}f|^{2}_{\overline{g}}=f
, the coefficients in this differential equation and their derivatives can be bounded in terms of
\overline{Rm}
, its derivatives
\overline{\nabla}^{l}\overline{Rm}
, and
\sup_{\Omega^{\prime}}f
.
Proposition
C.3
with
F=0
thus yields the desired result.

In the case where
\tau_{1}=\tau_{0}
, define

r_{0}\doteqdot\frac{1}{2}\dist_{\overline{g}}(\Omega,M\setminus\Omega^{\prime})>0.

Again, take
0<r\leq r_{0}
depending on
n
and
\sup_{M}|\overline{Rm}|_{\overline{g}}<\infty
such that, for all
p\in M
,
B_{\overline{g}}(p,2r)
contains no points conjugate to
p
.

At each point
p\in\Omega
and
\tau\in[\tau_{0}+(2r)^{2},\tau_{2}]
