, we can invoke the previous case with
\tau_{1}=\tau_{0}+(2r)^{2}>\tau_{0}
.
If instead
\tau\in[\tau_{0},\tau_{0}+(2r)^{2}]\cap[\tau_{0},\tau_{2}]\doteqdot[\tau_{0},\tau_{2}^{\prime}]
, the result follows from similar logic as the previous case using instead the domains
B_{\overline{g}}(p,r)\times[\tau_{0},\tau_{2}^{\prime}]\subset B_{\overline{g}}(p,2r)\times[\tau_{0},\tau_{2}^{\prime}]\subset\Omega^{\prime}\times[\tau_{0},\tau_{2}]
and Proposition
C.4
with
F=0
.
∎

Lemma 6.3

(Local Interior Integral Estimates)
.

Let
m\in\mathbb{N}
,
\Omega\Subset\Omega^{\prime}\Subset M
,
and
\tau_{0}\leq\tau_{1}<\tau_{2}
.
Assume

\partial_{\tau}h=\overline{\Delta}_{f}h+2\overline{Rm}[h]+\mathcal{E}_{1}+\mathcal{E}_{2}\qquad\text{on }\Omega^{\prime}\times(\tau_{0},\tau_{2})

and that
\mathcal{E}_{2}\equiv 0
on
\Omega^{\prime}\times[\tau_{0},\tau_{2}]
.

If
\tau_{1}>\tau_{0}
, then there exists
\epsilon>0
(depending only on
n,m
)
and
C
(depending on
n,M,\overline{g},f,m,\Omega,\Omega^{\prime},\tau_{1}-\tau_{0}
)
such that

\displaystyle\|h\|_{C^{m+1}(\Omega^{\prime}\times[\tau_{0},\tau_{2}])}<\epsilon\implies

\displaystyle\sup_{\tau\in[\tau_{1},\tau_{2}]}\|\nabla^{m}h(\tau)\|_{L^{2}_{f}(\Omega)}+\|h\|_{L^{2}H^{m+1}_{f}(\Omega\times[\tau_{1},\tau_{2}])}\leq C\|h\|_{L^{2}H^{m}_{f}(\Omega^{\prime}\times[\tau_{0},\tau_{2}])}.

If
\tau_{1}=\tau_{0}
, then there exists
\epsilon>0
(depending only on
n,m
) and

C
(depending only on
n,M,\overline{g},f,m,\Omega,\Omega^{\prime}
) such that

\displaystyle\|h\|_{C^{m+1}(\Omega^{\prime}\times[\tau_{0},\tau_{2}])}<\epsilon\ \implies

\displaystyle\sup_{\tau\in[\tau_{0},\tau_{2}]}\|\nabla^{m}h(\tau)\|_{L^{2}_{f}(\Omega)}+\|h\|_{L^{2}H^{m+1}_{f}(\Omega\times[\tau_{0},\tau_{2}])}

\displaystyle\leq C\left(\|h(\tau_{0})\|_{H^{m}_{f}(\Omega^{\prime})}+\|h\|_{L^{2}H^{m}_{f}(\Omega^{\prime}\times[\tau_{0},\tau_{2}])}\right).

Proof.

For the proof we will omit overlines and it will be understood that all derivatives and curvatures are taken with respect to
\overline{g}
.

We first show that
h
and its derivatives satisfy evolution equations of the form

\partial_{\tau}\nabla^{m}h=\Delta_{f}\nabla^{m}h+\sum_{l=0}^{m+1}B_{l}^{m}*\nabla^{l}h-\div_{f}(\hat{h}*\nabla^{m+1}h)

where the tensors
B_{l}^{m}
satisfy certain estimates specified later in the proof.

First, recall that by equation (
6.1
) with
\mathcal{E}_{2}\equiv 0
on
\Omega^{\prime}

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} \partial_{\tau}h={}&\Delta_{f}h-\hat{h}^{ab}\nabla_{a}\nabla_{b}h+2Rm[h]\\
&+(g+h)^{-1}(g+h)^{-1}(\nabla h*\nabla h)+Rm*\tilde{h}+Rm*\hat{h}*h,\end{aligned}

\displaystyle\text{where }(g+h)^{ab}=\overline{g}^{ab}-\hat{h}^{ab}\ \text{and}\ \hat{h}^{ab}=\overline{g}^{ak}\overline{g}^{bl}h_{kl}+\tilde{h}^{ab}.

This evolution equation can be rewritten as

(6.4)

\partial_{\tau}h=\Delta_{f}h+B^{0}_{1}*\nabla h+B^{0}_{0}*h-\div_{f}(\hat{h}*\nabla h)

where

\displaystyle(\hat{h}*\nabla h)^{{{i}\mathchoice{\makebox[8.13373pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[8.13373pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.00354pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.57394pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.68811pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.2058pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{jk}}}=\hat{h}^{ip}\nabla_{p}h_{jk},

\displaystyle B^{0}_{1}\doteqdot\nabla h-\hat{h}*\nabla h-\hat{h}*\hat{h}*\nabla h+\nabla\hat{h}-\nabla f*\hat{h},\text{ and}

\displaystyle B^{0}_{0}\doteqdot 2Rm*+Rm*\hat{h}+Rm*T

\displaystyle\text{ where }T^{akbl}\doteqdot\overline{g}^{ak}(\overline{g}+h)^{bl}-\overline{g}^{ak}\overline{g}^{bl}\text{ so that }\tilde{h}^{ab}=T^{akbl}h_{kl}.

Observe that if
0<\epsilon\ll 1
is sufficiently small depending only on
n
,
then there exists a constant
C_{0}
depending on
n,M,\overline{g},f,\Omega^{\prime}
such that

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} \|h(\tau)\|_{C^{1}(\Omega^{\prime})}<\epsilon\implies{}&\|B^{0}_{1}\|_{L^{\infty}(\Omega^{\prime})}+\|B^{0}_{0}\|_{L^{\infty}(\Omega^{\prime})}<C_{0}.\end{aligned}

Taking derivatives
\nabla^{m}
of the evolution equation (
6.4
) for
h
yields

\displaystyle\partial_{\tau}\nabla^{m}h

\displaystyle={}

\displaystyle\nabla^{m}\Delta_{f}h+\nabla^{m}(B^{0}_{1}*\nabla h)+\nabla^{m}(B^{0}_{0}*h)-\nabla^{m}\div_{f}(\hat{h}^{ip}\nabla_{p}\mathcal{H}_{jk})

\displaystyle={}

\displaystyle\Delta_{f}\nabla^{m}h+\sum_{l=0}^{m}\nabla^{l}Rm*\nabla^{m-l}h

\displaystyle+\sum_{l=1}^{m}\nabla f*\nabla^{l-1}Rm*\nabla^{m-l}h+\sum_{l=1}^{m}\nabla^{l+1}f*\nabla^{m-l+1}h

\displaystyle+\sum_{l=0}^{m}\nabla^{l}B^{0}_{1}*\nabla^{m-l+1}h+\sum_{l=0}^{m}\nabla^{l}B^{0}_{0}*\nabla^{m-l}h

\displaystyle-\div_{f}\left(\sum_{l=0}^{m}\nabla^{l}\hat{h}*\nabla^{m-l+1}h\right)+\sum_{l=0}^{m-1}\nabla^{l}Rm*\nabla^{m-1-l}(\hat{h}*\nabla h).

By the definition of
B_{0}^{0}
and
B_{0}^{1}
, this equation may be rewritten as

\partial_{\tau}h=\Delta_{f}\nabla^{m}h+\sum_{l=0}^{m+1}B_{l}^{m}*\nabla^{l}h-\div_{f}(\hat{h}*\nabla^{m+1}h)

where the tensors
B_{l}^{m}
are continuous functions of
h,\nabla h,\dots,\nabla^{m+1}h
, and
x\in M
.
Hence, if
0<\epsilon\ll 1
is sufficiently small depending on
n,m
, there exists a constant
C_{m}
depending on
n,M,\overline{g},f,m,\Omega^{\prime}
such that

\displaystyle\|h(\tau)\|_{C^{m+1}(\Omega^{\prime})}<\epsilon\implies\sum_{l=0}^{m+1}\|B^{m}_{l}\|_{L^{\infty}(\Omega^{\prime})}<C_{m}.

Now, assume we are in the case where
\tau_{1}>\tau_{0}
.
Take a bump function
\eta:M\times\mathbb{R}\to[0,1]
with

\eta\equiv 1\text{ on }\Omega\times[\tau_{1},\tau_{2}]\,\text{ and }\,\supp(\eta)\subset\Omega^{\prime}\times[\tau_{0},+\infty)

and assume
\eta
is suitably smooth so that its derivatives
\nabla^{m}\eta
are bounded by constants that depend only on
n,M,\overline{g},m,\Omega,\Omega^{\prime},\tau_{1}-\tau_{0}
.

From the evolution equation for
\nabla^{m}h
, we now estimate the time derivative of

\int\eta^{2}|\nabla^{m}h|^{2}e^{-f}=\int_{M}\eta^{2}|\nabla^{m}h|^{2}e^{-f}dV_{\overline{g}}.

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} &\frac{d}{d\tau}\frac{1}{2}\int\eta^{2}|\nabla^{m}h|^{2}e^{-f}\\
={}&\int\eta^{2}\langle\nabla^{m}h,\partial_{\tau}\nabla^{m}h\rangle e^{-f}+\int\eta\eta_{\tau}|\nabla^{m}h|^{2}e^{-f}\\
={}&\int\eta^{2}\langle\nabla^{m}h,\Delta_{f}\nabla^{m}h\rangle e^{-f}+\int\eta\eta_{\tau}|\nabla^{m}h|^{2}e^{-f}\\
&+\int\eta^{2}\langle\nabla^{m}h,B^{m}_{m+1}*\nabla^{m+1}h\rangle e^{-f}+\sum_{l=0}^{m}\int\eta^{2}\langle\nabla^{m}h,B^{m}_{l}*\nabla^{l}h\rangle e^{-f}\\
&-\int\eta^{2}\langle\nabla^{m}h,\div_{f}(\hat{h}*\nabla^{m+1}h)\rangle e^{-f}\\
\leq{}&-\int\eta^{2}|\nabla^{m+1}h|^{2}e^{-f}+C(n,m)\int\eta|\nabla\eta||\nabla^{m}h||\nabla^{m+1}h|e^{-f}+\int\eta\eta_{\tau}|\nabla^{m}h|^{2}e^{-f}\\
&+C(n,m)\int\eta^{2}|\nabla^{m}h||B^{m}_{m+1}||\nabla^{m+1}h|e^{-f}+C(n,m)\sum_{l=0}^{m}\int\eta^{2}|\nabla^{m}h||B^{m}_{l}||\nabla^{l}h|e^{-f}\\
&+C(n,m)\int\eta^{2}|\nabla^{m+1}h||\hat{h}||\nabla^{m+1}h|e^{-f}+C(n,m)\int 2\eta|\nabla\eta||\nabla^{m}h||\hat{h}||\nabla^{m+1}h|e^{-f}.\end{aligned}

Invoking Young’s inequality, for any
\delta>0
, the above expression can be bounded by

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} &\frac{d}{d\tau}\frac{1}{2}\int\eta^{2}|\nabla^{m}h|^{2}e^{-f}\\
\leq{}&-\int\eta^{2}|\nabla^{m+1}h|^{2}e^{-f}+\delta\int\eta^{2}|\nabla^{m+1}h|^{2}e^{-f}+\frac{C(n,m)}{4\delta}\int|\nabla\eta|^{2}|\nabla^{m}h|^{2}e^{-f}\\
&+\int\eta\eta_{\tau}|\nabla^{m}h|^{2}e^{-f}\\
&+\delta\int\eta^{2}|\nabla^{m+1}h|^{2}e^{-f}+\frac{C(n,m)}{4\delta}\|B^{m}_{m+1}(\tau)\|^{2}_{L^{\infty}(\Omega^{\prime})}\int\eta^{2}|\nabla^{m}h|^{2}e^{-f}\\
&+C(n,m)\left(\sum_{l=0}^{m}\|B^{m}_{l}(\tau)\|_{L^{\infty}(\Omega^{\prime})}\right)\|h(\tau)\|^{2}_{H^{m}_{f}(\Omega^{\prime})}\\
&+C(n,m)\|\hat{h}(\tau)\|_{L^{\infty}(\Omega^{\prime})}\int\eta^{2}|\nabla^{m+1}h|^{2}e^{-f}+C(n,m)\|\hat{h}(\tau)\|_{L^{\infty}(\Omega^{\prime})}\int|\nabla\eta|^{2}|\nabla^{m}h|^{2}e^{-f}\\
\leq{}&\left(-1+2\delta+C(n,m)\|\hat{h}(\tau)\|_{L^{\infty}(\Omega^{\prime})}\right)\int\eta^{2}|\nabla^{m+1}h|^{2}e^{-f}\\
&+C(n,m)\left(\frac{\|\nabla\eta\|_{\infty}^{2}}{\delta}+\|\eta\eta_{\tau}\|_{\infty}+\frac{\|B^{m}_{m+1}\|_{L^{\infty}(\Omega^{\prime})}^{2}}{\delta}+\|\hat{h}\|_{L^{\infty}(\Omega^{\prime})}\|\nabla\eta\|_{\infty}^{2}\right)\int_{\Omega^{\prime}}|\nabla^{m}h|^{2}e^{-f}\\
&+C(n,m)\left(\sum_{l=0}^{m}\|B^{m}_{l}\|_{L^{\infty}(\Omega^{\prime})}\right)\|h\|^{2}_{H^{m}_{f}(\Omega^{\prime})}.\end{aligned}

Take
0<\epsilon\ll 1
sufficiently small depending on
n,m
so that

\|h(\tau)\|_{C^{m+1}(\Omega^{\prime})}<\epsilon

implies

\displaystyle C(n,m)\|\hat{h}(\tau)\|_{L^{\infty}(\Omega^{\prime})}\leq\frac{1}{2}\quad\text{ and }\quad\sum_{l=0}^{m+1}\|B^{m}_{l}\|_{L^{\infty}(\Omega^{\prime})}\leq C(n,M,\overline{g},f,m,\Omega^{\prime}).

Taking
\delta=1/8
, it then follows that

\displaystyle\frac{d}{d\tau}\int\eta^{2}|\nabla^{m}h|^{2}e^{-f}dV+\frac{1}{4}\int\eta^{2}|\nabla^{m+1}h|^{2}e^{-f}\leq C\sum_{l=0}^{m}\int_{\Omega^{\prime}}|\nabla^{l}h|^{2}e^{-f}dV

for some constant
C=C(n,M,\overline{g},f,m,\Omega^{\prime},\Omega,\tau_{1}-\tau_{0})

whenever
\|h(\tau)\|_{C^{m+1}(\Omega^{\prime})}<\epsilon.

Integrating this estimate from
\tau_{0}
to
\tau\in[\tau_{1},\tau_{2}]

under the assumption that
\|h\|_{C^{m+1}(\Omega^{\prime}\times[\tau_{0},\tau_{2}])}<\epsilon

then implies

\displaystyle\quad\,\int_{\Omega}|\nabla^{m}h(\tau)|^{2}e^{-f}dV+\frac{1}{4}\int_{\tau_{1}}^{\tau}\int_{\Omega}|\nabla^{m+1}h|^{2}e^{-f}dVd\tilde{\tau}

\displaystyle\leq\int_{M}\eta^{2}|\nabla^{m}h(\tau)|^{2}e^{-f}dV+\frac{1}{4}\int_{\tau_{0}}^{\tau}\int_{M}\eta^{2}|\nabla^{m+1}h|^{2}e^{-f}dVd\tilde{\tau}

\displaystyle(\text{choice of $\eta$})

\displaystyle\leq C\iint_{\Omega^{\prime}\times[\tau_{0},\tau_{2}]}\sum_{l=0}^{m}|\nabla^{l}h|^{2}e^{-f}dVd\tilde{\tau}.

Since
\tau\in[\tau_{1},\tau_{2}]
was arbitrary, it follows that
\|h\|_{C^{m+1}(\Omega^{\prime}\times[\tau_{0},\tau_{2}])}<\epsilon
implies

\displaystyle\sup_{\tau\in[\tau_{1},\tau_{2}]}\|\nabla^{m}h\|_{L^{2}_{f}(\Omega)}+\|h\|_{L^{2}H^{m+1}_{f}(\Omega\times[\tau_{1},\tau_{2}])}\leq C\|h\|_{L^{2}H^{m}_{f}(\Omega^{\prime}\times[\tau_{0},\tau_{2}])}.

for some
C
depending on
n,M,\overline{g},f,m,\Omega^{\prime},\Omega,\tau_{1}-\tau_{0}
.

In the case where
\tau_{1}=\tau_{0}
, take instead the bump function
\eta:M\to[0,1]
to be independent of time
\tau
with

\eta\equiv 1\text{ on }\Omega\text{ and }\supp(\eta)\subset\Omega^{\prime}.

and assume
\eta
is suitably smooth so that its derivatives
\nabla^{m}\eta
are bounded by constants that depend only on
n,M,\overline{g},m,\Omega,\Omega^{\prime}
.
Then the same estimate as above (with
\partial_{\tau}\eta=0
) applies to show that

\displaystyle\frac{d}{d\tau}\int\eta^{2}|\nabla^{m}h|^{2}e^{-f}dV+\frac{1}{4}\int\eta^{2}|\nabla^{m+1}h|^{2}e^{-f}dV\leq C\sum_{l=0}^{m}\int_{\Omega^{\prime}}|\nabla^{l}h|^{2}e^{-f}dV

for some constant
C=C(n,M,\overline{g},f,m,\Omega^{\prime},\Omega)

whenever
\|h(\tau)\|_{C^{m+1}(\Omega^{\prime})}<\epsilon.

Integrating from
\tau_{0}
to
\tau\in[\tau_{0},\tau_{2}]
, it follows that

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} &\int_{\Omega}|\nabla^{m}h(\tau)|^{2}e^{-f}+\frac{1}{4}\int_{\tau_{0}}^{\tau}\int_{\Omega}|\nabla^{m+1}h|^{2}e^{-f}dVd\tilde{\tau}\\
\leq{}&\int_{\Omega^{\prime}}|\nabla^{m}h(\tau_{0})|^{2}e^{-f}+C\int_{\tau_{0}}^{\tau}\int_{\Omega^{\prime}}\sum_{l=0}^{m}|\nabla^{l}h|^{2}e^{-f}dV\,d\tilde{\tau}\end{aligned}

whenever
\|h\|_{C^{m+1}(\Omega^{\prime}\times[\tau_{0},\tau_{2}])}<\epsilon
as desired.
∎

Lemma 6.4
.

Let
\Gamma>0
and
\tau_{0}<\tau_{1}
.
Assume

\partial_{\tau}h=\overline{\Delta}_{f}h+2\overline{Rm}[h]+\mathcal{E}_{1}+\mathcal{E}_{2}\qquad\text{ on }M\times(\tau_{0},\tau_{1})

and that
\mathcal{E}_{2}=0
on
\{f<2\Gamma\}\times[\tau_{0},\tau_{1}]
.
Assume also that

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} \|h\|_{L^{\infty}(M\times[\tau_{0},\tau_{1}])}&<\epsilon,\\
\|h(\tau)\|_{L^{2}_{f}(M)}&\leq\mu e^{\lambda_{*}\tau}&&\text{ for all }\tau\in[\tau_{0},\tau_{1}],\text{ and}\\
\sum_{l=0}^{4n+2}|\overline{\nabla}^{l}h|_{\overline{g}}(x,\tau_{0})&\leq C_{0}\overline{p}e^{\lambda_{*}\tau_{0}}&&\text{ for all }x\in\{f<2\Gamma\},\tau=\tau_{0}.\end{aligned}

If

\Gamma\gg 1
is sufficiently large (depending on
n,M,\overline{g},f
),

0<\epsilon\ll 1
is sufficiently small (depending on
n,M,\overline{g},f,\Gamma
),

\tau_{0}\gg 1
is sufficiently large (depending on
n,M,\overline{g},f,\lambda_{*},\Gamma,C_{0}\overline{p}
),
then there exists
C_{1}
(depending on
n,M,\overline{g},f,\lambda_{*},\Gamma
) such that

\sum_{l=0}^{2}|\overline{\nabla}^{l}h|_{\overline{g}}(x,\tau)\leq C_{1}(\mu+C_{0}\overline{p})e^{\lambda_{*}\tau}\quad\text{ for all }(x,\tau)\in\{f<\Gamma\}\times[\tau_{0},\tau_{1}].

Proof.

Consider the domains

\Omega_{i}\doteqdot\left\{x\in M:f(x)<\left(1+\frac{i}{4n+3}\right)\Gamma\right\}\qquad\text{for }i=0,1,\dots,4n+3.

If
\Gamma\gg 1
is sufficiently large depending on
n,M,\overline{g},f
, then

\emptyset\neq\{f<\Gamma\}=\Omega_{0}\Subset\Omega_{1}\Subset\dots\Subset\Omega_{4n+3}=\{f<2\Gamma\}\Subset M.

Note that
\mathcal{E}_{2}\equiv 0
on
\Omega_{4n+3}\times[\tau_{0},\tau_{1}]
by assumption.
Observe that the Sobolev inequality
6
6

6

See
[
Aub98
]
for example.
on
\Omega_{0}=\{f<\Gamma\}
implies

(6.5)

\sup_{x\in\Omega_{0}}\sum_{l=0}^{2}|\overline{\nabla}^{l}h|(x,\tau)\lesssim_{n,M,\overline{g},f,\Gamma}\|h(\tau)\|_{H^{4n}_{f}(\Omega_{0})}\quad\text{for all }\tau\in[\tau_{0},\tau_{1}].

Hence, it suffices to prove

\|h(\tau)\|_{H^{4n}_{f}(\Omega_{0})}\lesssim_{n,M,\overline{g},f,\lambda_{*},\Gamma}(\mu+C_{0}\overline{p})e^{\lambda_{*}\tau}\qquad\text{for all }\tau\in[\tau_{0},\tau_{1}].

There are two cases to consider according to whether or not
\tau\geq\tau_{0}+1
as well.

Consider the first case where
\tau\in[\tau_{0}+1,+\infty)\cap[\tau_{0},\tau_{1}]
.
Then

\|h(\tau)\|_{H^{4n}_{f}(\Omega_{0})}\leq\|h\|_{L^{\infty}H^{4n}_{f}(\Omega_{1}\times[\tau-\frac{1}{4n+3},\tau])}

Observe that if
0<\epsilon\ll 1
is sufficiently small depending on
n
, then Lemma
6.2
implies that for all
i\in\{2,3,\dots,4n+2\}

\displaystyle\|h\|_{C^{4n+3-i}(\Omega_{i}\times[\tau-\frac{i}{4n+3},\tau])}

\displaystyle\leq\|h\|_{C^{4n+1}(\Omega_{4n+2}\times[\tau-\frac{4n+2}{4n+3},\tau])}

\displaystyle\lesssim_{n,M,\overline{g},f,\Gamma}\|h\|_{L^{\infty}(\Omega_{4n+3}\times[\tau-\frac{4n+3}{4n+3},\tau])}

\displaystyle(\text{Lemma \ref{Local Int Est C^0 to C^m_loc}})

\displaystyle\leq\epsilon.

In particular, if
0<\epsilon\ll 1
is sufficiently small depending on
n,M,\overline{g},f,\Gamma
,
then Lemma
6.3
applies repeatedly to show

\displaystyle\|h(\tau)\|_{H^{4n}_{f}(\Omega_{0})}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\|h\|_{L^{\infty}H^{4n}_{f}(\Omega_{1}\times[\tau-\frac{1}{4n+3},\tau])}

\displaystyle\lesssim

{}_{n,M,\overline{g},f,\Gamma}\|h\|_{L^{2}H^{4n}_{f}(\Omega_{2}\times[\tau-\frac{2}{4n+3},\tau])}

\displaystyle(\text{Lemma \ref{Local Int Integral Est+}})

\displaystyle\lesssim

{}_{n,M,\overline{g},f,\Gamma}\|h\|_{L^{2}H^{4n-1}_{f}(\Omega_{3}\times[\tau-\frac{3}{4n+3},\tau])}

\displaystyle(\text{Lemma \ref{Local Int Integral Est+}})

\displaystyle\lesssim

{}_{n,M,\overline{g},f,\Gamma}\|h\|_{L^{2}H^{0}_{f}(\Omega_{4n+2}\times[\tau-\frac{4n+2}{4n+3},\tau])}

\displaystyle(\text{Lemma \ref{Local Int Integral Est+}})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\|h\|_{L^{2}L^{2}_{f}(\Omega_{4n+2}\times[\tau-\frac{4n+2}{4n+3},\tau])}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\|h\|_{L^{\infty}L^{2}_{f}(\Omega_{4n+2}\times[\tau-\frac{4n+2}{4n+3},\tau])}

\displaystyle\left(\frac{4n+2}{4n+3}\leq 1\right)

\displaystyle\lesssim

{}_{\lambda_{*}}\mu e^{\lambda_{*}\tau}.

Now consider the second case where
\tau\in[\tau_{0},\tau_{0}+1]\cap[\tau_{0},\tau_{1}]=[\tau_{0},\min\{\tau_{0}+1,\tau_{1}\}]
. Denote
\tau_{1}^{\prime}\doteqdot\min\{\tau_{0}+1,\tau_{1}\}
.
Note that

\|h\|_{C^{4n+2}(\Omega_{4n+3}\times\{\tau_{0}\})}\leq C_{0}\overline{p}e^{\lambda_{*}\tau_{0}}

by assumption.
Hence, if
0<\epsilon\ll 1
is sufficiently small depending on
n

and
\tau_{0}\gg 1
is sufficiently large depending on
n,\lambda_{*},C_{0}\overline{p}
,
then Lemma
6.2
implies that
for all
i\in\{2,3,\dots,4n+2\}

\displaystyle\|h\|_{C^{4n+3-i}(\Omega_{i}\times[\tau_{0},\tau_{1}^{\prime}])}

\displaystyle\leq\|h\|_{C^{4n+1}(\Omega_{4n+2}\times[\tau_{0},\tau_{1}^{\prime}])}

\displaystyle\lesssim_{n,M,\overline{g},f,\Gamma}\|h\|_{L^{\infty}(\Omega_{4n+3}\times[\tau_{0},\tau_{1}^{\prime}])}+\|h\|_{C^{4n+2}(\Omega_{4n+3}\times\{\tau_{0}\})}

\displaystyle\leq\epsilon+C_{0}\overline{p}e^{\lambda_{*}\tau_{0}}.

In particular, if
0<\epsilon\ll 1
is sufficiently small depending on
n,M,\overline{g},f,\Gamma

and
\tau_{0}\gg 1
is sufficiently large depending on
n,M,\overline{g},f,\lambda_{*},\Gamma,C_{0}\overline{p}

then Lemma
6.3
applies repeatedly to show

\displaystyle\|h(\tau)\|_{H^{4n}_{f}(\Omega_{0})}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\|h\|_{L^{\infty}H^{4n}_{f}(\Omega_{1}\times[\tau_{0},\tau_{1}^{\prime}])}

\displaystyle\lesssim

{}_{n,M,\overline{g},f,\Gamma}\|h(\tau_{0})\|_{H^{4n}_{f}(\Omega_{2})}+\|h\|_{L^{2}H^{4n}_{f}(\Omega_{2}\times[\tau_{0},\tau_{1}^{\prime}])}

\displaystyle(\text{Lemma \ref{Local Int Integral Est+}})

\displaystyle\lesssim

{}_{n,M,\overline{g},f,\Gamma}\|h(\tau_{0})\|_{H^{4n}_{f}(\Omega_{3})}+\|h\|_{L^{2}H^{4n-1}_{f}(\Omega_{3}\times[\tau_{0},\tau_{1}^{\prime}])}

\displaystyle(\text{Lemma \ref{Local Int Integral Est+}})

\displaystyle\lesssim

{}_{n,M,\overline{g},f,\Gamma}\|h(\tau_{0})\|_{H^{4n}_{f}(\Omega_{4n+2})}+\|h\|_{L^{2}H^{0}_{f}(\Omega_{4n+2}\times[\tau_{0},\tau_{1}^{\prime}])}

\displaystyle(\text{Lemma \ref{Local Int Integral Est+}})

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\|h(\tau_{0})\|_{H^{4n}_{f}(\Omega_{4n+2})}+\|h\|_{L^{\infty}L^{2}_{f}(\Omega_{4n+2}\times[\tau_{0},\tau_{1}^{\prime}])}

\displaystyle\lesssim

{}_{n,M,\overline{g},f,\Gamma}C_{0}\overline{p}e^{\lambda_{*}\tau_{0}}+\mu e^{\lambda_{*}\tau_{0}}

\displaystyle\lesssim

{}_{\lambda_{*}}C_{0}\overline{p}e^{\lambda_{*}\tau}+\mu e^{\lambda_{*}\tau}.

∎

6.2.
Large Scale Barrier

Lemma 6.5
.

Assume
h
satisfies (
6.1
) on some spacetime domain
\Omega\times(\tau_{0},\tau_{1})\subset M\times(\tau_{0},\tau_{1})
.
If
0<\epsilon\ll 1
is sufficienly small (depending on
n
), then

|h|_{\overline{g}}^{2}\leq\epsilon\qquad\text{ on }\Omega\times(\tau_{0},\tau_{1})

implies

(6.6)

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} \partial_{\tau}|h|_{\overline{g}}^{2}&\leq g^{ab}\overline{\nabla}_{a}\overline{\nabla}_{b}|h|_{\overline{g}}^{2}-\overline{\nabla}_{\overline{\nabla}f}|h|_{\overline{g}}^{2}+4C_{n}(1+\epsilon)|\overline{Rm}|_{\overline{g}}|h|_{\overline{g}}^{2}+|h|_{\overline{g}}|\mathcal{E}_{2}|_{\overline{g}}\\
&\leq(1+C_{n}|h|_{\overline{g}})\overline{\Delta}|h|_{\overline{g}}^{2}-\overline{\nabla}_{\overline{\nabla}f}|h|_{\overline{g}}^{2}+4C_{n}(1+\epsilon)|\overline{Rm}|_{\overline{g}}|h|_{\overline{g}}^{2}+|h|_{\overline{g}}|\mathcal{E}_{2}|_{\overline{g}}\end{aligned}

on
\Omega\times(\tau_{0},\tau_{1})
.

Proof.

If
0<\epsilon\ll 1
is sufficiently small depending on
n
, then

|h|_{\overline{g}}^{2}\leq\epsilon
implies
g=\overline{g}+h
and
\overline{g}
are comparable.
Moreover, (
4.13
) implies

(6.7)

|\hat{h}|_{\overline{g}}\lesssim_{n}|h|_{\overline{g}}\qquad\text{ and }\qquad|\tilde{h}|_{\overline{g}}\lesssim_{n}|h|_{\overline{g}}^{2}

when
0<\epsilon\ll 1
is sufficiently small depending on
n
.

We compute the evolution equation for
|h|^{2}_{\overline{g}}
using (
6.1
):

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} &\partial_{\tau}|h|^{2}\\
={}&2\langle h,\partial_{\tau}h\rangle_{\overline{g}}\\
={}&2\overline{g}^{ip}\overline{g}^{jq}h_{pq}g^{ab}\overline{\nabla}_{a}\overline{\nabla}_{b}h_{ij}-2\langle h,\overline{\nabla}_{\overline{\nabla}f}h\rangle_{\overline{g}}+4\overline{R}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.68811pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.2058pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{k}\mathchoice{\makebox[3.71356pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.71356pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.29834pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.64166pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{l}}}_{{{i}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.42017pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.42017pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.7052pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.93228pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{j}\mathchoice{\makebox[2.56946pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[2.56946pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.55847pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.11319pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}h_{kl}h_{pq}\overline{g}^{ip}\overline{g}^{jq}\\
&\qquad+2\overline{R}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[8.05121pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[8.05121pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[4.88843pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.49171pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{p}\mathchoice{\makebox[3.51666pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.51666pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.1029pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.50208pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{ja}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.12234pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.12234pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.46532pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.76094pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{b}}}\left(\overline{g}_{ip}\tilde{h}^{ab}+\hat{h}^{ab}h_{ip}\right)h_{pq}\overline{g}^{ip}\overline{g}^{jq}+2\overline{R}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[7.16693pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[7.16693pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[4.2782pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.05585pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{p}\mathchoice{\makebox[3.51666pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.51666pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.1029pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.50208pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{ia}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.12234pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.12234pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.46532pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.76094pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{b}}}\left(\overline{g}_{jp}\tilde{h}^{ab}+\hat{h}^{ab}h_{jp}\right)h_{pq}\overline{g}^{ip}\overline{g}^{jq}\\
&\qquad+2g^{ab}g^{pq}(\overline{\nabla}h*\overline{\nabla}h)_{abpqij}h_{pq}\overline{g}^{ip}\overline{g}^{jq}+\langle h,\mathcal{E}_{2}\rangle_{\overline{g}}\\
={}&g^{ab}\overline{\nabla}_{a}\overline{\nabla}_{b}|h|_{\overline{g}}^{2}-2g^{ab}\langle\overline{\nabla}_{a}h,\overline{\nabla}_{b}h\rangle_{\overline{g}}-\overline{\nabla}_{\overline{\nabla}f}|h|^{2}_{\overline{g}}+4\overline{R}^{pkql}h_{kl}h_{pq}\\
&\qquad+2\overline{R}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[8.05121pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[8.05121pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[4.88843pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.49171pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{p}\mathchoice{\makebox[3.51666pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.51666pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.1029pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.50208pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{ja}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.12234pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.12234pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.46532pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.76094pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{b}}}\left(\overline{g}_{ip}\tilde{h}^{ab}+\hat{h}^{ab}h_{ip}\right)h_{pq}\overline{g}^{ip}\overline{g}^{jq}+2\overline{R}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[7.16693pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[7.16693pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[4.2782pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.05585pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{p}\mathchoice{\makebox[3.51666pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.51666pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.1029pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.50208pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{ia}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.12234pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.12234pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.46532pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.76094pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{b}}}\left(\overline{g}_{jp}\tilde{h}^{ab}+\hat{h}^{ab}h_{jp}\right)h_{pq}\overline{g}^{ip}\overline{g}^{jq}\\
&\qquad+2g^{ab}g^{pq}(\overline{\nabla}h*\overline{\nabla}h)_{abpqij}h_{pq}\overline{g}^{ip}\overline{g}^{jq}+\langle h,\mathcal{E}_{2}\rangle_{\overline{g}}.\end{aligned}

Invoking (
6.7
), it follows that

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} &\partial_{\tau}|h|^{2}\\
\leq{}&g^{ab}\overline{\nabla}_{a}\overline{\nabla}_{b}|h|_{\overline{g}}^{2}-2(1-C_{n}\epsilon)|\overline{\nabla}h|_{\overline{g}}^{2}-\overline{\nabla}_{\overline{\nabla}f}|h|^{2}_{\overline{g}}+4C_{n}|\overline{Rm}|_{\overline{g}}|h|_{\overline{g}}^{2}+4C_{n}|\overline{Rm}|_{\overline{g}}|h|_{\overline{g}}^{3}\\
&\qquad+C_{n}|h|_{\overline{g}}|\overline{\nabla}h|_{\overline{g}}^{2}+|h|_{\overline{g}}|\mathcal{E}_{2}|_{\overline{g}}\\
={}&g^{ab}\overline{\nabla}_{a}\overline{\nabla}_{b}|h|_{\overline{g}}^{2}-2(1-2C_{n}\epsilon)|\overline{\nabla}h|_{\overline{g}}^{2}-\overline{\nabla}_{\overline{\nabla}f}|h|^{2}_{\overline{g}}+4C_{n}(1+\epsilon)|\overline{Rm}|_{\overline{g}}|h|_{\overline{g}}^{2}+|h|_{\overline{g}}|\mathcal{E}_{2}|_{\overline{g}}.\end{aligned}

The first inequality in (
6.6
) follows from assuming
\epsilon
is sufficiently small so that
1-2C_{n}\epsilon>0
.
(
6.7
) then implies the second inequality in (
6.6
).
∎

Lemma 6.6
.

For
A,B,\kappa>0
, let

u\doteqdot e^{-\kappa\tau}\left(Af^{\kappa}-Bf^{\kappa-1}\right).

Then
u
is a supersolution of

\partial_{\tau}u\geq(1+C_{n}\sqrt{u})\overline{\Delta}u-\overline{\nabla}_{\overline{\nabla}f}u+4C_{n}(1+\epsilon)|\overline{Rm}|_{\overline{g}}u

\text{ on the region }\{(x,\tau)\in M\times[\tau_{0},\infty):\Gamma<f(x)<\gamma e^{\tau}\}

if

B-A\left\{\kappa\left(\kappa+\frac{n}{2}\right)+4C_{n}(1+\epsilon)\left(\sup_{M}|\overline{Rm}|_{\overline{g}}f\right)\right\}\geq\omega>0,

\Gamma\gg 1\text{ is sufficiently large depending on }n,B,\kappa,\omega\text{ and }

0<\gamma\ll 1\text{ is sufficiently small depending on }n,M,\overline{g},A,B,\kappa,\omega.

Additionally,

u>Ce^{-\kappa\tau}f^{\kappa}\quad\text{on }\{f>\Gamma\}\qquad\text{if }C+\frac{B}{\Gamma}\leq A.

In particular,
u>Ce^{-\kappa\tau}f^{\kappa}
throughout
\{f>\Gamma\}

if
A>C
and
\Gamma
is sufficiently large (depending on
A,B,C
).

Proof.

Throughout this proof, we omit overlines. Recall by Proposition
2.3
that
\Delta_{f}f=\frac{n}{2}-f
.
It follows that

\nabla f^{\kappa}=\kappa f^{\kappa-1}\nabla f,

\nabla_{i}\nabla_{j}f^{\kappa}=\kappa f^{\kappa-1}\nabla_{i}\nabla_{j}f+\kappa(\kappa-1)f^{\kappa-2}\nabla_{i}f\nabla_{j}f,

\displaystyle\Delta f^{\kappa}

\displaystyle=\kappa f^{\kappa-1}\Delta f+\kappa(\kappa-1)f^{\kappa-2}|\nabla f|^{2}

\displaystyle=\frac{n}{2}\kappa f^{\kappa-1}-R\kappa f^{\kappa-1}+\kappa(\kappa-1)f^{\kappa-1}\frac{|\nabla f|^{2}}{f},

and so

\displaystyle\Delta_{f}f^{\kappa}

\displaystyle=\kappa f^{\kappa-1}\Delta f+\kappa(\kappa-1)f^{\kappa-2}|\nabla f|^{2}-\kappa f^{\kappa-1}|\nabla f|^{2}

\displaystyle=\kappa f^{\kappa-1}\Delta_{f}f+\kappa(\kappa-1)f^{\kappa-2}|\nabla f|^{2}

\displaystyle=\frac{n}{2}\kappa f^{\kappa-1}-\kappa f^{\kappa}+\kappa(\kappa-1)f^{\kappa-1}\frac{|\nabla f|^{2}}{f}.

In particular,

(6.8)

\Delta_{f}u=-\kappa u+e^{-\kappa\tau}f^{\kappa-1}\left[A\frac{n}{2}\kappa-B+A\kappa(\kappa-1)\frac{|\nabla f|^{2}}{f}\right]\\
-Be^{-\kappa\tau}f^{\kappa-2}\left[\frac{n}{2}(\kappa-1)+(\kappa-1)(\kappa-2)\frac{|\nabla f|^{2}}{f}\right].

A straightforward computation reveals

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} &\quad\partial_{\tau}u-(1+C_{n}\sqrt{u})\Delta u+\nabla_{\nabla f}u-4C_{n}(1+\epsilon)|Rm|u\\
&=\partial_{\tau}u-\Delta_{f}u-4C_{n}(1+\epsilon)|Rm|u-C_{n}\sqrt{u}\Delta u\\
&=-\kappa u+\kappa u+e^{-\kappa\tau}f^{\kappa-1}\left[B-A\kappa\frac{n}{2}-A\kappa(\kappa-1)\frac{|\nabla f|^{2}}{f}\right]\\
&\qquad+Be^{-\kappa\tau}f^{\kappa-2}\left[\frac{n}{2}(\kappa-1)+(\kappa-1)(\kappa-2)\frac{|\nabla f|^{2}}{f}\right]\\
&\qquad-4C_{n}(1+\epsilon)|Rm|e^{-\kappa\tau}(Af^{\kappa}-Bf^{\kappa-1})\\
&\qquad-C_{n}\sqrt{u}\Delta u\\
&\geq e^{-\kappa\tau}f^{\kappa-1}\left[B-A\kappa\frac{n}{2}-A\kappa(\kappa-1)\frac{|\nabla f|^{2}}{f}\right]\\
&\qquad+Be^{-\kappa\tau}f^{\kappa-2}\left[\frac{n}{2}(\kappa-1)+(\kappa-1)(\kappa-2)\frac{|\nabla f|^{2}}{f}\right]\\
&\qquad-4C_{n}(1+\epsilon)|Rm|e^{-\kappa\tau}Af^{\kappa}\\
&\qquad-C_{n}\sqrt{u}Ae^{-\kappa\tau}f^{\kappa-1}\left[\frac{n}{2}\kappa-R\kappa+\kappa(\kappa-1)\frac{|\nabla f|^{2}}{f}\right]\\
&\qquad+C_{n}\sqrt{u}Be^{-\kappa\tau}f^{\kappa-2}\left[\frac{n}{2}(\kappa-1)-R(\kappa-1)+(\kappa-1)(\kappa-2)\frac{|\nabla f|^{2}}{f}\right].\end{aligned}

Next, note that

0\leq\frac{|\nabla f|^{2}}{f}\leq 1

by (
2.1
) and Lemma
2.30
.
Moreover, by the definition of
u
,

u\leq Ae^{-\kappa\tau}f^{\kappa}.

These inequalities imply

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} &\partial_{\tau}u-(1+C_{n}\sqrt{u})\Delta u+\nabla_{\nabla f}u-4C_{n}(1+\epsilon)|Rm|u\\
\geq{}&e^{-\kappa\tau}f^{\kappa-1}\left[B-A\kappa\frac{n}{2}-A\kappa^{2}\right]\\
&+Be^{-\kappa\tau}f^{\kappa-2}\left[-\frac{n}{2}-3\kappa\right]\\
&-4C_{n}(1+\epsilon)(\sup_{M}f|Rm|)Ae^{-\kappa\tau}f^{\kappa-1}\\
&+C_{n}\sqrt{A}e^{-\kappa\tau/2}f^{\kappa/2}Ae^{-\kappa\tau}f^{\kappa-1}\left[-\frac{n}{2}\kappa-\kappa^{2}\right]\\
&+C_{n}\sqrt{A}e^{-\kappa\tau/2}f^{\kappa/2}Be^{-\kappa\tau}f^{\kappa-2}\left[-\frac{n}{2}-R\kappa-3\kappa\right]\\
\geq{}&e^{-\kappa\tau}f^{\kappa-1}\left[B-A\kappa\frac{n}{2}-A\kappa^{2}-4C_{n}(1+\epsilon)(\sup_{M}f|Rm|)A\right]\\
&-e^{-\kappa\tau}f^{\kappa-1}\frac{B}{\Gamma}\left[\frac{n}{2}+3\kappa\right]-e^{-\kappa\tau}f^{\kappa-1}\gamma^{\kappa/2}C_{n}A^{3/2}\left[\frac{n}{2}\kappa+\kappa^{2}\right]\\
&-e^{-\kappa\tau}f^{\kappa-1}\gamma^{\kappa/2}\frac{B}{\Gamma}C_{n}\sqrt{A}\left[\frac{n}{2}+R\kappa+3\kappa\right].\end{aligned}

This quantity is positive if

B-A\left\{\kappa\left(\kappa+\frac{n}{2}\right)+4C_{n}(1+\epsilon)\left(\sup_{M}|Rm|_{g}f\right)\right\}\geq\omega>0,

\Gamma\gg 1
is sufficiently large depending on
n,B,\kappa,\omega
,
and
0<\gamma\ll 1
is sufficiently small depending on
n,M,\overline{g},A,B,\kappa,\omega
.

The final two statements of the lemma follow from straightforward estimates using the assumption that
f>\Gamma
.
∎

Lemma 6.7
.

For
a,B>0
, let

u\doteqdot a-\frac{B}{f}.

Then
u
is a supersolution of

\partial_{\tau}u\geq(1+C_{n}\sqrt{u})\overline{\Delta}u-\overline{\nabla}_{X}u+4C_{n}(1+\epsilon)|\overline{Rm}|_{\overline{g}}u+\frac{C_{0}}{\Gamma}e^{-\tau}\sqrt{u}

\text{ on the region }\{(x,\tau)\in M\times[\tau_{0},\infty):\gamma e^{\tau}<f(x)<\Gamma e^{\tau}\}

if

B>4C_{n}(1+\epsilon)(\sup f|Rm|)a+C_{0}\sqrt{a}

and
\tau_{0}\gg 1
is sufficiently large (depending on
n,M,\overline{g},f,C_{0},\epsilon,\gamma,a,B
).

Additionally,

u>c\text{ on }\{f>\gamma e^{\tau}\geq\gamma e^{\tau_{0}}\}\qquad\text{if }\frac{B}{\gamma}e^{-\tau_{0}}<a-c.

In particular, for any
a>c
,
u>c
throughout
\{f>\gamma e^{\tau}>\gamma e^{\tau_{0}}\}
if
\tau_{0}\gg 1
is sufficiently large (depending on
a,B,c,\gamma
).

Proof.

We omit overlines throughout this proof.
By mimicking the computations in the previous proof with
\kappa=0
, it follows that

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} &\Delta_{f}u+4C_{n}(1+\epsilon)|Rm|u+C_{n}\sqrt{u}\Delta u+\frac{C_{0}}{\Gamma}e^{-\tau}\sqrt{u}\\
={}&\frac{Bn}{2}f^{-2}-Bf^{-1}-2Bf^{-2}\frac{|\nabla f|^{2}}{f}\\
&+4(1+\epsilon)C_{n}|Rm|a-4C_{n}(1+\epsilon)|Rm|\frac{B}{f}\\
&-C_{n}\sqrt{a-B/f}B\left\{-\frac{n}{2}f^{-2}+Rf^{-2}+2f^{-2}\frac{|\nabla f|^{2}}{f}\right\}\\
&+\frac{C_{0}}{\Gamma}e^{-\tau}\sqrt{a-B/f}\\
\leq{}&\frac{Bn}{2}f^{-2}-Bf^{-1}+4C_{n}(1+\epsilon)|Rm|a+BC_{n}\frac{n}{2}f^{-2}\sqrt{a}+\frac{C_{0}}{\Gamma}e^{-\tau}\sqrt{a}\\
\leq{}&f^{-1}\left[-B+4C_{n}(1+\epsilon)\left(\sup f|Rm|\right)a\right]+f^{-2}\left[\frac{Bn}{2}+BC_{n}\frac{n}{2}\sqrt{a}\right]+C_{0}f^{-1}\sqrt{a}\\
\leq{}&f^{-1}\left[-B+4C_{n}(1+\epsilon)\left(\sup f|Rm|\right)a+C_{0}\sqrt{a}\right]+\frac{1}{\gamma e^{\tau_{0}}}f^{-1}\left[\frac{Bn}{2}+BC_{n}\frac{n}{2}\sqrt{a}\right].\end{aligned}

This quantity can be made negative by taking
B>4C_{n}(1+\epsilon)(\sup f|Rm|)a+C_{0}\sqrt{a}
and
\tau_{0}\gg 1
sufficiently large depending on
n,M,\overline{g},f,C_{0},\epsilon,\gamma,a,B
.

The final two statements of the lemma follow from straightforward estimates using the assumption that
f>\gamma e^{\tau_{0}}
.
∎

6.3.
Large Scale Interior Estimates

The barriers from the previous subsection provide
C^{0}
control of
h
at large scales.
In this subsection, we prove interior estimates that permit us to bootstrap these
C^{0}
bounds up to
C^{2}
bounds on a smaller domain so long as the initial data is suitably smooth.
These interior estimates are based on interior estimates from Appendix
C
.

Lemma 6.8
.

Assume

\partial_{\tau}h=\overline{\Delta}_{f}h+2\overline{Rm}[h]+\mathcal{E}_{1}\qquad\text{on }\{(x,\tau)\in M\times(\tau_{0},\tau_{1}):\Gamma<f(x)<\gamma e^{\tau}\}.

Assume also that

|h|_{\overline{g}}\leq C_{0}e^{-\kappa\tau}f^{\kappa}\qquad\text{on }\{(x,\tau)\in M\times[\tau_{0},\tau_{1}]:\Gamma\leq f(x)\leq\gamma e^{\tau}\}

and

\sum_{m=1}^{3}|\overline{\nabla}^{m}h|_{\overline{g}}\leq C_{0}e^{-\kappa\tau_{0}}f^{\kappa}\qquad\text{on }\{x\in M:\Gamma\leq f(x)\leq\gamma e^{\tau_{0}}\}.

If
\Gamma\gg 1
is sufficiently large (depending on
n,M,\overline{g},f
) and
C_{0}\gamma^{\kappa}\ll 1
is sufficiently small (depending on
n,M,\overline{g},f
) then

|\overline{\nabla}^{m}h|_{\overline{g}}+|\overline{\nabla}^{m}h|_{\overline{g}}\lesssim_{n,M,\overline{g},f,\kappa}C_{0}e^{-\kappa\tau}f^{\kappa}\\
\text{on }\{(x,\tau)\in M\times[\tau_{0},\tau_{1}]:4\Gamma\leq f(x)\leq\frac{1}{4}\gamma e^{\tau}\}.

Proof.

The unbounded coefficient
\overline{\nabla}f
of the drift term
\overline{\nabla}_{\overline{\nabla}f}h
precludes us from applying Proposition
C.3
or Proposition
C.4
directly.
To circumvent this issue, we appeal to a rescaling argument.

Fix
\tau_{*}\in(\tau_{0},\tau_{1}]
.
Consider the family
\mathcal{H}(s)
of symmetric 2-tensors on
M
given by

\mathcal{H}(s)\doteqdot(1-s)\varphi_{s}^{*}\left[h(\tau_{*}-\ln(1-s))\right]

where
\varphi_{s}:M\to M
is defined as the family of diffeomorphisms solving (
2.2
), that is

\partial_{s}\varphi_{s}=\frac{1}{1-s}\overline{\nabla}f\circ\varphi_{s}\qquad\text{ with }\qquad\varphi_{0}=Id_{M}:M\to M.

Then
\mathcal{H}(s)
satisfies the evolution equation

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} &\quad\partial_{s}\mathcal{H}\\
&=-\frac{1}{1-s}\mathcal{H}+(1-s)\frac{1}{1-s}\varphi_{s}^{*}\partial_{\tau}h+(1-s)\varphi_{s}^{*}\mathcal{L}_{\partial_{s}\varphi_{s}\circ\varphi_{s}^{-1}}h\\
&=-\frac{1}{1-s}\mathcal{H}+\varphi_{s}^{*}\partial_{\tau}h+\varphi_{s}^{*}\mathcal{L}_{\overline{\nabla}f}h\\
&=-\frac{1}{1-s}\mathcal{H}+\varphi_{s}^{*}\left\{g^{ab}\overline{\nabla}_{a}\overline{\nabla}_{b}h-\overline{\nabla}_{\overline{\nabla}f}h+2\overline{Rm}[h]\right.\\
&\qquad+\overline{Rm}_{jab}^{p}\left(\overline{g}_{ip}\tilde{h}^{ab}+\hat{h}^{ab}h_{ip}\right)+\overline{Rm}_{iab}^{p}\left(\overline{g}_{jp}\tilde{h}^{ab}+\hat{h}^{ab}h_{jp}\right)\\
&\qquad+g^{ab}g^{pq}(\overline{\nabla}h*\overline{\nabla}h)_{abpqij}\\
&\qquad\left.+\overline{\nabla}_{\overline{\nabla}f}h+h_{ip}\overline{\nabla}_{j}\overline{\nabla}^{p}f+h_{pj}\overline{\nabla}_{i}\overline{\nabla}^{p}f\right\}.\end{aligned}

Let
\check{g}\doteqdot(1-s)\varphi_{s}^{*}\overline{g}
. Then

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} &\quad\partial_{s}\mathcal{H}\\
&=((1-s)\varphi_{s}^{*}g)^{ab}\check{\nabla}_{a}\check{\nabla}_{b}\mathcal{H}+2\check{Rm}[\mathcal{H}]+\check{Rc}*\mathcal{H}\\
&\qquad+((1-s)\varphi_{s}^{*}g)^{ab}((1-s)\varphi_{s}^{*}g)^{pq}(\check{\nabla}\mathcal{H}*\check{\nabla}\mathcal{H})_{abpqij}\\
&\qquad+\frac{1}{1-s}\check{Rm}_{jab}^{p}\check{g}_{ip}(\varphi_{s}^{*}\tilde{h})^{ab}+\frac{1}{1-s}\check{Rm}_{jab}^{p}(\varphi_{s}^{*}\hat{h})^{ab}\mathcal{H}_{ip}\\
&\qquad+\frac{1}{1-s}\check{Rm}_{iab}^{p}\check{g}_{jp}(\varphi_{s}^{*}\tilde{h})^{ab}+\frac{1}{1-s}\check{Rm}_{iab}^{p}(\varphi_{s}^{*}\hat{h})^{ab}\mathcal{H}_{jp}\\
&=((1-s)\varphi_{s}^{*}g)^{ab}\check{\nabla}_{a}\check{\nabla}_{b}\mathcal{H}+2\check{Rm}[\mathcal{H}]+\check{Rc}*\mathcal{H}\\
&\qquad+((1-s)\varphi_{s}^{*}g)^{ab}((1-s)\varphi_{s}^{*}g)^{pq}(\check{\nabla}\mathcal{H}*\check{\nabla}\mathcal{H})_{abpqij}\\
&\qquad+\frac{1}{1-s}\check{Rm}_{jab}^{p}\check{g}_{ip}(\varphi_{s}^{*}\tilde{h})^{ab}+\frac{1}{1-s}\check{Rm}_{jab}^{p}(\varphi_{s}^{*}\hat{h})^{ab}\mathcal{H}_{ip}\\
&\qquad+\frac{1}{1-s}\check{Rm}_{iab}^{p}\check{g}_{jp}(\varphi_{s}^{*}\tilde{h})^{ab}+\frac{1}{1-s}\check{Rm}_{iab}^{p}(\varphi_{s}^{*}\hat{h})^{ab}\mathcal{H}_{jp}.\end{aligned}

From the definition of
\tilde{h}
and
\hat{h}
in (
4.13
) as

g^{ab}=\overline{g}^{ab}-\hat{h}^{ab}\qquad\text{ and }\qquad\tilde{h}^{ab}=\hat{h}^{ab}-\overline{g}^{ak}\overline{g}^{bl}h_{kl},

it follows that

\frac{1}{1-s}(\varphi_{s}^{*}\tilde{h})^{ab}=\tilde{\mathcal{H}}^{ab}\qquad\text{ and }\qquad\frac{1}{1-s}(\varphi_{s}^{*}\hat{h})^{ab}=\hat{\mathcal{H}}^{ab}

where
\tilde{\mathcal{H}},\hat{\mathcal{H}}
are defined by

((1-s)\varphi_{s}^{*}g)^{ab}=\check{g}^{ab}-\hat{\mathcal{H}}^{ab}\qquad\text{ and }\qquad\tilde{\mathcal{H}}^{ab}=\hat{\mathcal{H}}^{ab}-\check{g}^{ak}\check{g}^{bl}\mathcal{H}_{kl}.

Note also that
\check{g}(s)=(1-s)\varphi_{s}^{*}\overline{g}
is the Ricci flow solution corresponding to the soliton metric
\check{g}(0)=\overline{g}

and thus remains smooth for all
s\in(-\infty,1)
.
Additionally,

(1-s)\varphi_{s}^{*}g=(1-s)\varphi_{s}^{*}\overline{g}+(1-s)\varphi_{s}^{*}h=\check{g}(s)+\mathcal{H}(s).

Therefore, the evolution equation for
\mathcal{H}
may be written as

(6.9)

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} \partial_{s}\mathcal{H}={}&\check{\Delta}\mathcal{H}+2\check{Rm}[\mathcal{H}]+\check{Rc}*\mathcal{H}\\
&-\hat{\mathcal{H}}^{ab}\check{\nabla}_{a}\check{\nabla}_{b}\mathcal{H}+(\check{g}-\hat{\mathcal{H}})^{ab}(\check{g}-\hat{\mathcal{H}})^{pq}(\check{\nabla}\mathcal{H}*\check{\nabla}\mathcal{H})_{abpqij}\\
&+\check{Rm}_{jab}^{p}\check{g}_{ip}\tilde{\mathcal{H}}^{ab}+\check{Rm}_{jab}^{p}\hat{\mathcal{H}}^{ab}\mathcal{H}_{ip}+\check{Rm}_{iab}^{p}\check{g}_{jp}\tilde{\mathcal{H}}^{ab}+\check{Rm}_{iab}^{p}\hat{\mathcal{H}}^{ab}\mathcal{H}_{jp}.\end{aligned}

Let
s_{0}=s_{0}(\tau_{*},\tau_{0})<0
be defined so that

\tau_{*}-\ln(1-s_{0})=\tau_{0}

and set
s_{0}^{\prime}\doteqdot\max\{s_{0},-1\}
.
Recall from Lemma
2.33
that

0\leq(1-s)\partial_{s}\varphi_{t}^{*}f\leq\varphi_{s}^{*}f\qquad\text{for all }s<1.

Integrating this derivative estimate from
s\leq 0
to
0
yields

\frac{1}{1-s}f(x)\leq f(\varphi_{s}(x))\leq f(x)\qquad\text{ for all }s\leq 0.

It follows that if

(x,s)\in\left\{2\Gamma<f(x)<\frac{1}{2}\gamma e^{\tau_{*}}\right\}\times[s_{0}^{\prime},0]

then

\displaystyle f(\varphi_{s}(x))\geq\frac{1}{1-s}f(x)>\frac{2}{1-s}\Gamma\geq\Gamma\qquad\text{and }

\displaystyle f(\varphi_{s}(x))\leq f(x)<\frac{1}{2}\gamma e^{\tau_{*}}\leq\gamma e^{\tau_{*}-\ln(1-s)}.

In other words,

\displaystyle(x,s)\in\left\{2\Gamma<f(x)<\frac{1}{2}\gamma e^{\tau_{*}}\right\}\times[s_{0}^{\prime},0]\quad\text{ implies}

\displaystyle(\varphi_{s}(x),\tau_{*}-\ln(1-s))\in\{(y,\tau)\in M\times\mathbb{R}:\Gamma<f(y)<\gamma e^{\tau}\},

the region where the assumed bounds on
h
apply.
Therefore,

|\mathcal{H}|_{\check{g}}(x,s)=\left|h\right|_{\overline{g}}(\varphi_{s}(x),\tau_{*}-\ln(1-s))\leq C_{0}e^{\kappa(\tau_{*}-\ln(1-s))}f(\varphi_{s}(x))^{\kappa}\leq C_{0}\gamma^{\kappa}

for all
(x,s)\in\{2\Gamma<f(x)<\frac{1}{2}\gamma e^{\tau_{*}}\}\times[s_{0}^{\prime},0]
.

Since
f
grows quadratically at infinity, we may assume
\Gamma\gg 1
is sufficiently large depending on
n,M,\overline{g},f
so that, for any
p\in M
,

f(p)>\Gamma\implies B_{\overline{g}}(p,1)\subset\left\{\frac{1}{2}f(p)<f<2f(p)\right\}.

Thus, the radius 1 neighborhood of
\{4\Gamma<f<\frac{1}{4}\gamma e^{\tau_{*}}\}
with respect to
\overline{g}
is contained in
\{2\Gamma<f<\frac{1}{2}\gamma e^{\tau_{*}}\}
, that is

\bigcup_{x\in\{4\Gamma<f<\frac{1}{4}\gamma e^{\tau_{*}}\}}B_{\overline{g}}(x,1)\subset\left\{2\Gamma<f<\frac{1}{2}\gamma e^{\tau_{*}}\right\}.

Since
\overline{Rm}
decays quadratically at infinity, we may additionally assume
\Gamma\gg 1
is sufficiently large depending on
n,M,\overline{g},f
so that, for all
p\in M
with
f(p)>\Gamma
,
B_{\overline{g}}(p,1)
contains no points conjugate to
p
.

Let
p_{*}\in M
such that
4\Gamma<f(p_{*})<\frac{1}{4}\gamma e^{\tau_{*}}
.
After possibly passing to a local cover and pulling back to
\mathbb{R}^{n}
using the exponential map based at
p_{*}
,
Proposition
C.3
or Proposition
C.4
applies to equation (
6.9
) on the unit ball in
\mathbb{R}^{n}
.
Note that the terms in the equation (
6.9
) are bounded independently of
p_{*}
and
\tau_{*}
,
so the resulting constants will depend only on
n,M,\overline{g},f
.
There are two possible cases to consider depending on whether or not
s_{0}\geq-1
.

In the first case where
s_{0}\geq-1
,
Proposition
C.4
with
F=0
implies
that if
C_{0}\gamma^{\kappa}\ll 1
is sufficiently small depending on
n,M,\overline{g},f
,
there exists
C_{1}=C_{1}(n,M,\overline{g},f)
such that

\displaystyle|\overline{\nabla}h|_{\overline{g}}(p_{*},\tau_{*})+|\overline{\nabla}^{2}h|_{\overline{g}}(p_{*},\tau_{*})

\displaystyle={}

\displaystyle|\check{\nabla}\mathcal{H}|_{\check{g}}(p_{*},s=0)+|\check{\nabla}^{2}\mathcal{H}|_{\check{g}}(p_{*},s=0)

\displaystyle\leq{}

\displaystyle C_{1}\left(\sup_{B_{\overline{g}}(p_{*},r_{0})\times[s_{0},0]}|\mathcal{H}|(x,s)+\sup_{B_{\overline{g}}(p_{*},r_{0})}\sum_{m=1}^{3}|\check{\nabla}^{m}\mathcal{H}|(x,s_{0})\right)

\displaystyle\leq{}

\displaystyle C_{1}\left(\sup_{\{\frac{1}{2}f(p_{*})<f<2f(p_{*})\}\times[s_{0},0]}|\mathcal{H}|(x,s)+\sup_{\{\frac{1}{2}f(p_{*})<f<2f(p_{*})\}}\sum_{m=1}^{3}|\check{\nabla}^{m}\mathcal{H}|(x,s_{0})\right)

\displaystyle\leq{}

\displaystyle C_{1}\left(\sup_{\{\frac{1}{4}f(p_{*})<f<4f(p_{*})\}\times[\tau_{0},\tau_{*}]}|h|_{\overline{g}}(x,s)+\sup_{\{\frac{1}{4}f(p_{*})<f<4f(p_{*})\}}\sum_{m=1}^{3}\frac{1}{(1-s_{0})^{m/2}}|\overline{\nabla}^{m}h|_{\overline{g}}(x,\tau_{0})\right)

\displaystyle\leq{}

\displaystyle 4C_{1}\sup_{\{\frac{1}{4}f(p_{*})<f<4f(p_{*})\}\times[\tau_{0},\tau_{*}]}C_{0}e^{-\kappa\tau}f^{\kappa}

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle{}_{\kappa}C_{1}C_{0}e^{-\kappa\tau_{*}}f(p_{*})^{\kappa}

where in the last line we used the fact that
s_{0}\geq-1
implies
\tau_{*}-\tau_{0}=\ln(1-s_{0})\leq\ln(2)
.

In the second case where
s_{0}<-1
, Proposition
C.3
with
F=0
implies
that if
C_{0}\gamma^{\kappa}\ll 1
is sufficiently small depending on
n,M,\overline{g},f
,
there exists
C_{1}=C_{1}(n,M,\overline{g},f)
such that

|\check{\nabla}\mathcal{H}|_{\check{g}}(p_{*},s=0)+|\check{\nabla}^{2}\mathcal{H}|_{\check{g}}(p_{*},s=0)\leq C_{2}\sup_{B_{\overline{g}}(p_{*},r_{0})\times[-r_{0}^{2},0]}|\mathcal{H}|(x,s)

The remainder of the proof of the second case follows by similar logic as in the proof of the first case.

In both cases,

|\overline{\nabla}h|_{\overline{g}}(p_{*},\tau_{*})+|\overline{\nabla}^{2}h|_{\overline{g}}(p_{*},\tau_{*})\lesssim_{n,M,\overline{g},f,\kappa}C_{0}e^{-\kappa\tau_{*}}f(p_{*})^{\kappa}

when
C_{0}\gamma^{\kappa}\ll 1
is sufficiently small depending on
n,M,\overline{g},f
.
Since
\tau_{*}\in(\tau_{0},\tau_{1}]
and
p_{*}\in\{4\Gamma<f<\frac{1}{4}\gamma e^{\tau_{*}}\}
were arbitrary and the constants depend only on
n,M,\overline{g},f,\kappa
, the statement of the lemma follows.
∎

Lemma 6.9
.

Assume

\partial_{\tau}h=\overline{\Delta}_{f}h+2\overline{Rm}[h]+\mathcal{E}_{1}+\mathcal{E}_{2}\qquad\text{ on }M\times(\tau_{0},\tau_{1}).

Assume also that

|h|_{\overline{g}}(x,\tau)\leq\delta\qquad\text{ on }\{(x,\tau)\in M\times[\tau_{0},\tau_{1}]:\gamma e^{\tau}<f(x)\},

\sum_{m=1}^{3}|\overline{\nabla}^{m}h|_{\overline{g}}(x,\tau_{0})\leq\delta\qquad\text{ on }\{x\in M:\gamma e^{\tau_{0}}\leq f(x)\},

and

|\mathcal{E}_{2}|_{\overline{g}}(x,\tau)+|\overline{\nabla}\mathcal{E}_{2}|_{\overline{g}}(x,\tau)\leq\delta\qquad\text{ on }\{(x,\tau)\in M\times[\tau_{0},\tau_{1}]:\gamma e^{\tau}<f(x)\}.

If
\delta\ll 1
is sufficiently small (depending on
n,M,\overline{g},f
)
and
\tau_{0}\gg 1
is sufficiently large (depending on
n,M,\overline{g},f,\gamma
),
then

|\overline{\nabla}h|_{\overline{g}}(x,\tau)+|\overline{\nabla}^{2}h|_{\overline{g}}(x,\tau)\lesssim_{n,M,\overline{g},f}\delta\qquad\text{ on }\{(x,\tau)\in M\times[\tau_{0},\tau_{1}]:4\gamma e^{\tau}<f(x)\}.

Proof.

The proof is analogous to that of Lemma
6.8
.
We outline the argument nonetheless.

Fix
\tau_{*}\in(\tau_{0},\tau_{1}]

and consider

\mathcal{H}(s)\doteqdot(1-s)\varphi_{s}^{*}h(\tau_{*}-\ln(1-s)).

Then
\mathcal{H}(s)
satisfies the evolution equation

(6.10)

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} \partial_{s}\mathcal{H}={}&\check{\Delta}\mathcal{H}+2\check{Rm}[\mathcal{H}]+\check{Rc}*\mathcal{H}+\varphi_{s}^{*}\mathcal{E}_{2}\\
&-\hat{\mathcal{H}}^{ab}\check{\nabla}_{a}\check{\nabla}_{b}\mathcal{H}+(\check{g}-\hat{\mathcal{H}})^{ab}(\check{g}-\hat{\mathcal{H}})^{pq}(\check{\nabla}\mathcal{H}*\check{\nabla}\mathcal{H})_{abpqij}\\
&+\check{Rm}_{jab}^{p}\check{g}_{ip}\tilde{\mathcal{H}}^{ab}+\check{Rm}_{jab}^{p}\hat{\mathcal{H}}^{ab}\mathcal{H}_{ip}+\check{Rm}_{iab}^{p}\check{g}_{jp}\tilde{\mathcal{H}}^{ab}+\check{Rm}_{iab}^{p}\hat{\mathcal{H}}^{ab}\mathcal{H}_{jp}\end{aligned}

where
\check{g}(s)=(1-s)\varphi_{s}^{*}\overline{g}
.

Let
s_{0}=s_{0}(\tau_{*},\tau_{0})<0
be defined by

\tau_{*}-\ln(1-s_{0})=\tau_{0}

and set
s_{0}^{\prime}\doteqdot\max\{s_{0},-1\}
.
Then

\displaystyle(x,s)\in\{f>2\gamma e^{\tau_{*}}\}\times[s_{0}^{\prime},0]\quad\text{ implies}

\displaystyle(\varphi_{s}(x),\tau_{*}-\ln(1-s))\in\{(y,\tau)\in M\times\mathbb{R}:f(y)>\gamma e^{\tau}\}.

Hence,

\displaystyle|\mathcal{H}|_{\check{g}}(x,s)\leq\delta,\qquad|\varphi_{s}^{*}\mathcal{E}_{2}|_{\check{g}}(x,s)=\frac{1}{1-s}|\mathcal{E}_{2}|_{\overline{g}}(\varphi_{s}(x),\tau_{*}-\ln(1-s))\leq\delta,

\displaystyle\text{and}\quad|\check{\nabla}\varphi_{s}^{*}\mathcal{E}_{2}|_{\check{g}}(x,s)=\frac{1}{(1-s)^{3/2}}|\overline{\nabla}\mathcal{E}_{2}|_{\overline{g}}(\varphi_{s}(x),\tau_{*}-\ln(1-s))\leq\delta

for all
(x,s)\in\{f(x)>2\gamma e^{\tau_{*}}\}\times[s_{0}^{\prime},0]
.

Assume
\tau_{0}\gg 1
is sufficiently large depending on
n,M,\overline{g},f,\gamma
so that

f(p)>\gamma e^{\tau_{0}}\implies B_{\overline{g}}(p,1)\subset\left\{f>\frac{1}{2}f(p)\right\}

and
f(p)>\gamma e^{\tau_{0}}
implies
B_{\overline{g}}(p,1)
contains no points conjugate to
p
.

As in the proof of Lemma
6.8
,
Propositions
C.3
and
C.4
may be applied to (
6.10
) to deduce that if
\delta\ll 1
is sufficiently small depending on
n,M,\overline{g},f
,
then

|\overline{\nabla}h|_{\overline{g}}(p_{*},\tau_{*})+|\overline{\nabla}^{2}h|_{\overline{g}}(p_{*},\tau_{*})\lesssim_{n,M,\overline{g},f}\delta

for all
p_{*}\in M
with
f(p_{*})>4\gamma e^{\tau_{*}}
.
∎

6.4.
Proof of Theorem
6.1

Proof of Theorem
6.1
.

We first seek to prove a
C^{0}
bound.
The proof of Proposition
4.7

shows that there exists
C_{1}=C_{1}(n,M,\overline{g},f,\lambda_{*})\geq 1\geq\overline{p}
such that

\left|\sum_{j=1}^{K}p_{j}h_{j}\right|\leq C_{1}\overline{p}e^{\lambda_{*}\tau_{0}}f^{-\lambda_{*}}\leq C_{1}e^{\lambda_{*}\tau_{0}}f^{-\lambda_{*}}

for all
|\mathbf{p}|\leq\overline{p}e^{\lambda_{*}\tau_{0}}
.

By Lemma
6.5
,
|h|_{\overline{g}}:M\times[\tau_{0},\tau_{1}]\to\mathbb{R}
satisfies the differential inequality

(6.11)

\displaystyle\partial_{\tau}|h|_{\overline{g}}^{2}\leq{}

\displaystyle(1+C_{n}|h|_{\overline{g}})\overline{\Delta}|h|_{\overline{g}}^{2}-\overline{\nabla}_{\overline{\nabla}f}|h|_{\overline{g}}^{2}+4C_{n}(1+\epsilon)|\overline{Rm}|_{\overline{g}}|h|_{\overline{g}}^{2}+|\mathcal{E}_{2}|_{\overline{g}}|h|_{\overline{g}}

(6.12)

\displaystyle\leq{}

\displaystyle(1+C_{n}|h|_{\overline{g}})\overline{\Delta}|h|_{\overline{g}}^{2}-\overline{\nabla}_{\overline{\nabla}f}|h|_{\overline{g}}^{2}+4C_{n}(1+\epsilon)|\overline{Rm}|_{\overline{g}}|h|_{\overline{g}}^{2}+\frac{C_{0}}{\Gamma_{0}}e^{-\tau}|h|_{\overline{g}}

on
M\times(\tau_{0},\tau_{1})
so long as
0<\epsilon\ll 1
is sufficiently small depending on
n
.
Moreover, the assumption that
\supp\mathcal{E}_{2}\subset\{e^{\tau}\leq f\leq\Gamma_{0}e^{\tau}\}

implies

(6.13)

\partial_{\tau}|h|_{\overline{g}}^{2}\leq(1+C_{n}|h|_{\overline{g}})\overline{\Delta}|h|_{\overline{g}}^{2}-\overline{\nabla}_{\overline{\nabla}f}|h|_{\overline{g}}^{2}+4C_{n}(1+\epsilon)|\overline{Rm}|_{\overline{g}}|h|_{\overline{g}}^{2}

on
\{(x,\tau)\in M\times(\tau_{0},\tau_{1}):f(x)<e^{\tau}\}
.

We aim to use Lemmas
6.6
and
6.7
to construct a supersolution for
|h|_{\overline{g}}^{2}
outside of some compact subset of
M
.
To this end, let
0<\delta\leq\epsilon
and define

\displaystyle A=A(n,M,\overline{g},f,\lambda_{*})\doteqdot C_{1}^{2}+1>0

\displaystyle B_{0}=B_{0}(n,M,\overline{g},f,\lambda_{*})\doteqdot A\left\{2|\lambda_{*}|\left(2|\lambda_{*}|+\frac{n}{2}\right)+4C_{n}(1+1)\sup_{M}\left(|\overline{Rm}|_{\overline{g}}f\right)\right\}+1>0,

\displaystyle a\doteqdot\delta^{2}\in(0,\epsilon^{2}]\subset(0,1),\text{ and }

\displaystyle B_{1}=B_{1}(n,M,\overline{g},f)\doteqdot 4C_{n}(1+1)\left(\sup_{M}|\overline{Rm}|_{\overline{g}}f\right)+C_{0}>0.

By definition,

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} &B_{0}-A\left\{2|\lambda_{*}|\left(2|\lambda_{*}|+\frac{n}{2}\right)+4C_{n}(1+\epsilon)\sup_{M}(|\overline{Rm}|f)\right\}\\
\geq{}&B_{0}-A\left\{2|\lambda_{*}|\left(2|\lambda_{*}|+\frac{n}{2}\right)+4C_{n}(1+1)\sup_{M}(|\overline{Rm}|f)\right\}\\
={}&1,\end{aligned}

and

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} B_{1}=4C_{n}(1+1)\left(\sup_{M}|\overline{Rm}|_{\overline{g}}f\right)+C_{0}>4C_{n}(1+\epsilon)(\sup f|Rm|)a+C_{0}\sqrt{a}.\end{aligned}

Additionally, define

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} \gamma_{+}&\doteqdot\left(\frac{2\delta^{2}}{A}\right)^{1/(2|\lambda_{*}|)}\lesssim_{n,M,\overline{g},f,\lambda_{*}}\epsilon^{1/|\lambda_{*}|}\text{ and}\\
\gamma_{-}&\doteqdot\left(\frac{\delta^{2}}{2A}\right)^{1/(2|\lambda_{*}|)}\lesssim_{n,M,\overline{g},f,\lambda*}\epsilon^{1/|\lambda_{*}|}.\end{aligned}

Note that
0<\gamma_{-}\leq\gamma_{+}\leq 1
.

By Lemma
6.6
with
\kappa=-2\lambda_{*}>0
,

e^{2\lambda_{*}\tau}(Af^{-2\lambda_{*}}-B_{0}f^{-2\lambda_{*}-1})
is a supersolution of equation (
6.13
) on the domain
\{\Upsilon<f<\gamma_{+}e^{\tau}\}

for some large constant
\Upsilon=\Upsilon(n,M,\overline{g},f,\lambda_{*})
that depends only on
n,M,\overline{g},f,\lambda_{*}

and so long as
\gamma_{+}\ll 1
is sufficiently small depending on
n,M,\overline{g},f,\lambda_{*}
.
In fact, since
\gamma_{+}\lesssim_{n,M,\overline{g},f,\lambda_{*}}\epsilon^{1/|\lambda_{*}|}
, it instead suffices to assume
\epsilon\ll 1
is sufficiently small depending on
n,M,\overline{g},f,\lambda_{*}
.
Additionally,

e^{2\lambda_{*}\tau}(Af^{-2\lambda_{*}}-B_{0}f^{-2\lambda_{*}-1})\geq e^{2\lambda_{*}\tau}f^{2|\lambda_{*}|}(A-B_{0}/\Upsilon)\qquad\text{ on }\{\Upsilon\leq f\leq\gamma_{+}e^{\tau}\}.

In particular, by taking
\Upsilon
possibly larger depending on
n,M,\overline{g},f,\lambda_{*}
, we may assume that

A-B_{0}/\Upsilon>A-1=C_{1}^{2}>0,

that Lemma
6.4
holds with
\Gamma=\Upsilon
,
and that Lemma
6.8
holds with
\Gamma=\frac{1}{4}\Upsilon
.

Similarly, by Lemma
6.7
,
a-B_{1}/f
is a positive supersolution of equation (
6.12
)
on the domain
\{\gamma_{-}e^{\tau}<f<\Gamma_{0}e^{\tau}\}

if
\tau_{0}\gg 1
is sufficiently large depending on
n,M,\overline{g},f,\lambda_{*},\epsilon,\delta
.

Next, we claim that if
\tau_{0}\gg 1
is sufficiently large depending on
n,M,\overline{g},f,\lambda_{*},\delta

and
\tau\geq\tau_{0}
, then

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} a-B_{1}/f&<e^{2\lambda_{*}\tau}f^{2|\lambda_{*}|}(A-B_{0}/f)\text{ when }f=\gamma_{+}e^{\tau}\text{ and}\\
a-B_{1}/f&>e^{2\lambda_{*}\tau}f^{2|\lambda_{*}|}(A-B_{0}/f)\text{ when }f=\gamma_{-}e^{\tau}.\end{aligned}

For the first inequality,

\displaystyle a-B_{1}/f

\displaystyle\leq a

\displaystyle(B_{1},f>0)

\displaystyle=\delta^{2}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2}A\gamma_{+}^{2|\lambda_{*}|}

\displaystyle\left(\gamma_{+}=(2\delta^{2}/A)^{1/(2|\lambda_{*}|)}\right)

\displaystyle<A\gamma_{+}^{2|\lambda_{*}|}-\frac{B_{0}}{\gamma_{+}e^{\tau}}\gamma_{+}^{2|\lambda_{*}|}

\displaystyle(\tau\geq\tau_{0}\gg 1)

\displaystyle=\left(A-\frac{B_{0}}{f}\right)e^{2\lambda_{*}\tau}f^{2|\lambda_{*}|}

\displaystyle(f=\gamma_{+}e^{\tau}).

For the second inequality,

\displaystyle(A-B_{0}/f)e^{2\lambda_{*}\tau}f^{2|\lambda_{*}|}

\displaystyle\leq Ae^{2\lambda_{*}\tau}f^{2|\lambda_{*}|}

\displaystyle(B_{0},f>0)

\displaystyle=A\gamma_{-}^{2|\lambda_{*}|}

\displaystyle(f=\gamma_{-}e^{\tau})

\displaystyle=\frac{\delta^{2}}{2}

\displaystyle\left(\gamma_{-}=(\delta^{2}/(2A))^{1/(2|\lambda_{*}|)}\right)

\displaystyle<\delta^{2}-\frac{B_{1}}{\gamma_{-}e^{\tau}}

\displaystyle(\tau\geq\tau_{0}\gg 1)

\displaystyle=a-\frac{B_{1}}{f}

\displaystyle(f=\gamma_{-}e^{\tau}).

Having proven the claim, it then follows that the function
u:M\times[\tau_{0},\infty)\to\mathbb{R}
given by

u=\begin{cases}\left(A-\frac{B_{0}}{f}\right)e^{2\lambda_{*}\tau}f^{2|\lambda_{*}|},&\text{if }f\leq\gamma_{-}e^{\tau},\\
\min\left\{\left(A-\frac{B_{0}}{f}\right)e^{2\lambda_{*}\tau}f^{2|\lambda_{*}|},a-\frac{B_{1}}{f}\right\},&\text{if }\gamma_{-}e^{\tau}\leq f\leq\gamma_{+}e^{\tau},\\
a-\frac{B_{1}}{f},&\text{if }\gamma_{+}e^{\tau}\leq f,\end{cases}

is a locally Lipschitz continuous function which is also positive
u>0
on
\{\Upsilon\leq f\}

when
\tau_{0}\gg 1
is sufficiently large (depending on
n,M,\overline{g},f,\lambda_{*},\epsilon,\delta
).
Moreover,
u
is a supersolution of equation (
6.11
) on
\{\Upsilon\leq f\leq\Gamma_{0}e^{\tau}\}
.
Indeed,
u
equals supersolutions on
\{\Upsilon<f\leq\gamma_{-}e^{\tau}\}\cup\{\gamma_{+}e^{\tau}\leq f<\Gamma_{0}e^{\tau}\}
,
and, on the region
\{\gamma_{-}e^{\tau}\leq f\leq\gamma_{+}e^{\tau}\}
,
u
is a minimum of two supersolutions which is again a supersolution.

Notice that
\Upsilon=\Upsilon(n,M,\overline{g},f,\lambda_{*})
depends only on
n,M,\overline{g},f,\lambda_{*}
.
Since the eigenmodes
h_{j}
are smooth,
there exists
C_{2}=C_{2}(n,M,\overline{g},f,\lambda_{*})
such that, for all
x\in\{f<2\Upsilon\}
,

\displaystyle\sum_{l=0}^{4n+2}|\overline{\nabla}^{l}h|_{\overline{g}}(x,\tau_{0})

\displaystyle={}

\displaystyle\sum_{l=0}^{4n+2}|\overline{\nabla}^{l}\sum_{j=1}^{K}p_{j}h_{j}|_{\overline{g}}(x,\tau_{0})

\displaystyle\left(\tau_{0}\gg 1\implies 2\Upsilon\leq\frac{\gamma_{0}}{2}e^{\tau_{0}}\right)

\displaystyle\leq{}

\displaystyle\overline{p}e^{\lambda_{*}\tau_{0}}\sum_{j=1}^{K}\sum_{l=0}^{4n+2}|\overline{\nabla}^{l}h_{j}|

\displaystyle\leq{}

\displaystyle C_{2}\overline{p}e^{\lambda_{*}\tau_{0}}

\displaystyle(\text{smoothness of the $h_{j}$}).

By Lemma
6.4
, if
\epsilon\ll 1
is sufficiently small depending on
n,M,\overline{g},f,\lambda_{*}
and

\tau_{0}\gg 1
is sufficiently large depending on
n,M,\overline{g},f,\lambda_{*},\overline{p}
,
then

\displaystyle\sum_{l=0}^{2}|\overline{\nabla}^{l}h|_{\overline{g}}(x,\tau)

\displaystyle\lesssim_{n,M,\overline{g},f,\lambda_{*}}(\mu+C_{2}\overline{p})e^{\lambda_{*}\tau}

\displaystyle\lesssim_{n,M,\overline{g},f,\lambda_{*}}(\mu+C_{2}\overline{p})e^{\lambda_{*}\tau}f^{|\lambda_{*}|}

for all
(x,\tau)\in\{f<\Upsilon\}\times[\tau_{0},\tau_{1}]
.
Let
C_{3}=C_{3}(n,M,\overline{g},f,\lambda_{*})
be a constant such that

\sum_{l=0}^{2}|\overline{\nabla}^{l}h|_{\overline{g}}(x,\tau)\leq C_{3}(\mu+C_{2}\overline{p})e^{\lambda_{*}\tau}f^{|\lambda_{*}|}

for all
(x,\tau)\in\{f<\Upsilon\}\times[\tau_{0},\tau_{1}]
.

Since
C_{1},C_{2},C_{3}
depend only on
n,M,\overline{g},f,\lambda_{*}
,
it follows that

C_{3}(\mu+C_{2}\overline{p})\leq C_{1}<\sqrt{A-B_{0}/\Upsilon}

if
0<\mu,\overline{p}\ll 1
are sufficiently small depending on
n,M,\overline{g},f,\lambda_{*}
.
Under this condition,

|h|^{2}_{\overline{g}}(x,\tau)<u(x,\tau)\qquad\text{ for all }(x,\tau)\in\{f=\Upsilon\}\times[\tau_{0},\tau_{1}].

Note additionally that

|h|^{2}_{\overline{g}}(x,\tau)=0<u(x,\tau)\qquad\text{ for all }(x,\tau)\in\{f\geq\Gamma_{0}e^{\tau}\}.

Finally, for all
x\in\{\Upsilon\leq f\}
,

\displaystyle|h|^{2}_{\overline{g}}(x,\tau_{0})={}

\displaystyle\eta^{2}_{\gamma_{0}}\left|\sum_{j=1}^{K}p_{j}h_{j}\right|^{2}

\displaystyle\leq{}

\displaystyle C_{1}^{2}e^{2\lambda_{*}\tau_{0}}f^{-2\lambda_{*}}

\displaystyle<{}

\displaystyle(A-B_{0}/\Upsilon)e^{2\lambda_{*}\tau_{0}}f^{-2\lambda_{*}}

\displaystyle\leq{}

\displaystyle(A-B_{0}/f)e^{2\lambda_{*}\tau_{0}}f^{-2\lambda_{*}},

and, for all
x\in\{f\geq\gamma_{-}e^{\tau}\}
,

\displaystyle|h|^{2}_{\overline{g}}(x,\tau_{0})={}

\displaystyle\eta^{2}_{\gamma_{0}}\left|\sum_{j=1}^{K}p_{j}h_{j}\right|^{2}

\displaystyle\leq{}

\displaystyle\eta^{2}_{\gamma_{0}}C_{1}^{2}e^{2\lambda_{*}\tau_{0}}f^{-2\lambda_{*}}

\displaystyle\leq{}

\displaystyle C_{1}^{2}\gamma_{0}^{2|\lambda_{*}|}

\displaystyle(\supp\eta_{\gamma_{0}}\subset\{f\leq\gamma_{0}e^{\tau}\})

\displaystyle\leq{}

\displaystyle\frac{\delta^{2}}{2}

\displaystyle(\gamma_{0}\ll 1)

\displaystyle<{}

\displaystyle a-\frac{B_{1}}{f}

\displaystyle(\tau\geq\tau_{0}\gg 1)

if
0<\gamma_{0}\ll 1
is sufficiently small depending on
n,M,\overline{g},f,\lambda_{*},\delta

and
\tau_{0}\gg 1
is sufficiently large depending on
n,M,\overline{g},f,\lambda_{*},\delta
.
Therefore,
|h^{2}|_{\overline{g}}(x,\tau_{0})\leq u(x,\tau_{0})
for all
x\in\{\Upsilon\leq f\leq\Gamma_{0}e^{\tau}\}
.

Since
u
is a supersolution and
|h|^{2}_{\overline{g}}
a subsolution of equation (
6.11
),
the maximum principle then implies that
|h|^{2}_{\overline{g}}\leq u
for all
(x,\tau)\in\{\Upsilon\leq f\leq\Gamma_{0}e^{\tau}\}\times[\tau_{0},\tau_{1}]
.
Combined with the fact that
h\equiv 0
on
\{f\geq\Gamma_{0}e^{\tau}\}
and the bound

|h|_{\overline{g}}(x,\tau)\leq C_{1}e^{\lambda_{*}\tau}f^{-\lambda_{*}}\qquad\text{ for all }(x,\tau)\in\{f\leq\Upsilon\}\times[\tau_{0},\tau_{1}],

it then follows that

\displaystyle|h|_{\overline{g}}(x,\tau)

\displaystyle\lesssim_{n,M,\overline{g},f,\lambda_{*}}e^{\lambda_{*}\tau}f^{-\lambda_{*}}\text{ and}

\displaystyle|h|_{\overline{g}}(x,\tau)

\displaystyle\leq\delta

throughout
M\times[\tau_{0},\tau_{1}]
so long as
\tau_{0}\gg 1
is sufficiently large depending on
n,M,\overline{g},f,\lambda_{*},\delta
.

Now, we use the interior estimates to improve this
C^{0}
bound to a
C^{2}
bound.
Since
\lambda_{*}<\min\{\lambda_{K},0\}=\min_{j\leq K}\min\{\lambda_{j},0\}
,
there exists some positive
\delta^{\prime}=\delta^{\prime}(n,M,\overline{g},f,\lambda_{*})>0
such that

\max\{-\lambda_{j},0\}+\delta^{\prime}<|\lambda_{*}|
for all
j\leq K
.
The initial data thus satisfies the estimate

\displaystyle\quad\sum_{l=0}^{3}|\overline{\nabla}^{l}h|_{\overline{g}}(x,\tau_{0})

\displaystyle=\sum_{l=0}^{3}\left|\overline{\nabla}^{l}\left(\eta_{\gamma_{0}}\sum_{j=1}^{K}p_{j}h_{j}\right)\right|_{\overline{g}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\left(\sum_{l=0}^{3}\left|\overline{\nabla}^{l}\eta_{\gamma_{0}}\right|\right)\left(\sum_{l=0}^{3}\sum_{j=1}^{K}|p_{j}||\overline{\nabla}^{l}h_{j}|\right)

\displaystyle\lesssim_{n,M,\overline{g},f,\lambda_{*}}\left(\sum_{l=0}^{3}\left|\overline{\nabla}^{l}\eta_{\gamma_{0}}\right|\right)\left(\sum_{j=1}^{K}|p_{j}|f^{\max\{-\lambda_{j},0\}+\delta^{\prime}}\right)

\displaystyle(\text{Proposition \ref{Prop Eigmode Derivative Growth}})

\displaystyle\lesssim_{n,M,\overline{g},f,\lambda_{*}}\left(\sum_{l=0}^{3}\left|\overline{\nabla}^{l}\eta_{\gamma_{0}}\right|\right)\overline{p}e^{\lambda_{*}\tau_{0}}f^{|\lambda_{*}|}

\displaystyle(\text{choice of $\delta^{\prime}$})

\displaystyle\lesssim_{n,M,\overline{g},f}\overline{p}e^{\lambda_{*}\tau_{0}}f^{|\lambda_{*}|}

\displaystyle\leq e^{\lambda_{*}\tau_{0}}f^{|\lambda_{*}|}.

Thus, there exists some
0<\gamma_{1}\ll 1
sufficiently small depending on
n,M,\overline{g},f,\lambda_{*}
so that Lemma
6.8
applies with
\Gamma=\frac{1}{4}\Upsilon
and
\gamma=4\gamma_{1}
to show that

|\overline{\nabla}h|_{\overline{g}}+|\overline{\nabla}^{2}h|_{\overline{g}}\lesssim_{n,M,\overline{g},f,\lambda_{*}}e^{\lambda_{*}\tau}f^{|\lambda_{*}|}\qquad\text{ on }\{\Upsilon\leq f\leq\gamma_{1}e^{\tau}\}.

The above estimate on the initial data also shows

\displaystyle\sum_{l=0}^{3}|\overline{\nabla}^{l}h|_{\overline{g}}(x,\tau_{0})\lesssim

{}_{n,M,\overline{g},f,\lambda_{*}}\left(\sum_{l=0}^{3}\left|\overline{\nabla}^{l}\eta_{\gamma_{0}}\right|\right)\overline{p}e^{\lambda_{*}\tau_{0}}f^{|\lambda_{*}|}

\displaystyle\lesssim

{}_{n,M,\overline{g},f}\overline{p}\gamma_{0}^{|\lambda_{*}|}

\displaystyle\leq{}

\displaystyle\gamma_{0}^{|\lambda_{*}|}.

Hence, if
\gamma_{0}\ll 1
is sufficiently small depending on
n,M,\overline{g},f,\lambda_{*},\delta
then

\sum_{l=0}^{3}|\overline{\nabla}^{l}h|_{\overline{g}}(x,\tau_{0})\leq\delta.\qquad\text{for all }(x,\tau)\in M\times\{\tau_{0}\}.

Moreover,

\displaystyle|\mathcal{E}_{2}|+|\overline{\nabla}\mathcal{E}_{2}|\leq\frac{C_{0}}{\Gamma_{0}}e^{-\tau_{0}}+\frac{C_{0}}{\Gamma_{0}^{3/2}}e^{-\frac{3}{2}\tau_{0}}\leq\delta

if
\tau_{0}\gg 1
is sufficiently large depending on
n,M,\overline{g},f,\delta
.
Therefore, Lemma
6.9
applies to show that

|\overline{\nabla}h|+|\overline{\nabla}^{2}h|\lesssim_{n,M,\overline{g},f}\delta\qquad\text{ on }\{\gamma_{1}e^{\tau}\leq f\}

if
0<\delta\leq\epsilon\ll 1
is sufficiently small depending on
n,M,\overline{g},f

and
\tau_{0}\gg 1
is sufficiently large depending on
n,M,\overline{g},f,\lambda_{*},\delta
.

Together with the local
C^{2}
estimate

\sum_{l=0}^{2}|\overline{\nabla}^{l}h|\lesssim_{n,M,\overline{g},f,\lambda_{*}}e^{\lambda_{*}\tau}f^{|\lambda_{*}|}\qquad\text{ on }\{f\leq\Upsilon\},

it therefore follows that

\displaystyle\sum_{l=0}^{2}|\overline{\nabla}^{l}h|_{\overline{g}}(x,\tau)

\displaystyle\lesssim_{n,M,\overline{g},f,\lambda_{*}}e^{\lambda_{*}\tau}f^{|\lambda_{*}|}\text{ and}

\displaystyle\sum_{l=0}^{2}|\overline{\nabla}^{l}h|_{\overline{g}}(x,\tau)

\displaystyle\lesssim_{n,M,\overline{g},f}\delta

throughout
M\times[\tau_{0},\tau_{1}]
if
\tau_{0}\gg 1
is sufficiently large depending on
n,M,\overline{g},f,\lambda_{*},\delta
.
This completes the proof.
∎

7.
Behavior of
L^{2}_{f}(M)
Quantities

In this section, we explore the dynamics of the
L^{2}_{f}(M)
estimates included in Definition
4.21
of
\mathcal{B}
.

Lemma 7.1

(
L^{2}_{f}
Estimates for the Error Terms
\mathcal{E}_{1},\mathcal{E}_{2}
)
.

Assume

|h|_{\overline{g}}+|\overline{\nabla}h|_{\overline{g}}+|\overline{\nabla}^{2}h|_{\overline{g}}\leq Wf^{|\lambda_{*}|}e^{\lambda_{*}\tau}\qquad(\text{for some }W\geq 1),

|h|_{\overline{g}}+|\overline{\nabla}h|_{\overline{g}}+|\overline{\nabla}^{2}h|_{\overline{g}}\leq\epsilon,

|\mathcal{E}_{2}|_{\overline{g}}\leq C_{0}e^{-\tau}\qquad\text{ and }\qquad\supp\mathcal{E}_{2}\subset\{f\geq e^{\tau}\}

throughout
M\times[\tau_{0},\tau_{1}]
.

If
0<\epsilon\ll 1
is sufficiently small (depending on
n
)
and
\tau_{0}\gg 1
is sufficiently large (depending on
n,M,\overline{g},f,\lambda_{*},C_{0}
)
then there exists a constant
C_{1}
(depending on
n,M,\overline{g},f,\lambda_{*}
) such that

\|\mathcal{E}_{1}(\tau)\|_{L^{2}_{f}}+\|\mathcal{E}_{2}(\tau)\|_{L^{2}_{f}}\leq C_{1}W^{2}e^{2\lambda_{*}\tau}\qquad\text{for all }\tau\in[\tau_{0},\tau_{1}].

Proof.

Recall that
\mathcal{E}_{1}
is defined in (
6.2
) as

(7.1)

\mathcal{E}_{1}(\tau)\doteqdot\overline{R}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[8.05121pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[8.05121pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[4.88843pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.49171pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{p}\mathchoice{\makebox[3.51666pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.51666pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.1029pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.50208pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{ja}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.12234pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.12234pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.46532pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.76094pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{b}}}\left(\overline{g}_{ip}\tilde{h}^{ab}+\hat{h}^{ab}h_{ip}\right)+\overline{R}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[7.16693pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[7.16693pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[4.2782pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.05585pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{p}\mathchoice{\makebox[3.51666pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.51666pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.1029pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.50208pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{ia}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.12234pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.12234pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.46532pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.76094pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{b}}}\left(\overline{g}_{jp}\tilde{h}^{ab}+\hat{h}^{ab}h_{jp}\right)\\
+g^{ab}g^{pq}(\overline{\nabla}h*\overline{\nabla}h)-\hat{h}^{ab}\overline{\nabla}_{a}\overline{\nabla}_{b}h.

If
0<\epsilon\ll 1
is sufficiently small depending on
n
, then

|\mathcal{E}_{1}|_{\overline{g}}\lesssim_{n}(1+|\overline{Rm}|_{\overline{g}})\left(|h|_{\overline{g}}+|\overline{\nabla}h|_{\overline{g}}+|\overline{\nabla}^{2}h|_{\overline{g}}\right)^{2}\lesssim_{n,M,\overline{g},f}\left(|h|_{\overline{g}}+|\overline{\nabla}h|_{\overline{g}}+|\overline{\nabla}^{2}h|_{\overline{g}}\right)^{2}

throughout
M\times[\tau_{0},\tau_{1}]
.
Therefore,

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} &\qquad\|\mathcal{E}_{1}\|_{L^{2}_{f}}^{2}\\
&=\int_{M}|\mathcal{E}_{1}|^{2}_{\overline{g}}e^{-f}dV_{\overline{g}}\\
&=\int_{\{f\leq e^{\tau}\}}|\mathcal{E}_{1}|^{2}_{\overline{g}}e^{-f}dV_{\overline{g}}+\int_{\{f>e^{\tau}\}}|\mathcal{E}_{1}|^{2}_{\overline{g}}e^{-f}dV_{\overline{g}}\\
&\lesssim_{n,M,\overline{g},f}\int_{\{f\leq e^{\tau}\}}W^{4}f^{4|\lambda_{*}|}e^{4\lambda_{*}\tau}e^{-f}dV_{\overline{g}}\\
&\qquad+\int_{\{f>e^{\tau}\}}\epsilon^{4}e^{-f}dV_{\overline{g}}\\
&\leq W^{4}e^{4\lambda_{*}\tau}\int_{M}f^{4|\lambda_{*}|}e^{-f}dV_{\overline{g}}+\epsilon^{4}\int_{\{f>e^{\tau}\}}e^{-f}dV_{\overline{g}}\\
\end{aligned}

The moment estimates for
f
in Proposition
2.8
imply that

\int_{M}f^{4|\lambda_{*}|}e^{-f}dV_{\overline{g}}\leq C(n,M,\overline{g},f,\lambda_{*}).

Additionally, the weighted volume decay in Proposition
2.31
implies

\int_{\{f>e^{\tau}\}}e^{-f}dV_{\overline{g}}\lesssim C(n,M,\overline{g},f)(e^{\tau})^{\frac{n}{2}-1}e^{-\frac{1}{8}(e^{\tau})}\leq e^{4\lambda_{*}\tau}

if
\tau\geq\tau_{0}\gg 1
is sufficiently large depending on
n,M,\overline{g},f,\lambda_{*}
.
The estimate for
\mathcal{E}_{1}
then follows.

We now estimate
\mathcal{E}_{2}
.

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} \|\mathcal{E}_{2}\|_{L^{2}_{f}}^{2}&=\int_{M}|\mathcal{E}_{2}|_{\overline{g}}^{2}e^{-f}dV_{\overline{g}}\\
&\leq C_{0}^{2}\int_{\supp\mathcal{E}_{2}}e^{-2\tau}e^{-f}dV_{\overline{g}}\\
&\leq C_{0}^{2}e^{-2\tau}\int_{\{f\geq e^{\tau}\}}e^{-f}dV_{\overline{g}}&&(\supp\mathcal{E}_{2}\subset\{f\geq e^{\tau}\})\\
&\lesssim_{n,M,g,f}C_{0}^{2}e^{-2\tau}(e^{\tau})^{\frac{n}{2}-1}e^{-\frac{1}{8}(e^{\tau})}&&(\text{Proposition \ref{Prop Weighted Vol Decay}})\\
&\leq W^{4}e^{4\lambda_{*}\tau}&&(\tau\geq\tau_{0}\gg 1).\end{aligned}

∎

Lemma 7.2

(Evolutions of Projections)
.

Assume
h
is a smooth section of
\pi^{*}Sym^{2}T^{*}M\to M\times[\tau_{0},\tau_{1}]
such that

h(\cdot,\tau)\text{ is compactly supported in }M\text{ for all }\tau\in[\tau_{0},\tau_{1}],

\partial_{\tau}h=\overline{\Delta}_{f}h+2\overline{Rm}[h]+\mathcal{E}_{1}+\mathcal{E}_{2}\qquad\text{ on }M\times(\tau_{0},\tau_{1}),

\text{and }\|\mathcal{E}_{1}+\mathcal{E}_{2}\|_{L^{2}_{f}}\leq Ce^{2\lambda_{*}\tau}.

Then the projections
\pi_{u}h=h_{u},\pi_{s}=h_{s}
as in Definition
4.20
satisfy

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} \frac{d}{d\tau}\|e^{-\lambda_{*}\tau}h_{u}\|_{L^{2}_{f}}&\geq(\lambda_{K}-\lambda_{*})\|e^{-\lambda_{*}\tau}h_{u}\|_{L^{2}_{f}}-Ce^{\lambda_{*}\tau_{0}}&&\text{and}\\
\frac{d}{d\tau}\|e^{-\lambda_{*}\tau}h_{s}\|_{L^{2}_{f}}&\leq(\lambda_{K+1}-\lambda_{*})\|e^{-\lambda_{*}\tau}h_{s}\|_{L^{2}_{f}}+Ce^{\lambda_{*}\tau_{0}}.\end{aligned}

Proof.

To simplify notation, write

\displaystyle\partial_{\tau}h=\overline{\Delta}_{f}h+2\overline{Rm}[h]+\mathcal{E}_{1}+\mathcal{E}_{2}=\mathcal{A}h+\mathcal{E}

\displaystyle\text{where }\mathcal{A}=\overline{\Delta}_{f}+2\overline{Rm}\quad\text{ and }\quad\mathcal{E}=\mathcal{E}_{1}+\mathcal{E}_{2}.

Note that the projections
\pi_{u},\pi_{s}
commute with
\mathcal{A}
when applied to smooth compactly supported tensors.

It follows that

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} &\qquad\frac{d}{d\tau}\left(e^{-2\lambda_{*}\tau}\|h_{u}\|^{2}_{L^{2}_{f}}\right)\\
&=-2\lambda_{*}e^{-2\lambda_{*}\tau}\|h_{u}\|^{2}_{L^{2}_{f}}+2e^{-2\lambda_{*}\tau}\|h_{u}\|_{L^{2}_{f}}(\mathcal{A}h_{u},h_{u})_{L^{2}_{f}}+2e^{-2\lambda_{*}\tau}(\mathcal{E},h_{u})_{L^{2}_{f}}\\
&\geq-2\lambda_{*}e^{-2\lambda_{*}\tau}\|h_{u}\|^{2}_{L^{2}_{f}}+2e^{-2\lambda_{*}\tau}\|h_{u}\|^{2}_{L^{2}_{f}}\lambda_{K}\\
&\qquad-2e^{-2\lambda_{*}\tau}\|\mathcal{E}\|_{L^{2}_{f}}\|h_{u}\|_{L^{2}_{f}}\\
&=2e^{-2\lambda_{*}\tau}\|h_{u}\|^{2}_{L^{2}_{f}}(\lambda_{K}-\lambda_{*})-2e^{-2\lambda_{*}\tau}\|\mathcal{E}\|_{L^{2}_{f}}\|h_{u}\|_{L^{2}_{f}}\\
\end{aligned}

Note that

\lambda_{K}-\lambda_{*}>0

by assumption
3.1

and

\frac{d}{d\tau}e^{-2\lambda_{*}\tau}\|h_{u}\|^{2}_{L^{2}_{f}}=2e^{-\lambda_{*}\tau}\|h_{u}\|_{L^{2}_{f}}\frac{d}{d\tau}\|e^{-\lambda_{*}\tau}h_{u}\|_{L^{2}_{f}}.

Therefore,

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} \frac{d}{d\tau}\|e^{-\lambda_{*}\tau}h_{u}\|_{L^{2}_{f}}&\geq(\lambda_{K}-\lambda_{*})\|e^{-\lambda_{*}\tau}h_{u}\|_{L^{2}_{f}}-e^{-\lambda_{*}\tau}\|\mathcal{E}\|_{L^{2}_{f}}\\
&\geq(\lambda_{K}-\lambda_{*})\|e^{-\lambda_{*}\tau}h_{u}\|_{L^{2}_{f}}-Ce^{\lambda_{*}\tau}\\
&\geq(\lambda_{K}-\lambda_{*})\|e^{-\lambda_{*}\tau}h_{u}\|_{L^{2}_{f}}-Ce^{\lambda_{*}\tau_{0}}.\end{aligned}

A similar computation shows that

\displaystyle\frac{d}{d\tau}\|e^{-\lambda_{*}\tau}h_{s}\|_{L^{2}_{f}}

\displaystyle\leq(\lambda_{K+1}-\lambda_{*})\|e^{-\lambda_{*}\tau}h_{s}\|_{L^{2}_{f}}+e^{-\lambda_{*}\tau}\|\mathcal{E}\|_{L^{2}_{f}}

\displaystyle\leq(\lambda_{K+1}-\lambda_{*})\|e^{-\lambda_{*}\tau}h_{s}\|_{L^{2}_{f}}+Ce^{\lambda_{*}\tau_{0}}.

∎

Lemma 7.3
.

Let
\tau_{0}<\tau_{1}^{+}
.
Assume
h
is a smooth section of
\pi^{*}Sym^{2}T^{*}M\to M\times[\tau_{0},\tau_{1}^{+})
such that

\displaystyle h(\tau)\doteqdot h(\cdot,\tau)\text{ is a compactly supported in }M\text{ for all }\tau\in[\tau_{0},\tau_{1}^{+}),

\displaystyle\partial_{\tau}h=\overline{\Delta}_{f}h+2\overline{Rm}[h]+\mathcal{E}_{1}+\mathcal{E}_{2}\qquad\text{ on }M\times(\tau_{0},\tau_{1}^{+}),

\displaystyle\text{and }\|\mathcal{E}_{1}+\mathcal{E}_{2}\|_{L^{2}_{f}}\leq Ce^{2\lambda_{*}\tau}.

For any
\mu_{u},\mu_{s}>0
,
if
\tau_{0}\gg 1
is sufficiently large (depending on
n,M,\overline{g},f,\lambda_{*},C,\mu_{u},\mu_{s}
)
then the following two implications hold:

(1)

(Immediately Exits on Unstable Side)

For all
\tau_{1}\in[\tau_{0},\tau_{1}^{+})
,

\|h_{u}(\tau_{1})\|_{L^{2}_{f}}=\mu_{u}e^{\lambda_{*}\tau_{1}}\implies\|h_{u}(\tau)\|_{L^{2}_{f}}>\mu_{u}e^{\lambda_{*}\tau}

\text{ for all $\tau$ in a neighborhood of $\tau_{1}$ with $\tau>\tau_{1}$},

and

(2)

(Can’t Exit through Stable Side)

\|h_{s}(\tau_{0})\|_{L^{2}_{f}}\leq\mu_{s}e^{\lambda_{*}\tau_{0}}\implies\|h_{s}(\tau)\|_{L^{2}_{f}}\leq\mu_{s}e^{\lambda_{*}\tau}\qquad\text{for all }\tau\in[\tau_{0},\tau_{1}^{+}).

Proof.

(Immediately Exits on Unstable Side)

At a time
\tau_{1}\geq\tau_{0}
when

\|h_{u}(\tau_{1})\|_{L^{2}_{f}}=\mu_{u}e^{\lambda_{*}\tau_{1}},

Lemma
7.2
implies

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} \left.\frac{d}{d\tau}\right|_{\tau=\tau_{1}}\|e^{-\lambda_{*}\tau}h_{u}\|_{L^{2}_{f}}\geq{}&(\lambda_{K}-\lambda_{*})\|e^{-\lambda_{*}\tau_{1}}h_{u}\|_{L^{2}_{f}}-Ce^{\lambda_{*}\tau_{0}}\\
={}&(\lambda_{K}-\lambda_{*})\mu_{u}-Ce^{\lambda_{*}\tau_{0}}\\
>{}&0&&(\lambda_{*}<\lambda_{K})\end{aligned}

if
\tau_{0}\gg 1
is sufficiently large depending on
n,M,\overline{g},f,\lambda_{*},C,\mu_{u}
.

(Can’t Exit through Stable Side)

At a time
\tau_{1}\geq\tau_{0}
when

\|h_{s}(\tau_{1})\|=\mu_{s}e^{\lambda_{*}\tau_{1}},

Lemma
7.2
implies

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} \left.\frac{d}{d\tau}\right|_{\tau=\tau_{1}}\|e^{-\lambda_{*}\tau}h_{s}\|_{L^{2}_{f}}\leq{}&(\lambda_{K+1}-\lambda_{*})\|e^{-\lambda_{*}\tau}h_{s}\|_{L^{2}_{f}}+Ce^{\lambda_{*}\tau_{0}}\\
={}&(\lambda_{K+1}-\lambda_{*})\mu_{s}+Ce^{\lambda_{*}\tau_{0}}\\
<{}&0&&(\lambda_{K+1}<\lambda_{*})\end{aligned}

if
\tau_{0}\gg 1
is sufficiently large depending on
n,M,\overline{g},f,\lambda_{*},C,\mu_{s}
.
The statement follows.
∎

8.
Proof of Theorem
1.1

In this section, we combine the results of the prior sections to complete the proof of Theorem
1.1
.
First, we record some estimates on the
L^{2}_{f}
and
C^{m}
norms of the initial data
h_{\mathbf{p}}(t_{0})
that will be used in the remaining susbsections.
Second, we establish well-posedness results for the harmonic map heat flow solution
\tilde{\Phi}
that hold so long as
h=\frac{1}{1-t}(\Phi_{t}^{-1})^{*}\acute{G}-\overline{g}
remains small in a
C^{2}
sense.
Informally, these well-posedness results imply that, at the first time
\mathbf{p}
exits the set
\mathcal{P}
, it must be because
h_{\mathbf{p}}
fails to satisfy the estimates defining
\mathcal{B}
and not because the harmonic map heat flow solutions
\tilde{\Phi}
fail to exist.
Finally, we make rigorous the Ważewski box argument used to prove Theorem
1.1
.

8.1.
Estimates on the Initial Data

Lemma 8.1

(
L^{2}_{f}
Estimates at
t_{0}
)
.

If
|\mathbf{p}|\leq\overline{p}e^{\lambda_{*}\tau_{0}}\leq e^{\lambda_{*}\tau_{0}}
and

\tau_{0}\gg 1
is sufficiently large (depending on
n,M,\overline{g},f,\gamma_{0}
),
then the following estimates hold:

(8.1)

\displaystyle\left|(h_{\mathbf{p}}(t_{0}),h_{j})_{L^{2}_{f}}-p_{j}\right|

\displaystyle\lesssim_{n,M,\overline{g},f,\lambda_{*}}e^{-\frac{\gamma_{0}}{100}e^{\tau_{0}}}

\displaystyle\text{for }j\leq K,\text{ and}

(8.2)

\displaystyle\|\pi_{s}h_{\mathbf{p}}(t_{0})\|_{L^{2}_{f}}

\displaystyle\lesssim_{n,M,\overline{g},f,\lambda_{*}}e^{-\frac{\gamma_{0}}{100}e^{\tau_{0}}}.

Proof.

First, observe that

\displaystyle h_{\mathbf{p}}(t_{0})=\frac{1}{1-t_{0}}(\varphi_{t_{0}}^{-1})^{*}\acute{G}_{\mathbf{p}}(t_{0})-\overline{g}=\eta_{\gamma_{0}}\sum_{i=1}^{K}p_{i}h_{i}

by Remark
4.11
.
Hence, for any
j\in\mathbb{N}
,

(8.3)

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} (h_{\mathbf{p}}(t_{0}),h_{j})_{L^{2}_{f}}&=\sum_{i=1}^{K}p_{i}(\eta_{\gamma_{0}}h_{i},h_{j})_{L^{2}_{f}}\\
&=\sum_{i=1}^{K}p_{i}(h_{i},h_{j})_{L^{2}_{f}}+\sum_{i=1}^{K}p_{i}((\eta_{\gamma_{0}}-1)h_{i},h_{j})_{L^{2}_{f}}\\
&=\sum_{i=1}^{K}p_{i}\delta_{ij}+\sum_{i=1}^{K}p_{i}((\eta_{\gamma_{0}}-1)h_{i},h_{j})_{L^{2}_{f}},\end{aligned}

where here
\delta_{\cdot\cdot}
denotes the Kronecker delta function.
For any
j\in\mathbb{N}
and any
i\leq K
,

\displaystyle\quad\left|((\eta_{\gamma_{0}}-1)h_{i},h_{j})_{L^{2}_{f}}\right|

\displaystyle\leq\int_{\supp(\eta_{\gamma_{0}}-1)}|\eta_{\gamma_{0}}-1||\langle h_{i},h_{j}\rangle|e^{-f}dV

\displaystyle\leq\int_{\{f\geq\frac{1}{2}\gamma_{0}e^{\tau_{0}}\}}|h_{i}||h_{j}|e^{-f}dV

\displaystyle\leq\left(\int_{\{f\geq\frac{1}{2}\gamma_{0}e^{\tau_{0}}\}}|h_{i}|^{2}e^{-f}dV\right)^{1/2}\|h_{j}\|_{L^{2}_{f}(M)}

\displaystyle(\text{Cauchy-Schwartz})

\displaystyle\leq\left(\int_{\{f\geq\frac{1}{2}\gamma_{0}e^{\tau_{0}}\}}|h_{i}|^{2}e^{-f}dV\right)^{1/2}

\displaystyle(\|h_{j}\|_{L^{2}_{f}(M)}=1).

Using the eigenmode growth condition (Definition
2.23
) with
\delta=1
,

|h_{i}|\leq C_{i}f^{\max\{-\lambda_{i},0\}+1}
and
the integral may then be estimated by

(8.4)

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} &\quad\left(\int_{\{f\geq\frac{1}{2}\gamma_{0}e^{\tau_{0}}\}}|h_{i}|^{2}e^{-f}dV\right)^{1/2}\\
&\leq\left(\int_{\{f\geq\frac{1}{2}\gamma_{0}e^{\tau_{0}}\}}C_{i}^{2}f^{2\max\{-\lambda_{i},0\}+2}e^{-f}dV\right)^{1/2}\\
&\lesssim_{n,M,\overline{g},f,\lambda_{*}}\left(\int_{\{f\geq\frac{1}{2}\gamma_{0}e^{\tau_{0}}\}}f^{2\max\{-\lambda_{K},0\}+2}e^{-f}dV\right)^{1/2}&&(i\leq K=K(n,M,\overline{g},f,\lambda_{*}))\\
&\leq\left(\int_{M}f^{4\max\{-\lambda_{K},0\}+4}e^{-f}dV\right)^{1/4}\left(\int_{\{f\geq\frac{1}{2}\gamma_{0}e^{\tau_{0}}\}}e^{-f}dV\right)^{1/4}&&(\text{Cauchy-Schwartz})\\
&\lesssim_{n,M,\overline{g},f,\lambda_{*}}\left(\frac{1}{2}\gamma_{0}e^{\tau_{0}}\right)^{\frac{n}{8}-\frac{1}{4}}e^{-\frac{1}{32}\left(\frac{1}{2}\gamma_{0}e^{\tau_{0}}\right)}&&(\text{Propositions \ref{Prop Moment Ests} and \ref{Prop Weighted Vol Decay}})\\
&\leq e^{-\frac{1}{100}\gamma_{0}e^{\tau_{0}}}&&(\tau_{0}\gg 1).\end{aligned}

Recalling (
8.3
), it follows that for any
j\in\mathbb{N}
,

(8.5)

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} \left|(h_{\mathbf{p}}(t_{0}),h_{j})_{L^{2}_{f}}-\sum_{i=1}^{K}p_{i}\delta_{ij}\right|&=\left|\sum_{i=1}^{K}p_{i}((\eta_{\gamma_{0}}-1)h_{i},h_{j})_{L^{2}_{f}}\right|\\
&\leq\overline{p}e^{\lambda_{*}\tau_{0}}\sum_{i=1}^{K}\left|((\eta_{\gamma_{0}}-1)h_{i},h_{j})_{L^{2}_{f}}\right|\\
&\leq\sum_{i=1}^{K}\left|((\eta_{\gamma_{0}}-1)h_{i},h_{j})_{L^{2}_{f}}\right|\\
&\lesssim_{n,M,\overline{g},f,\lambda_{*}}e^{-\frac{1}{100}\gamma_{0}e^{\tau_{0}}}&&(\tau_{0}\gg 1),\end{aligned}

and estimate (
8.1
) then follows from taking
j\leq K
.

To prove (
8.2
), we estimate as follows:

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} \|\pi_{s}h_{\mathbf{p}}(t_{0})\|_{L^{2}_{f}}&=\left\|\pi_{s}\left(h_{\mathbf{p}}(t_{0})-\sum_{j=1}^{K}p_{j}h_{j}\right)\right\|_{L^{2}_{f}}\\
&\leq\left\|h_{\mathbf{p}}(t_{0})-\sum_{j=1}^{K}p_{j}h_{j}\right\|_{L^{2}_{f}}\\
&=\left\|(\eta_{\gamma_{0}}-1)\left(\sum_{j=1}^{K}p_{j}h_{j}\right)\right\|_{L^{2}_{f}}\\
&\leq\sum_{j=1}^{K}\overline{p}e^{\lambda_{*}\tau_{0}}\|(\eta_{\gamma_{0}}-1)h_{j}\|_{L^{2}_{f}}\\
&\leq\sum_{j=1}^{K}\|(\eta_{\gamma_{0}}-1)h_{j}\|_{L^{2}_{f}}&&(\tau_{0}\gg 1)\\
&\leq\sum_{j=1}^{K}\left(\int_{\{f\geq\frac{1}{2}\gamma_{0}e^{\tau_{0}}\}}|h_{j}|^{2}e^{-f}dV\right)^{1/2}\\
&\lesssim_{n,M,\overline{g},f,\lambda_{*}}e^{-\frac{1}{100}\gamma_{0}e^{\tau_{0}}}&&\eqref{L^2_f Est of h_i at Infty}.\end{aligned}

∎

Lemma 8.2

(
C^{m}
Estimates at
t_{0}
)
.

For any
m\in\mathbb{N}
there exists a constant
C
(depending on
n,M,\overline{g},f,\lambda_{*},m
) such that

|\overline{\nabla}^{m}h_{\mathbf{p}}(t_{0})|_{\overline{g}}\leq C\overline{p}\gamma_{0}^{|\lambda_{K}|}

if
0<1-t_{0}\ll 1
is sufficiently small (depending on
n,M,\overline{g},f,\gamma_{0},m
).

Proof.

The proof is similar to the proof of Proposition
4.7
but we provide the details nonetheless.

At
t=t_{0}
,

h_{\mathbf{p}}(t_{0})=\frac{1}{1-t_{0}}(\varphi_{t_{0}}^{-1})^{*}\acute{G}_{\mathbf{p}}(t_{0})-\overline{g}=\eta_{\gamma_{0}}\sum_{j=1}^{K}p_{j}h_{j}

by Remark
4.11
.
Let
m\in\mathbb{N}
. By Proposition
2.35
, it follows that

\displaystyle\quad|\overline{\nabla}^{m}h_{\mathbf{p}}(t_{0})|_{\overline{g}}

\displaystyle\lesssim_{n,m}\sum_{l=0}^{m}|\overline{\nabla}^{m-l}\eta_{\gamma_{0}}|_{\overline{g}}\left(\overline{p}e^{\lambda_{*}\tau_{0}}\sum_{j=1}^{K}|\overline{\nabla}^{l}h_{j}|_{\overline{g}}\right)

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{l=0}^{m}|\overline{\nabla}^{m-l}\eta_{\gamma_{0}}|_{\overline{g}}\left(\overline{p}e^{\lambda_{*}\tau_{0}}\sum_{j=1}^{K}C(n,M,\overline{g},f,j,\delta,l)f^{\max\{-\lambda_{j},0\}+\delta}\right)

\displaystyle\lesssim_{n,M,\overline{g},f,\lambda_{*},\delta,m}\sum_{l=0}^{m}|\overline{\nabla}^{m-l}\eta_{\gamma_{0}}|_{\overline{g}}\left(\overline{p}e^{\lambda_{*}\tau_{0}}\sum_{j=1}^{K}f^{\max\{-\lambda_{j},0\}+\delta}\right).

Since
\lambda_{*}<\min\{\lambda_{K},0\}=\min_{j\leq K}\min\{\lambda_{j},0\}
,
there exists positive
\delta=\delta(n,M,\overline{g},f,\lambda_{*})
such that

\lambda_{*}+\delta<\min\{\lambda_{K},0\}=\min_{j\leq K}\min\{\lambda_{j},0\}
.
Combined with the fact that
f
is bounded below on
M
(Lemma
2.30
), we can then estimate

\displaystyle\quad\,\sum_{l=0}^{m}|\overline{\nabla}^{m-l}\eta_{\gamma_{0}}|_{\overline{g}}\left(\overline{p}e^{\lambda_{*}\tau_{0}}\sum_{j=1}^{K}f^{\max\{-\lambda_{j},0\}+\delta}\right)

\displaystyle=\sum_{l=0}^{m}|\overline{\nabla}^{m-l}\eta_{\gamma_{0}}|_{\overline{g}}\left(\overline{p}e^{\lambda_{*}\tau_{0}}\sum_{j=1}^{K}f^{\max\{-\lambda_{j},0\}+\delta+\lambda_{*}}f^{-\lambda_{*}}\right)

\displaystyle\lesssim_{n,M,\overline{g},f,\lambda_{*}}\sum_{l=0}^{m}|\overline{\nabla}^{m-l}\eta_{\gamma_{0}}|_{\overline{g}}\overline{p}f^{-\lambda_{*}}e^{\lambda_{*}\tau_{0}}

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{l=0}^{m}|\overline{\nabla}^{m-l}\eta_{\gamma_{0}}|_{\overline{g}}\overline{p}\gamma_{0}^{|\lambda_{*}|}

where the last inequality follows from the fact that
\supp\eta_{\gamma_{0}}\subset\{f\leq\gamma_{0}e^{\tau_{0}}\}
and
-\lambda_{*}=|\lambda_{*}|
.
The derivative estimates for
\eta_{\gamma_{0}}
(see Subsection
4.2
) then complete the proof.
∎

8.2.
Must Exit Through
\mathcal{B}

In this subsection, we establish well-posedness results for the harmonic map heat flow solution
\tilde{\Phi}
that hold so long as
