\displaystyle-d_{\overline{g}}(F_{1},F_{2})\cdot\sum_{i}\nabla^{2}d\left(\nabla_{i}F_{1}\oplus\nabla_{i}F_{2},\nabla_{i}F_{1}\oplus\nabla_{i}F_{2}\right)

\displaystyle\leq{}

\displaystyle 0

holds since
\nabla^{2}d
is positive semidefinite on
U
.

Applying these estimates to (
B.1
), it follows that

(\partial_{t}-\Delta_{g})u\leq C_{n}\overline{\mathcal{K}}u\qquad\text{on }M\times(t_{0}^{\prime},t_{0}^{\prime}+\tau).

The drift control estimate Lemma
B.3
implies additionally that
u
is uniformly bounded.
Therefore,
u:M\times[t_{0}^{\prime},t_{0}^{\prime}+\tau]\to\mathbb{R}
is a bounded subsolution of

\displaystyle\partial_{t}u

\displaystyle\leq\Delta_{g}u+C_{n}\overline{\mathcal{K}}u

\displaystyle\text{ on }M\times(t_{0}^{\prime},t_{0}^{\prime}+\tau),

\displaystyle u(\cdot,t_{0}^{\prime})

\displaystyle=0

\displaystyle\text{ on }M.

The maximum principle then implies that
u\equiv 0
on
M\times[t_{0}^{\prime},t_{0}^{\prime}+\tau]
.
The definition of
u
then implies that
F_{1}(x,t)=F_{2}(x,t)
for all
(x,t)\in M\times[t_{0}^{\prime},t_{0}^{\prime}+\tau]
,
which contradicts the choice of
t_{0}^{\prime}
.
∎

Appendix C
Hölder Estimates in Euclidean Space

In this appendix, we provide the a priori Hölder estimates for parabolic equations on domains in Euclidean space.
The content here closely follows that of
[
Bam14
, Subsection 2.5]
and
[
App18
, Section 4]
.
While the results in this appendix are stated only for scalar-valued functions
u
for simplicity, all the results in this appendix hold for vector-valued functions
u
and the proofs carry over mutatis mutandis.

If
\Omega\subset\mathbb{R}^{n}\times\mathbb{R}
is some parabolic neighborhood (e.g.
\Omega=B_{r}(0)\times[0,T]
) and

m\in\mathbb{N}
, then
C^{2m}(\Omega)
denotes the space of functions
u(x,t)
on
\Omega
such that
\nabla^{i}\partial_{t}^{j}u
is continuous and
\sup_{\Omega}|\nabla^{i}\partial_{t}^{j}u|<\infty

for all
j\in\mathbb{N}
and multi-indices
i
with
|i|+2j\leq m
.
Let
\alpha\in(0,1)
. We define weighted Hölder norms
\|\cdot\|_{C^{2m,\alpha}(\Omega)}
which are invariant under parabolic dilation:
Assume

r_{\Omega}=\min\{r:\Omega\subset B_{r}(p)\times[t-r^{2},t]\text{ for some }(p,t)\in\mathbb{R}^{n}\times\mathbb{R}\}<\infty,

then

\|u\|_{C^{2m,\alpha}(\Omega)}\doteqdot\sum_{i+2j\leq 2m}r_{\Omega}^{|i|+2j}\left(\|\nabla^{i}\partial_{t}^{j}u\|_{C^{0}(\Omega)}+r_{\Omega}^{\alpha}[\nabla^{i}\partial_{t}^{j}u]_{\alpha,\alpha/2}\right).

C^{2m,\alpha}(\Omega)
then refers to the space of functions
u(x,t)\in C^{2m}(\Omega)
with
\|u\|_{C^{2m,\alpha}(\Omega)}<\infty
.

Throughout
L
will denote an linear second order operator of the form

Lu=a^{ij}(x,t)\nabla_{i}\nabla_{j}u+b^{i}(x,t)\nabla_{i}u+c(x,t)u.

We first recall some interior Hölder estimates for inhomogeneous linear parabolic equations.

Lemma C.1
.

Let
r>0
.
Consider the parabolic neighborhoods
\Omega=B_{r}\times[-r^{2},0]
and
\Omega^{\prime}=B_{2r}\times[-4r^{2},0]
.
Assume that
u\in C^{2m,\alpha}(\Omega^{\prime})
satisfies the equation

\displaystyle(\partial_{t}-L)u=f\qquad\text{on }\Omega^{\prime}

where

Lu=a^{ij}(x,t)\nabla_{i}\nabla_{j}u+b^{i}(x,t)\nabla_{i}u+c(x,t)u,

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\Lambda}\leq a^{ij}\leq\Lambda,\qquad\|a^{ij}\|_{C^{2m-2,\alpha}(\Omega^{\prime})}\leq\Lambda,

\displaystyle\|b^{i}\|_{C^{2m-2,\alpha}(\Omega^{\prime})}\leq r^{-1}\Lambda,\qquad\|c\|_{C^{2m-2,\alpha}(\Omega^{\prime})}\leq r^{-2}\Lambda,

and
f\in C^{2m-2,\alpha}(\Omega^{\prime})
.
Then

\|u\|_{C^{2m,\alpha}(\Omega)}\leq C\left(r^{2}\|f\|_{C^{2m-2,\alpha}(\Omega^{\prime})}+\|u\|_{C^{0}(\Omega^{\prime})}\right)

where
C=C(n,m,\alpha,\Lambda)
depends only on
n,m,\alpha,\Lambda
.

Proof.

For
m=1
, this lemma is identical to
[
Kry96
, Theorem 8.11.1]
.
The result for
m>1
follows from differentiating the evolution equation of
u
.
∎

Lemma C.2
.

Let
2r\geq s>0
.
Consider the parabolic neighborhoods
\Omega=B_{r}\times[-s^{2},0]
and
\Omega^{\prime}=B_{2r}\times[-s^{2},0]
.
Assume that
u\in C^{2m,\alpha}(\Omega^{\prime})
satisfies the equation

\displaystyle(\partial_{t}-L)u=f\qquad\text{on }\Omega^{\prime},\text{ and}

\displaystyle u(\cdot,0)=u_{0}\qquad\text{on }B_{2r}\times\{0\}

where

Lu=a^{ij}(x,t)\nabla_{i}\nabla_{j}u+b^{i}(x,t)\nabla_{i}u+c(x,t)u,

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\Lambda}\leq a^{ij}\leq\Lambda,\qquad\|a^{ij}\|_{C^{2m-2,\alpha}(\Omega^{\prime})}\leq\Lambda,

\displaystyle\|b^{i}\|_{C^{2m-2,\alpha}(\Omega^{\prime})}\leq r^{-1}\Lambda,\qquad\|c\|_{C^{2m-2,\alpha}(\Omega^{\prime})}\leq r^{-2}\Lambda,

f\in C^{2m-2,\alpha}(\Omega^{\prime})
, and
u_{0}\in C^{2m,\alpha}(B_{2r})
.
Then

\|u\|_{C^{2m,\alpha}(\Omega)}\leq C\left(r^{2}\|f\|_{C^{2m-2,\alpha}(\Omega^{\prime})}+\|u\|_{C^{0}(\Omega^{\prime})}+\|u_{0}\|_{C^{2m,\alpha}(B_{2r})}\right)

where
C=C(n,m,\alpha,\Lambda)
depends only on
n,m,\alpha,\Lambda
.

Proof.

For
m=1
, the result follows from
[
Kry96
, Exercise 9.2.5]
applied to
u-u_{0}
combined with
[
Kry96
, Remark 8.11.2]
.
The result for
m>1
follows from differentiation.
∎

Proposition C.3
.

Let
r>0
.
Consider the parabolic neighborhoods
\Omega=B_{r}\times[-r^{2},0]
and
\Omega^{\prime}=B_{2r}\times[-4r^{2},0]
.

Assume that
u\in C^{2}(\Omega^{\prime})
satisfies the equation

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} (\partial_{t}-L)u=Q[u]+F={}&r^{-2}f_{1}(r^{-1}x,r^{-2}t,u)\cdot u+r^{-1}f_{2}(r^{-1}x,r^{-2}t,u)\cdot\nabla u\\
&+f_{3}(r^{-1}x,r^{-2}t,u)\cdot\nabla u\otimes\nabla u+f_{4}(r^{-1}x,r^{-2}t,u)\cdot u\otimes\nabla^{2}u\\
&+F(x,t)\end{aligned}

where
f_{1},\dots,f_{4}
are smooth functions such that
f_{2},\dots,f_{4}
can be paired with the tensors
u\otimes\nabla u,\nabla u\otimes\nabla u,u\otimes\nabla^{2}u
respectively.
Assume that the linear operator
L
has the form

Lu=a^{ij}(x,t)\partial_{ij}^{2}u+b^{i}(x,t)\partial_{i}u+c(x,t)u

and that the coefficients satisfy the following bounds for
m\geq 1
and
\alpha\in(0,1)
:

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\Lambda}\leq a^{ij}\leq\Lambda,\qquad\|a^{ij}\|_{C^{2m-2,\alpha}(\Omega^{\prime})}\leq\Lambda,

\displaystyle\|b^{i}\|_{C^{2m-2,\alpha}(\Omega^{\prime})}\leq r^{-1}\Lambda,\qquad\|c\|_{C^{2m-2,\alpha}(\Omega^{\prime})}\leq r^{-2}\Lambda.

Then there are constants
\epsilon,C>0
depending only on
n,m,\alpha,\Lambda
and the
f_{i}
such that

\displaystyle\|u\|_{C^{0}(\Omega^{\prime})}+r^{2}\|F\|_{C^{2m-2,\alpha}(\Omega^{\prime})}\leq\epsilon\implies

\displaystyle\|u\|_{C^{2m,\alpha}(\Omega)}\leq C\left(\|u\|_{C^{0}(\Omega^{\prime})}+r^{2}\|F\|_{C^{2m-2,\alpha}(\Omega^{\prime})}\right).

The proof of Proposition
C.3
follows verbatim the proof of Proposition 2.5 in
[
Bam14
]

with

H=\|u\|_{C^{0}(\Omega^{\prime})}+r^{2}\|F\|_{C^{2m-2,\alpha}(\Omega^{\prime})}

instead of
H=\|u\|_{C^{0}(\Omega^{\prime})}
.
The upcoming proof of Proposition
C.4
uses similar logic and we instead provide the full details there.

Proposition C.4
.

Let
2r\geq s>0
.
Consider the parabolic neighborhoods

\Omega=B_{r}\times[0,s^{2}]
and
\Omega^{\prime}=B_{2r}\times[0,s^{2}]
.

Assume that
u\in C^{2}(\Omega^{\prime})
satisfies the equation

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} (\partial_{t}-L)u=Q[u]+F={}&r^{-2}f_{1}(r^{-1}x,r^{-2}t,u)\cdot u+r^{-1}f_{2}(r^{-1}x,r^{-2}t,u)\cdot\nabla u\\
&+f_{3}(r^{-1}x,r^{-2}t,u)\cdot\nabla u\otimes\nabla u+f_{4}(r^{-1}x,r^{-2}t,u)\cdot u\otimes\nabla^{2}u\\
&+F(x,t)\\
\text{ and }u(\cdot,0)={}&u_{0},\end{aligned}

where
f_{1},\dots,f_{4}
are smooth functions such that
f_{2},\dots,f_{4}
can be paired with the tensors
\nabla u,\nabla u\otimes\nabla u,u\otimes\nabla^{2}u
respectively.
Assume that the linear operator
L
has the form

Lu=a^{ij}(x,t)\partial_{ij}^{2}u+b^{i}(x,t)\partial_{i}u+c(x,t)u

and that the coefficients satisfy the following bounds for
m\geq 1
and
\alpha\in(0,1)
:

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\Lambda}\leq a^{ij}\leq\Lambda,\qquad\|a^{ij}\|_{C^{2m-2,\alpha}(\Omega^{\prime})}\leq\Lambda,

\displaystyle\|b^{i}\|_{C^{2m-2,\alpha}(\Omega^{\prime})}\leq r^{-1}\Lambda,\qquad\|c\|_{C^{2m-2,\alpha}(\Omega^{\prime})}\leq r^{-2}\Lambda.

Then there are constants
\epsilon,C>0
depending only on
n,m,\alpha,\Lambda,
and the
f_{i}
such that

\displaystyle\|u\|_{C^{0}(\Omega^{\prime})}+\|u_{0}\|_{C^{2m,\alpha}(B_{2r})}+r^{2}\|F\|_{C^{2m-2,\alpha}(\Omega^{\prime})}\leq\epsilon\implies

\displaystyle\|u\|_{C^{2m,\alpha}(\Omega)}\leq C\left(\|u\|_{C^{0}(\Omega^{\prime})}+\|u_{0}\|_{C^{2m,\alpha}(B_{2r})}+r^{2}\|F\|_{C^{2m-2,\alpha}(\Omega^{\prime})}\right).

Proof.

We adapt the proofs of
[
Bam14
, Proposition 2.5]
and
[
App18
, Lemma 4.4]
.
Begin by introducing a weighted norm for any
\theta\in(0,1]
and domain
\Omega
as

\|u\|^{(\theta)}_{C^{2m,\alpha}(\Omega)}\doteqdot\sum_{i+2j\leq 2m}(r_{\Omega}\theta)^{i+2j}\left(\|\nabla^{i}\partial_{t}^{j}u\|_{C^{0}(\Omega)}+(r_{\Omega}\theta)^{\alpha}[\nabla^{i}\partial_{t}^{j}u]_{\alpha,\alpha/2}\right).

This weighted norm with
\theta=1
equals the
C^{2m,\alpha}
norm, that is
\|\cdot\|^{(1)}_{C^{2m,\alpha}(\Omega)}=\|\cdot\|_{C^{2m,\alpha}(\Omega)}
.

Choosing any
x\in\mathbb{R}^{n}
such that
B_{\theta r}(x)\subset B_{r}

and applying
Lemma
C.1
on
B_{\theta r}(x)\times[t-(\theta r)^{2},t]
for all
t\in[(2\theta r)^{2},s^{2}]
and
Lemma
C.2
on
B_{\theta r}(x)\times[0,(2\theta r)^{2}]
we deduce:

In the setting of Lemma
C.2
,

(C.1)

\|u\|_{C^{2m,\alpha}(B_{r}\times[0,s^{2}])}^{(\theta)}\leq C\left((r\theta)^{2}\|f\|^{(\theta)}_{C^{2m-2,\alpha}(B_{(1+\theta)r}\times[0,s^{2}])}\right.\\
\left.+\|u\|^{(\theta)}_{C^{0}(B_{(1+\theta)r}\times[0,s^{2}])}+\|u_{0}\|^{(\theta)}_{C^{2m,\alpha}(B_{(1+\theta)r})}\right).

By scaling, we shall henceforth assume without loss of generality that
r=1
. Any constants
C
in the rest of the proof will depend on
n,m,\alpha,\Lambda
and the
f_{i}
.

Define

H\doteqdot\|u\|_{C^{0}(B_{2}\times[0,s^{2}])}+\|u_{0}\|_{C^{2m,\alpha}(B_{2})}+\|F\|_{C^{2m-2,\alpha}(B_{2}\times[0,s^{2}])},

r_{k}\doteqdot\sum_{i=0}^{k}2^{-i}=2-2^{-k},\qquad\theta_{k}\doteqdot\frac{r_{k+1}}{r_{k}}-1,\quad\text{ and }\quad\Omega_{k}\doteqdot B_{r_{k}}\times[0,s^{2}].

Note that
1\leq r_{k}\leq 2
and
s\leq 2\leq 2r_{k}
.
(
C.1
) applied to
\Omega=\Omega_{k}
implies

(C.2)

a_{k}\doteqdot\|u\|_{C^{2m,\alpha}(\Omega_{k})}^{(\theta_{k})}\leq C\left(\theta_{k}^{2}\|Q[u]\|_{C^{2m-2,\alpha}(\Omega_{k+1})}^{(\theta_{k+1})}+H\right).

Additionally, because
\lim_{k\to\infty}\theta_{k}=0
,
a_{\infty}\doteqdot\lim_{k\to\infty}a_{k}=\|u\|_{C^{0}(B_{2}\times[0,s^{2}])}\leq H
.

We now estimate
Q[u]
in terms of
u
.
First, observe that for all
i=1,\dots,4
,

\|f_{i}(x,t,u)\|_{C^{2m-2,\alpha}(\Omega_{k+1})}^{(\theta_{k+1})}\leq C\left(1+\left(\|u\|_{C^{2m-2,\alpha}(\Omega_{k+1})}^{(\theta_{k+1})}\right)^{2m-1}\right)\leq C\left(1+a_{k+1}^{2m-1}\right).

Hence,

\|f_{1}\cdot u\|_{C^{2m-2,\alpha}(\Omega_{k+1})}^{(\theta_{k+1})}\leq C\|f_{1}\|_{C^{2m-2,\alpha}(\Omega_{k+1})}^{(\theta_{k+1})}\|u\|_{C^{2m-2,\alpha}(\Omega_{k+1})}^{(\theta_{k+1})}\leq C(a_{k+1}+a_{k+1}^{2m}),

and similarly

\displaystyle\|f_{2}\cdot\nabla u\|_{C^{2m-2,\alpha}(\Omega_{k+1})}^{(\theta_{k+1})}

\displaystyle\leq\|f_{2}\|_{C^{2m-2,\alpha}(\Omega_{k+1})}^{(\theta_{k+1})}\theta_{k+1}^{-1}\|u\|_{C^{2m,\alpha}(\Omega_{k+1})}^{(\theta_{k+1})}

\displaystyle\leq C\theta_{k+1}^{-1}(a_{k+1}+a_{k+1}^{2m}),

\displaystyle\|f_{3}\cdot\nabla u\otimes\nabla u\|_{C^{2m-2,\alpha}(\Omega_{k+1})}^{(\theta_{k+1})}

\displaystyle\leq C\theta_{k+1}^{-2}(a_{k+1}^{2}+a_{k+1}^{2m+1}),\text{ and}

\displaystyle\|f_{3}\cdot u\otimes\nabla^{2}u\|_{C^{2m-2,\alpha}(\Omega_{k+1})}^{(\theta_{k+1})}

\displaystyle\leq C\theta_{k+1}^{-2}(a_{k+1}^{2}+a_{k+1}^{2m+1}).

Therefore, using that
0<\theta_{k+1}\leq 1
,

\|Q[u]\|_{C^{2m-2,\alpha}(\Omega_{k+1})}^{(\theta_{k+1})}\leq C\left(\theta_{k+1}^{-1}a_{k+1}+\theta_{k+1}^{-2}a_{k+1}^{2}+\theta_{k+1}^{-1}a_{k+1}^{2m}+\theta_{k+1}^{-2}a_{k+1}^{2m+1}\right),

and so, by (
C.2
),

a_{k}\leq C\left(\theta_{k+1}a_{k+1}+a_{k+1}^{2}+a_{k+1}^{2m}+a_{k+1}^{2m+1}+H\right).

Defining
b_{k}\doteqdot a_{k}/H
, it follows that

(C.3)

b_{k}\leq C_{0}\left(\theta_{k+1}b_{k+1}+Hb_{k+1}^{2}+H^{2m-1}b_{k+1}^{2m}+H^{2m}b_{k+1}^{2m+1}+1\right).

Assume without loss of generality that
C_{0}>1

and set
\epsilon=\frac{1}{16C_{0}^{2}}
.
Choose
k_{0}
such that
\theta_{k+1}\leq\epsilon
for all
k\geq k_{0}
.
Then for all
k\geq k_{0}
,
H\leq\epsilon
implies

b_{k}\leq\frac{1}{16}b_{k+1}+\frac{1}{16C_{0}}b_{k+1}^{2}+\frac{1}{16^{2m-1}C_{0}^{4m-3}}b_{k+1}^{2m}+\frac{1}{16^{2m}C_{0}^{4m-1}}b_{k+1}^{2m+1}+C_{0}.

Thus,
b_{k+1}<2C_{0}
implies
b_{k}<2C_{0}
as well.
By induction and the fact that
\lim_{k\to\infty}b_{k}=a_{\infty}/H\leq 1<2C_{0}
,
it then follows that
b_{k_{0}}<2C_{0}
.

Using (
C.3
), the bound
b_{k_{0}}<2C_{0}
yields a bound for
b_{0}
, say
b_{0}\leq C^{\prime}
.
Hence,
a_{0}\leq C^{\prime}H
which completes the proof.
∎

Appendix D
Constants

The following list summarizes the parameters used throughout the proof of
1.1
for the reader’s convenience.
It is ordered so that any constant depends only on the ones above it.

•

(M^{n},\overline{g},f)
is the smooth, complete, asymptotically conical, shrinking, gradient Ricci soliton fixed at the outset.

•

\lambda_{*}<0
governs the
L^{2}_{f}
decay rate.
\lambda_{*}
is chosen so that
\lambda_{*}\notin\{\lambda_{j}:j\in\mathbb{N}\}=\sigma(\overline{\Delta}_{f}+2\overline{Rm})
.

•

K=K(n,M,\overline{g},f,\lambda_{*})\in\mathbb{N}
denotes the index of the eigenvalue
\lambda_{K}\in\sigma(\overline{\Delta}_{f}+2\overline{Rm})
such that
\lambda_{K+1}<\lambda_{*}<\lambda_{K}
.

•

\Gamma_{0}\gg 1
is a parameter that governs the transition from the diffeomorphic copies of
M
and its soliton metric
(1-t_{0})\varphi_{t_{0}}^{*}\overline{g}
to the rest of the closed manifold
\mathcal{M}
.

•

0<\overline{p}\ll 1
controls how much of the lower eigenmodes
\{h_{j}:j\leq K\}
the initial perturbation
h_{\mathbf{p}}(t_{0})
contains via
|\mathbf{p}|\leq\overline{p}e^{\lambda_{*}\tau_{0}}
.

•

0<\epsilon_{0},\epsilon_{1},\epsilon_{2}\ll 1
govern the global
C^{2}
estimates for
h_{\mathbf{p}}
.
0<\epsilon_{0}\ll 1
may also be taken sufficiently small depending on
\epsilon_{1},\epsilon_{2}
.

•

0<\gamma_{0}\ll 1
is another macro level parameter used to distinguish scales smaller than
\Gamma_{0}
.
\gamma_{0}
governs the support of the initial perturbation
h_{\mathbf{p}}(t_{0})
at initial time
t_{0}
.

•

0<\mu_{u},\mu_{s}\ll 1
govern the
L^{2}_{f}
decay estimates of
h_{\mathbf{p}}
.

•

t_{0}\in[0,1)
with
0<1-t_{0}\ll 1
or equivalently
\tau_{0}=-\ln(1-t_{0})\gg 1
is the initial time of the Ricci flow
G_{\mathbf{p}}
.
